    Web

    Projects panel

    The budget, metering, and cost-ceiling RNs live in Business rules — Cost.

    Same reason as the Authentication section below: size, not subject. #rn-NNN anchors unchanged.

    Cost

    RN-039 — Project status dot: risk always beats inactivity

    The dashboard sidebar shows a health dot per project, derived from three independent signals: budget (the same 70%/90% thresholds as RN-018), blocked task (tasks.blocked), and recent activity (7 days). Colors: green = healthy and active; amber = budget ≥70%; red = budget ≥90% or blocked task; gray = no activity in the last 7 days. When a risk signal (amber/red) and the inactivity signal (gray) apply at the same time, the risk one wins — an overrun, stalled project is still something to look at, not something to hide behind "no activity".

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/project-status.ts (deriveProjectStatus)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/project-status.test.ts
    • Origin: fidelity of the projects dashboard to the approved design

    RN-064 — Heartbeat não encerra sessão com trabalho pendente

    Estendida pela RN-581 (AT-072): um agente conversacional esperando o usuário passou a ser o QUINTO sinal, e o único com teto (8h, causa conversation_idle_timeout); a resposta ganhou aguardandoUsuarioDesde. O resto desta regra vale como está.

    O timeout de heartbeat mede inatividade da aba, não do trabalho. Antes de encerrar, o SessionServer pergunta à api se sobrou trabalho (GET /internal/sessions/:id/pending-work); havendo, reagenda o timeout e registra o motivo no log em vez de matar a sessão.

    O default são 30 segundos. Sair da sessão para a aba de Backlog já bastava para matá-la — e numa execução real isso prendeu um handoff offered para o Arquiteto dentro de uma sessão fechada: épico e quatro histórias prontos, e a cadeia sem como seguir, porque não existe onde aceitar handoff de sessão morta.

    Fechar sessão é sobre o trabalho ter acabado, não sobre quem está olhando.

    A api fora do ar NÃO impede o encerramento: {:error, _} encerra assim mesmo, com aviso no log. Trocar sessão órfã por sessão imortal seria trocar um defeito por outro.

    "Trabalho pendente" são três sinais — o segundo entrou pelo achado V, o terceiro pelo bug real do Criativo→PO→Arquiteto:

    1. handoff offered — o caso original acima;
    2. proposed_action com status pending — alguém está esperando a SUA decisão, e um agente pode estar suspenso esperando o desfecho (RN-073);
    3. agente ATIVADO ainda em turno — o último agent.status de cada ator que já falou na sessão é working sem um idle posterior.

    O segundo é o mesmo defeito do primeiro um nível abaixo, e a execução do hello-limpo mostrou o custo: a sessão nasceu 23:34:12, uma ação ficou pending às 23:34:13, e o heartbeat a fechou às 23:34:42 — exatamente os 30s do timeout. O dev agent seguiu trabalhando por mais de uma hora numa sessão que o banco dava por encerrada, e isso envenena toda métrica por sessão: duração, custo e "quantas terminaram bem" passam a ler um estado que não descreve o que houve.

    O terceiro é a mesma janela, um passo antes de qualquer um dos dois primeiros existir: AcceptHandoffUseCase marca o handoff antigo como accepted e ativa o próximo agente na hora, mas a ativação no engine é GenServer.cast fire-and-forget — responde 201 antes de o agente sequer começar. O PO ativado pelo handoff do Criativo roda um kickoff de até 12 iterações de LLM usando só ferramentas category: :direct (create_epic/create_story/create_task), que nunca geram proposed_action, e só oferece o handoff seguinte (o sinal 1) no FIM do turno inteiro. Entre a ativação e esse fim, nem o sinal 1 nem o sinal 2 existiam — só o ping do canal Phoenix a cada 10s segurava a sessão, e qualquer atraso maior que os 30s do timeout fechava a sessão com o PO ainda gerando o backlog, quebrando a cadeia de handoff pela raiz: o handoff seguinte acabava sendo oferecido numa sessão já closed, que não aceita mais nada.

    agent.status (working/idle) é o que todo agente conversacional (Criativo/PO/Arquiteto/Dev Lead/Infra) já narra nos limites de turno, e é PERSISTIDO no event log, não só broadcastado no canal (Engine.Sessions.LiveBroadcast.agent_status/4, ADR 0021) — o mesmo sinal que o painel do time já lê para derivar o roster (conversationalStatus em apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts). Reaproveitá-lo aqui não exigiu evento novo nenhum: o terceiro sinal é o último agent.status de CADA ator que já falou na sessão, e é genérico por tipo de evento — cobre qualquer agente ativado por handoff, não só o PO.

    A versão anterior desta regra dizia, por escrito, que incluir trabalho de agente "sem um teste que prove a interação seria adivinhar". A execução produziu a prova, e o teste agora existe.

    O que continua fora: task in_progress sem ação pendente nem handoff nem turno em aberto. O dev agent tem máquina de estados própria e retém o worktree por conta dele; o sinal que a api possui e que a execução comprovou é a ação pendente. Incluir a task exigiria a api ler dev_agent_states, que é do engine — decisão de fronteira, não conserto de passagem. Os dev agents também não emitem agent.status (rodam com máquina de estados própria, não com o loop conversacional de turno) — o terceiro sinal não os cobre, e não precisa: a ação pendente já cobre o caminho deles.

    • Onde: apps/api/src/application/use-cases/sessions/get-session-pending-work.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/application/ports/session-event-repository.port.ts (listByTypeInSession), apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/session_server.ex (handle_info(:heartbeat_timeout, …))
    • Teste: apps/engine/test/engine/sessions/session_lifecycle_test.exs (heartbeat NÃO encerra sessão com trabalho pendente e o caso oposto) e apps/api/test/application/use-cases/sessions/get-session-pending-work.use-case.spec.ts (os três sinais, a ação já decidida que NÃO segura, o idle que libera, o isolamento por ator, a genericidade por tipo de agente e o escopo por sessão)
    • Origem: execução real da FASE 13b; achado V, Fase H do backlog; bug real do encadeamento Criativo→PO→Arquiteto

    RN-088 — Load failure is said on screen; 429 is not retried

    Every screen that loads data from the api distinguishes three states, never two: loading (skeleton), error (the message the api sent, with the trace_id and a try-again button), and empty (the empty-list text). if (!dado) return null collapses the three, and the observed outcome was the worst possible: with the api rate-limiting with 429, the main area of /projects/:id was completely blank — no message, no error, no skeleton — and the reason existed only in the console. On the dashboard it was worse than blank: !projects was also true on error, so the screen invited you to create the first project of a workspace that could have twenty.

    It's RN-059 on the other end of the wire: failure never becomes an empty response. Whoever fails, says so.

    The sentence is the api's, extracted by mensagemDaApi — the same function the mutation path already used in toasts. A generic text of ours doesn't know the difference between "rate limit exceeded, try again shortly" (wait) and "access denied" (waiting won't help).

    The other side of the same rule is not answering the limit with more traffic. 4xx is not retried: 429 is literally the server telling you to stop, and 401 has already been renewed once inside request(). And polling stops when the query errors — it comes back on window focus, on remount, or on the button. Before, there were three retries per failure added to ~25 polls of 3 to 5 seconds that didn't know how to stop: a real session accumulated 1128 429 errors against a limit of 300 requests per minute per user (RateLimitGuard), and the loop kept the sliding window from recovering. 5xx and network failure keep the three attempts, where repeating is the right reaction.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/query-policy.ts (deveRetentar, pollQueParaNoErro), apps/web/src/components/ErroDeCarregamento.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPage.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/Dashboard.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx, apps/web/src/main.tsx
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/query-policy.test.ts; apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPage.test.tsx (429 becomes a message with the api's sentence; 403 shows the 403's reason; loading is not an error); apps/web/src/routes/Dashboard.test.tsx (error on the list doesn't become "no project yet"); apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx (the sidebar says so instead of staying empty)
    • Origin: real navigation on /projects/:id with 1128 429 errors in the console

    RN-089 — A repeated project name is disambiguated in the sidebar

    Project name is not unique — nothing in the domain prevents it. When two or more projects in the list have the same name, each of them shows in the sidebar the abbreviated id and the creation date (#a1b2c3d4 · 07/08 14:32), both already present in the project payload. Projects with a unique name show nothing: the caption on every row would be noise in the place with the least space on screen.

    Concrete origin: a validation run created twenty projects called validacao-real, and the twenty sidebar rows were visually identical.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/project-label.ts (nomesRepetidos, desempateDoProjeto), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx (repeated name gets a tiebreaker, unique name doesn't)
    • Origin: real navigation with the sidebar full of validation projects

    Session

    RN-098 — The session name adds to the hashtag, never replaces it

    The session can receive a friendly name (sessions.name, optional), at creation or later, via PATCH /projects/:projectId/sessions/:sessionId. The label shown on screen is composite: <name> · #<first 8 characters of the id>. With no name, it degrades to the hashtag alone.

    The hashtag never disappears, and the reason is operational: it's what gets pasted into a URL, a command, or a conversation, and a name chosen by a person isn't unique — two sessions named "Checkout" are normal. A blank name (or whitespace only) counts as absence, both on creation and on renaming: writing '' would turn the label into " · #a1b2c3d4", worse than the hashtag alone. null in the PATCH body is the way to undo.

    Renaming is not a session event. The event log is what the session lived through, and the name is a navigation label, changed as many times as desired — N renaming events would push exactly what matters out of the 200-event tail.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-label.ts:61 (rotuloDaSessao), apps/api/src/application/ports/session-repository.port.ts:52 (rename). Reachable both from inside the session (apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:439, handleRename) and from the project's list, without needing to open the session first (apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.tsx:101, handleRenomear) — both screens call the same renameSession and the same rotuloDaSessao.
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/session-label.test.ts, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/sessions/session-kind-e-nome.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.test.tsx
    • Origin: ADR 0061

    Projects panel

    RN-099 — Activity paginates the past without repolling what doesn't change

    The Overview's Activity column shows a window of 100 events anchored at the session's tail, and goes down into the past page by page. Each page uses the afterSeq/nextCursor that GET /projects/:p/sessions/:s/events already returns: no new route, no new parameter.

    Two questions, two queries. useSessionEvents answers "how is it now" — the last 200 under polling — and feeds the team panel, the tree timeline, the execution section, and the Approvals tab. None of them paginates. Activity's answers "what happened", and it was the only one asking that question with the other one's answer: 200 items at once, with no beginning or end, and still not reaching the start of a long session.

    The anchor is the tail, not the start of the session. The endpoint paginates FORWARD, and there's no beforeSeq — inventing one would be a new contract. But opening the feed at event #1 of a session with thousands delivers the wrong screen: whoever opens Activity wants what just happened. So the first page is the same latest read the screen already does, with the same queryKey (React Query serves both with ONE fetch), and each click goes down a fixed window with afterSeq.

    What pagination can't cost. One request per poll cycle — the same as before, and the savings of RN-090/RN-091 don't regress. Three pieces guarantee that:

    • the tail is the query the screen already had, shared by queryKey;
    • the first click does not become a request: the latest read brings 200 and the window shows 100, so revealing what was already paid for comes before asking again;
    • an old page is a closed seq window over immutable events (there's never an UPDATE on an event table): staleTime: Infinity and no refetchInterval. A poll there would be paying N requests to receive N identical responses.

    The count is honest about what it knows. The filters by agent and by type run over the loaded page, not over the session — so the UI says N of M loaded, not "N results". Taking the filter to the server would give the true total and touch the event repository; it's not for this phase, and saying the wrong total would be worse than saying less.

    The three states of RN-088 apply here: error before empty — the column said "no activity" when the api had refused.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/hooks.ts (useSessionEventHistory, EVENTOS_POR_PAGINA), apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.tsx, apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/session-event.repository.ts (listPaginated, already existing)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/session-history.test.tsx (opens at the tail; the first click costs no request; the next paginates with contiguous afterSeq; the loop ends at the start of the session; mounted together with the current state, the tail is ONE fetch; an old page doesn't repoll); apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.test.tsx (the "N of M loaded" follows the filter; without the pagination props nothing changes)
    • Origin: user request on the first real navigation — "Activity in the Overview tab must be paginated, so it isn't infinite"

    Session

    RN-104 — The tab derives from the recorded kind, and creates in that kind

    The project has two session tabs — Creative and Chat — and each one is a kind: it lists the sessions whose sessions.kind is its own, and its CTA creates in that kind, without asking again. There's no third tab listing both together.

    The kind was already immutable (RN-097), and that's what makes it a legitimate navigation coordinate instead of a saved filter: a session never switches tabs. The tab reads the recorded field — it doesn't derive the kind from any event, for the same reason as RN-097: execution.activated classifies execution STATE, and a tab that looked at it would move on its own.

    Two consequences the rule fixes, and which aren't screen detail:

    • The kind choice leaves the form. It used to live in a radio-button fieldset, and now it happens the moment the person clicks the tab. Keeping both would offer the chance to create a session that contradicts the place you're standing in. What's left in the form is the name, which stays optional (RN-098).
    • One action, one place at a time. "Start ideation" still exists — it's what brings the Creative agent into the session, and from there on the owner's key starts being spent (RN-058); no one steps in alone. What changed is where it lives: inside the invite while the invite is on screen, and in the topbar when it isn't. The invite used to POINT to the topbar ("use Start ideation, at the top of the screen"), which is the literal version of the problem that originated RN-097 — the action in one place and the explanation in another. The topbar can't simply lose the button: it's possible to type into a creative session without ever having called the Creative agent, and then the invite leaves the scene.

    The three screens honor the three states of RN-088, with error before empty: the filtered list is empty in both cases, and saying "no ideation yet" after a 429 would be lying about what happened.

    The Chat's deep-link key stays sessions, not chat. That's what makes an old bookmarked ?tab=sessions link open the Chat with the tab marked in the tab bar — resolving the old key as an alias only in the panel would leave the tab bar with no selection at all, because Tabs compares active to key, and whoever writes active receives the raw key from validateSearch.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts:95 (both entries), apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.tsx:114 (the filter by recorded kind) and :98 (the CTA creating in the tab's kind), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:716 (conviteVisivel, the one question the topbar and the invite share)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.sessao.test.tsx
    • Edge case: a project with no session of that kind shows the empty state OF THAT KIND, even when it has sessions of the other kind — that's information, not an error.
    • Origin: ADR 0061

    RN-107 — The Code tab has a FOURTH state: blocked by a pending decision

    The three states of RN-088 (loading/error/empty) do not describe sem_decisao (RN-105): it is not loading (the api already answered), it is not error (it answered CORRECTLY) and it is not empty (no data is missing — a DECISION is missing, which is something else). Treating sem_decisao as "empty" would show an editor without files, inviting one to think the repository is empty; treating it as "error" would make the screen suggest "try again" for something only the Arquiteto resolves.

    The tab asks for the container state BEFORE trying to read code (GET /projects/:id/container), instead of waiting for the first tree or file to return 409 — the same check the api already does in the ReadProjectCodeUseCase funnel (RN-105), only asked first, so the fourth state is born as its own message and not as the footer of a generic error. While blocked, the screen re-queries on its own every 15s — once decided, the image does not change without new human action, and re-querying a stable state would be the same family of unnecessary traffic as PÓS-FASE 15.

    The presentation of the fourth state was EXTRACTED to ContainerImageGateNotice (apps/web/src/components/ContainerImageGate.tsx) — a usage finding: the PRs tab (apps/web/src/routes/code/PrListAndDiff.tsx, consumed by ProjectPrsTab.tsx and by CodeDiffPanel.tsx) calls getCodePullRequests/ getCodeDiff, which go through the SAME funnel (RN-105) and can return the SAME 409 — but, unlike this tab, without asking first. It showed that 409 in the generic error banner with "Tentar de novo", the wrong affordance for a state only the Arquiteto resolves. isContainerImageGateError (apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts) identifies the cause by status === 409 — the only cause of ConflictException in ReadProjectCodeUseCase.alvo — and PrListAndDiff swaps the banner for ContainerImageGateNotice when it matches, without a pre-check of its own (it reacts to the 409 of the query that was going to run anyway).

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectCodeTab.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectCodeTab.module.css, apps/web/src/components/ContainerImageGate.tsx (shared presentation), apps/web/src/routes/code/PrListAndDiff.tsx (consumer reactive to the 409), apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts (isContainerImageGateError)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectCodeTab.test.tsx ("o gate"), apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeDiffPanel.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPrsTab.test.tsx ("o gate do container não é erro genérico")
    • Edge case: the front-end check does NOT replace the api's — it is a UX convenience. If the api changes state between the gate query and the actual read, the read route still refuses with 409 (RN-105); the front only avoids the common case of showing the empty editor for an instant. The PRs tab does no pre-check: it discovers the block when the query has already failed, because there is no tree/file there for the "empty" instant that the Code tab's pre-check avoids.
    • Origin: ADR 0065

    RN-108 — The session socket requires a single-use opaque ticket, not the reused JWT

    EngineWeb.SessionSocket.connect/3 refused the whole connection with only the session_id (UUID) needing to exist in the Registry — whoever discovered the UUID entered the session:<id> channel and received all the live broadcasts of the session. Closing this was a deliberate limitation documented in the module itself since Fase 3.

    POST /projects/:projectId/sessions/:sessionId/socket-ticket (scope: "heartbeat"|"terminal") issues an opaque ticket (32 bytes of CSPRNG, TokenFactory), TTL of 30 seconds, single use. scope: "heartbeat" requires the viewer role; scope: "terminal" requires developer — the same minimum role as MIN_ROLE_FOR_ACTION_TYPE.terminal in domain/actions/decide.ts (today no path really asks for terminal; the value is born right for FASE 25, the interactive terminal). The api persists only the HASH (plain SHA-256, not hashDeToken/HMAC — the engine does not have the api's pepper, and a 256-bit CSPRNG token does not need a pepper against a dictionary, the same reasoning that hashDeToken itself already records), never the raw token.

    Consumption belongs to the ENGINE, which reads session_socket_tickets directly (same pattern as Engine.Outbox.Event over outbox_events — never changeset/insert, only the narrow write that single use requires) in TWO steps: SocketTicket.validar/1 (peek, marking nothing — called by connect/3, which does not yet know which session_id will be requested) and SocketTicket.consumir/2 (conditional UPDATE requiring the topic's session_id to match the row's — called by SessionChannel.join/3, which also checks the ticket's project_id against the session's, defense in depth against a ticket of one project opening a channel of another). Without a ticket, or with an invalid one: the whole connection is refused ({:error, %{reason: "unauthorized"}}), not only the channel join.

    The web (session-channel.ts) fetches a NEW ticket before EVERY socket.connect() — including on automatic reconnection, which exists. The native reconnect of Phoenix.Socket would reuse the same params from construction (the old ticket, already expired or consumed), so it is neutralized (reconnectAfterMs that practically never fires) and reconnection becomes entirely manual, fetching a fresh ticket on each attempt.

    That neutralization holds for EVERY client of the socket, and not only for the web's — including the /runner socket, whose ticket (POST .../runner-ticket) is of the same caliber. It failed in TWO of the four Socket constructors in the repository, and the cost was measured in real execution: apps/runner/src/ channel.ts built the socket without the option — while the module's docblock CLAIMED the reconnect was "por isso neutralizado" — and the auto-reconnect repeated the same already-consumed ticket every ~5.13s (the ceiling of the phoenix.js internal backoff), yielding 61 REFUSED CONNECTION TO EngineWeb.RunnerSocket in a few hours. Added to the runner's OWN policy (index.ts, that one correct), the result was 530 requests in one minute against the ceiling of 300 of RATE_LIMIT_USER — and since the limit is per USER, the 429 showed up in the account owner's BROWSER. Two consequences, and the second is worse than the 429: while it is in that loop the runner is NOT connected, so RunnerReadiness (RN-507) refuses and the project looks like it has "no local agent". apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts had the SAME defect line (same single-use ticket, and no reconnection policy of its own — its onClose says "close and reopen") and was fixed along with it.

    What prevents the third instance is MECHANISM, not a comment: in the runner the Socket options became a type with a MANDATORY reconnectAfterMs (OpcoesDoSocket in channel.ts), and both sides gained a test asserting the OPTION PASSED to the constructor — a test that only checks "connects" would pass with the defect standing, and it did. Along with it, conectarERodar (index.ts) started to explicitly DISCARD the dropped connection (conexao.desconectar()): without it the Socket of the previous round survived the creation of the next one, and each drop left one more live object trying with the dead ticket.

    • Where: apps/api/src/db/schema/sessions.ts (sessionSocketTickets), apps/api/src/domain/sessions/socket-ticket-scope.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/sessions/create-socket-ticket.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/sessions/sessions.controller.ts (route socket-ticket), apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/socket_ticket.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine_web/channels/session_socket.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine_web/channels/session_channel.ex, apps/web/src/lib/session-channel.ts, apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts, apps/runner/src/channel.ts (OpcoesDoSocket, NUNCA_RECONECTAR_SOZINHO_MS), apps/runner/src/index.ts (conectarERodar, the discarding of the dropped connection)
    • Test: apps/api/test/application/use-cases/sessions/create-socket-ticket.use-case.spec.ts, apps/api/test/domain/sessions/socket-ticket-scope.spec.ts, apps/api/test/infrastructure/persistence/session-socket-ticket.repository.spec.ts, apps/engine/test/engine/sessions/socket_ticket_test.exs (reuse fails, wrong session_id fails, concurrent race only one wins), apps/engine/test/engine_web/channels/session_socket_test.exs (without a ticket the connection is refused), apps/engine/test/engine_web/channels/session_channel_test.exs (ticket from another project: join fails), apps/web/src/lib/session-channel.test.ts, apps/runner/src/channel.spec.ts ("neutraliza o auto-reconnect do Phoenix.Socket — ticket de uso único nunca é repetido"; asserts the OPTION passed to the constructor, and fails without it), apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.test.ts (the same, in the folder browser's transport via runner)
    • Edge case: the ticket is NOT the reused JWT — short TTL, single use, closed scope, and it is born from a route of its own that already checks the effective role, not from decoding the existing access token.
    • Origin: no ADR — a one-off extraction/hardening, not a structural change.

    RN-113 — Blame in the editor is an ON-DEMAND annotation — a toggle, never embedded in the file read

    The UI that consumes the foundation of RN-110 comes in here: the Code tab editor (CodeEditor.tsx) only calls getCodeBlame when the user turns on the "Blame" toggle — never together with the file read, which already fires by itself when opening a tab. The reason is the same as the composite-read budgets (ADR 0060): blame is a SECOND call to the provider per open file, and a file near the ceiling (GIT_BLAME_LINE_LIMIT, 2000 lines) is already expensive enough not to pay for it for free on every navigation. truncated (which RN-110 already exposes) becomes a visible warning, in the same pattern as the fileQuery.data.truncated warning.

    Consecutive lines of the SAME commit show author and short sha only on the FIRST line of the block — repeating the same text on each line of a block of dozens of lines would be noise, not annotation; the line only disappears from the text, never from the annotation (the element's title keeps the full date and the commit summary on any line of the block).

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.module.css
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.test.tsx
    • Origin: UI wave of FASE 26b (blame — rich branch dropdown and PR list are UI from two other agents, with no collision risk)

    Projects panel

    RN-118 — The team tree exposes execution detail per milestone, and opens the "last 5"

    AgentTimelineTree/timeline-tree.ts (PHASE 14b) already grouped the event log by agent, but with three gaps: the criterion for opening a branch was only active/stopped (unrelated to how many agents existed), the header counter was the TOTAL of milestones (never "what's new"), and tool.call/ tool.result/agent.response became a milestone showing only the tool name — args, result, and iteration, which the event log already records, never reached the screen.

    Default opening becomes "the 5 agents with the most recent activity (highest seq of the last milestone)", with active keeping priority over recency — they don't add up: since montarArvore already sorts actives first and, within each group, from most recent to oldest, the two criteria collapse into a single slice (ramosAbertosPorPadrao, the first max(number of actives, 5) branches). More than 5 actives: all open, with no cutoff.

    The novelty counter per agent uses the SAME mechanism as the bell (read-state.ts, localStorage), granular by projectId:agentId instead of only projectId — there was no "last seen" at that granularity before. A collapsed branch with a milestone seq greater than that agent's last seen shows the NOVELTY count in place of the total; opening the branch (automatically or manually) marks as seen up to the most recent seq.

    Expandable detail is per MILESTONE, not per branch — only tool.call, tool.result, and agent.response (EVENTOS_EXPANSIVEIS) become an individual button, because only they carry a payload worth expanding. The visual grouping by ITERATION uses iteration from the agent.response payload when it exists (ToolLoop — tool.call/tool.result inherit the iteration of the response that dispatched them, because in the ToolLoop they are emitted AFTER it); agents outside the ToolLoop (PO, Criativo, with no iteration in the payload) get their OWN counter per agent, incremented on each response — inference by seq proximity, not a new field in the event log.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/timeline-tree.ts (Marco.iteracao, Marco.eventType, Marco.payload, ramosAbertosPorPadrao, marcoExpansivel), apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.tsx, apps/web/src/lib/read-state.ts (getAgentLastSeenSeq/ setAgentLastSeenSeq)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/timeline-tree.test.ts (grouping by real and inferred iteration, ramosAbertosPorPadrao with active/recency/no cutoff), apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.test.tsx (default opening, novelty counter appearing/disappearing, milestone expanding with args/result/iteration)
    • Origin: user request — gaps confirmed by investigation of the existing PHASE 14b component; no ADR, same "unseen" semantics as the bell, only a new granularity

    Session

    RN-119 — The session's ACTIVE agent is the one with the most recent agent.activated — never a fixed chain

    SessionPage decides two things from the SAME agent: who the composer's message goes to, and which agent the topbar resolves the displayed model from (the full cascade session→agent→area→project→workspace, the same one RunLlmTurnUseCase uses to actually run the turn). The two questions used to have DIFFERENT answers before this rule: routing used a fixed precedence chain (architect > po > creative, based on historical EXISTENCE — "has already activated once", which never "turned off"), and the displayed model didn't even know who was active — the model-binding route received no agent at all and always fell back to the Creative agent's fixed fallback (herdarModeloDeStart), even after a handoff to the PO, Architect, or Dev Lead.

    The single definition: the ACTIVE agent is the one with the most RECENT agent.activated (by seq), among the conversational agents of the chat flow (Creative, PO, Architect, Dev Lead). Infra Lead is left out: agent_command_controller.ex (engine) only has a message route for po/dev-lead/arquiteto (plus Creative, as the last clause's implicit fallback) — Infra never had message wired, only start. Sending a message to "infra" today would silently fall through to the Creative agent; treating it as the composer's active agent would reopen that trap instead of closing one.

    Superseded in part by RN-631 (AT-251): "most recent agent.activated wins" was itself an implicit default recipient, and an invisible one. The composer's recipient is now CHOSEN by the person (a "To" selector listing the agents already in the session) and the topbar model follows that recipient. What survives from this rule is the half about the model: the binding route receives the agent, never falls back silently.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-destinatario.ts:272 (useDestinatarioDoChat, since RN-631), apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:1124 (getSessionModelBinding, the agentId), apps/api/src/interfaces/http/llm/model-bindings.controller.ts:166 (getSessionBinding, @Query('agentId'))
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.destinatario-do-chat.test.tsx (formerly SessionPage.agente-mais-recente.test.tsx), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.modelo-do-agente-ativo.test.tsx, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/llm/resolve-model-binding.use-case.spec.ts
    • Edge case: Infra Lead doesn't participate in composer routing nor in the "active agent" definition — it's proactive (Phase 4), not conversational through the composer.

    RN-120 — The session's event poll pauses during an in-progress turn

    useSessionEvents fetched events every few seconds with NO awareness of an in-progress turn. If the poll landed DURING a turn (common — turns usually last longer than the interval), it brought already-persisted chat.message/agent.response, which rendered ALONGSIDE the optimistic/streaming state still on screen: the same message, twice.

    The fix pauses only the TIMER (refetchInterval) while streaming is true — the query stays enabled, so the explicit invalidation that already fires at the end of the turn (finalizarTurnoDoAgente) keeps fetching fresh data at the right time. pausarPoll is an OPTIONAL parameter, default false: the hook's other consumers (Overview, Code, Provisioning, AdoptionPlan) have no conversational turn in progress and stay as they were.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/hooks.ts:221 (useSessionEvents), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:277 (eventsQuery)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/hooks.pausar-poll.test.tsx
    • Edge case: pausing the timer isn't disabling the query — explicit invalidation keeps working, and the fix depends on it to never miss data.

    Projects panel

    RN-121 — Dev agent and QA are "executors": their own tab, outside the "Agent team"

    The Overview's "Agent team" grid and "Team timeline" mixed Criativo/PO/Arquiteto/Infra with dev-<module> and QA (lead + qa-automacao/qa-performance-seguranca) — seven, eight agents in the same grid, without distinguishing who CREATES/DECIDES from who IMPLEMENTS/VERIFIES. The Executors tab isolates the last two, with the SAME rendering as the Overview (AgentCard, the area grid — extracted to AgentTeamGrid.tsx — and AgentTimelineTree), never a reinvented card.

    The separation rule is a single function, isExecutorGroup/ isExecutorAgentId (lib/agent-status.ts): dev-lead/dev-<any module> and the entire qa area (lead + sub-specialties) are executors; the rest isn't. BOTH screens filter the SAME groupRosterByArea with that function — the Overview with !isExecutorGroup, Executors with isExecutorGroup — so an agent can't appear in both nor vanish from both: it's always exactly one side.

    The tree doesn't duplicate: each tab filters the EVENTS before passing them to AgentTimelineTree (by whether actor.id belongs to the set of executors), so a dev agent's branch appears only in Executors, never also in the Overview. The Activity column (ActivityFeed, RN-099/100) was NOT filtered — it answers "what happened" in the whole session, dev/QA included, and the component's own per-agent filter already lists everyone who spoke; only the grid and the "who is doing what" tree change place.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts (isExecutorGroup, isExecutorAgentId, autonomyActionTypeFor — moved from ProjectOverviewTab.tsx), apps/web/src/components/AgentTeamGrid.tsx (extracted from the grid that already existed), apps/web/src/routes/ ProjectExecutorsTab.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts (key: 'executores', ordem: 12 — right after the Overview, before Criativo/Chat/Code)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.test.ts (isExecutorAgentId/ isExecutorGroup — dev-<module>, dev-lead, the entire qa area are executors; criativo/po/arquiteto/infra/secops are not); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectExecutorsTab.test.tsx (shows only dev/QA, empty state without both, header count); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.test.tsx (dev-backend/QA disappear from the grid, the rest of the team remains); apps/web/src/routes/ project-tabs.test.tsx (the new tab enters the registry's generic sweep — ruler, panel, deep-link, unique order)
    • Edge case: SecOps is NOT an executor — it enters the roster through the SAME pr.gate_changed that brings QA (Phase 4a, rosterFactsFromEvents), but stays in the Overview. dev-lead is in the executor list for completeness (RN-087 describes it as conversational), although today no path instantiates it in the session roster (the Dev Lead → dev-<module> delegation remains out of scope, ADR 0053 item 5).
    • Origin: user request — its own "Executors" for dev agent and QA, outside the Overview's mixed grid.

    Session

    RN-123 — Creative session with no active Creative agent: the first message ALSO activates it

    In a kind: 'criativa' session (RN-097) with no agent.activated yet, sending the first message through the composer activates the Creative agent first (waiting for activation to finish) and only then delivers that message to it through the real path (sendAgentMessage — history, system prompt, emit_artifact tool). Before this rule, whoever typed without clicking "Start ideation" fell into a generic SSE chat with no history and no business rules: it was NOT the real Creative agent, and the conversation recorded nothing in the domain.

    The "Start ideation" click still exists (RN-104) — it's still what triggers activation when the person prefers the explicit gesture, and it's from it that the owner's key (RN-058) starts being spent on either of the two paths. What changed is that the FIRST MESSAGE now also counts as that gesture: no one should need a separate click before talking to whoever the screen already invited them to talk to.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:678 (handleSend)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.ideacao-automatica.test.tsx
    • Edge case: a consultiva session has no Creative agent — the rule doesn't apply, and the generic SSE path stays the right one for it.

    Projects panel

    RN-124 — The PO narrates epic/story created in the thread, with a direct link to the Backlog

    backlog.epic_created/backlog.story_created — which the PO already recorded in the event log (CreateEpicUseCase/CreateStoryUseCase) — had no handling at all in the session timeline: creating a story left NO trace in the thread, only in the Backlog tab, for those who already knew to go look there. The two types get an entry in the timeline, in the SAME visual body as the agent's other messages (.message/.bubble) — no new class for the chip itself, only the link.

    The link ("See in Backlog") reuses the tab's generic deep-link (?tab=backlog, the same mechanism as the dashboard card's "Set budget" CTA) — it doesn't try to highlight the newly created story inside the tab, which is out of scope.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (timeline — the backlog.epic_created/backlog.story_created block)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-inline-e-links.test.tsx (item 2)
    • Verified by real execution: epic and story inserted into the event log of a real session appear as "Epic created"/"Story created" with the title, and the link navigates to ?tab=backlog for real.
    • Origin: user request — link from the PO to the created stories, straight to the Backlog.

    RN-125 — Handoff acceptance is INLINE in the thread, with a CTA for the Dev Lead pointing to Executors

    The "X passed the baton to Y" divider that already existed in the timeline (handoff.offered) becomes an actionable card — with the accept button embedded — whenever it represents the current pending offer (the same one offeredHandoff already resolved for the topbar button). The topbar button was removed: with acceptance living inside the thread, at the exact place where the handover happened, keeping both would pull two buttons with IDENTICAL text visible at the same time — the same problem ApprovalCard already avoids by never duplicating the action outside the thread (RN-096).

    Which event becomes the card is decided by PAIR, not by id. The handoff.offered payload carries only toAgent; the fromAgent is the event's actor (RN-054 already used that reading for the divider text). Without the handoff id in the event, "this is the current offer" is answered by finding the most RECENT handoff.offered (highest seq) with the SAME fromAgent/toAgent pair as offeredHandoff — which prevents reopening an accept invite that an older offer for the same pair has already resolved (accepted, declined, or superseded by a new offer).

    The card for the Dev Lead gets a second link, "Follow the execution in Executors", to the tab that already exists (RN-121) — it's the start of EXECUTION, and whoever accepts needs to know where to look afterwards. The other offers (PO, Arquiteto…) don't get the link: their session remains the place to follow along itself, there's no "elsewhere to look".

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (timeline — the handoff.offered block, offeredHandoffEventSeq; isActive moved to before the useMemo), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.handoffCard, .timelineLink)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-inline-e-links.test.tsx (actionable card only on the current offer, an old offer of the same pair stays silent, an already-accepted handoff shows no button, Executors link only on dev-lead); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.pista-e-status.test.tsx updated for the inline button (no longer in the topbar)
    • Verified by real execution: accepting a handoff to the PO in a creative session against an isolated build (its own Docker stack, real project/session) — the card becomes a silent divider and the event log advances as soon as the click returns from the api; the CTA for the Dev Lead navigates to ?tab=executores for real.
    • Origin: user request — handoff acceptance also inline in the thread, not only in the topbar.

    RN-126 — Story promotion is decidable INLINE in the thread, with the same mechanism as the Backlog tab

    backlog.story_promotion_proposed (RN-048) gets the same treatment that RN-125 gave to the handoff: the event becomes an actionable card in the session timeline, with the "Promote" and "Return" buttons calling the SAME promoteStories/returnStory that PromotionQueue (ProjectBacklogTab.tsx) already uses — no new endpoint, no second source of truth. The decision still happens in ONE place (the backend), only the trigger gained a second path.

    Resolution by later event, not by separately derived status. The card disappears on its own (becomes a silent divider, without buttons) as soon as an event with a GREATER seq decides the fate of the SAME story — backlog.story_transitioned{storyId} (promoted, emitted by PromoteStoriesUseCase via TransitionStoryUseCase) or backlog.story_promotion_returned{storyId} (returned). Without that check, promoting or returning would leave the same two buttons planted in the thread, offering again a decision already made.

    backlog.story_promotion_returned gets symmetric narration in the thread (reason included), reusing the same sentence that apps/web/src/lib/activity.ts already used only in the sidebar's collapsed log.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (timeline — the backlog.story_promotion_proposed/backlog.story_promotion_returned blocks, handlePromoteStory/handleReturnStory, reason modal reusing Modal/Textarea)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.promocao-inline-e-volta.test.tsx (card with the buttons, promoting calls promoteStories with the payload's storyId, returning requires a reason and calls returnStory, a story resolved by a later story_transitioned becomes a silent divider, story_promotion_returned narrates with the reason)
    • Origin: user request — inline story promotion in the thread, a cheap option reusing RN-048 instead of gating story creation.

    RN-131 — Three races confirmed LIVE in the session thread: invite over the history, anxious indicator, and a turn stuck in handleReadiness

    Three defects found by actually navigating SessionPage.tsx in Chrome, not by test — and all three were race conditions or an incomplete criterion disguised as a product decision.

    1. conversaComecou became "ANY event exists", not "a chat.message/agent.response exists". The previous criterion (finding G) was born so as not to confuse the git bootstrap cards with conversation, but had the opposite effect: a session created by git-bootstrap (commit/branch actions already approved, zero chat.message) showed the invite on top of them, and the same happened — much more seriously — in the session that execution activation uses, with dozens of real events (tool.call, tool.result, task events) and no chat.message/agent.response: the invite covered the entire execution history. A new session is the only one with no event at all — that's the right question.

    2. conviteVisivel waits for useSessionEvents to finish loading. With a cold cache (page reload), session could arrive while events was still [] — the default of data?.items, indistinguishable from "truly empty session" until the first fetch resolved. Without the !eventsQuery.isPending gate, the invite flickered over sessions with a large history.

    3. The "thinking" indicator (bubble with the 3 dots) only turns on after 5s with no text arriving. Before, it turned on the instant streaming/statusAgent became truthy — visual noise on most turns, which answer in under a second. A useEffect arms a setTimeout(…, 5000) when the turn starts with no text yet, and disarms it (via cleanup) as soon as the first delta arrives or the turn ends before the deadline. Real text (streamingText) still appears immediately, never waits for the timer — only the empty indicator gained patience.

    4. handleReadiness got the same safety net that handleSend already had. confirmReadiness is, like sendAgentMessage, a synchronous GenServer.call in the engine (up to 120s) — and the Phoenix channel may not have finished connecting (ticket + join, RN-108) when the turn ends, losing the agent.done broadcast forever. handleSend already called finalizarTurnoDoAgente() as soon as the synchronous call resolved, regardless of whether the channel had delivered the event; handleReadiness had been left out of that fix, and without it the agent's bubble stayed stuck empty (streaming: true, no text) until the page reloaded.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (conversaComecou, conviteVisivel, the pensandoVisivel useEffect right below agenteExibido, and the success block of handleReadiness)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.convite-so-em-sessao-vazia.test.tsx (an empty session shows the invite; a session with git-bootstrap events or with dozens of tool.call/tool.result hides it; eventsQuery.isPending holds the invite until the first fetch resolves), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.pista-e-status.test.tsx (finding B — the indicator doesn't appear before 5s, appears after, disappears on the first delta, and never appears when the turn ends before the deadline), and apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.readiness-turno-preso.test.tsx (confirmReadiness resolving without onAgentDone reconciles the state the same way SessionPage.turno-preso.test.tsx already proves for handleSend)
    • Origin: LIVE investigation of the product in Chrome — all three reproduced manually before the fix.

    RN-136 — The actionable handoff card in the chat only considers those who CONVERSE on this screen

    Since ADR 0185 (RN-658) the Criativo→PO handoff that "I'm ready" requested is born and accepted in the same call, on behalf of whoever clicked — its accept card only appears when that acceptance doesn't fit (a session predating the ADR, a brief that didn't come from the click) or failed. The filter below doesn't change.

    OfferInfraHandoffUseCase offers the handoff to Infra and, in the SAME confirmation, offers it to the Dev Lead right after (PHASE 14d) — two separate calls, the Infra one first. handoffs (what SessionPage.tsx reads from useHandoffs) comes sorted by createdAt ASC (DrizzleHandoffRepository#findBySession), and the offeredHandoff that decides which card becomes "actionable" resolved with a plain .find() — always the oldest offered. Since the Infra Lead isn't conversational (it isn't in AGENTES_DE_CHAT, and is never accepted by this screen), the Dev Lead's card would only become actionable after someone accepted the Infra one in a place this screen doesn't show — in practice, never: the user only saw "accept handoff from Infra" and the invite for the Dev Lead stayed invisible behind it.

    The filter restricts offeredHandoff to handoffs addressed to an agent of AGENTES_DE_CHAT. The handoff to Infra is still narrated in the thread — its handoff.offered becomes a silent divider, like any offer that isn't "the current one" — only the card with a button now ignores it: Infra never had (nor needs to have) a way to be accepted from here.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (offeredHandoff)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-devlead-e-colapso.test.tsx, describe "problema 1" — older Infra + newer Dev Lead resolves to the Dev Lead's card, never to Infra's; only Infra offered shows no card at all
    • Origin: code investigation + live test in Chrome — the handoff to the Dev Lead never became actionable after PHASE 14d

    RN-137 — "Activate execution" has an inline shortcut on the Dev Lead card, without lowering the role requirement

    The accept card of the handoff to the Dev Lead gained an "Activate execution" button next to the "Follow the execution in Executors" link — a shortcut for those who already know what they want, without going through the conversation. It calls the SAME activateExecution that the Overview uses, now with sessionId (the open chat session) as originSessionId — without this the session that brought the Dev Lead stayed active forever, even with execution (in a SEPARATE session) having already taken off through this shortcut (RN-135).

    The route still requires maintainer — DELIBERATELY not aligned with the developer that already suffices to accept the handoff on the same card. Whoever activates becomes the session.createdBy of the execution session, and that's the role that ProposeActionUseCase resolves (ResolveEffectiveRoleUseCase.forProject) as the EFFECTIVE one of every git_commit/git_push/pr_open the dev agents propose from then on — it's the same reason that already justified the maintainer of the Overview button (ExecutionController#activate). Lowering the requirement here would silently invert that resolution: every PR the execution opened would go from auto_approve to require_approval whenever whoever clicked was developer, without anyone having decided that. Whoever isn't maintainer receives the api's real sentence ("Papel insuficiente para esta ação", via mensagemDaApi) instead of a generic error.

    No client-side module_map gate: when this card exists, the Arquiteto has already defined it — it's the artifact that precedes the handoff offer to the Dev Lead —, so replicating the Overview's disabled={!hasModuleMap} would lock the button for nothing. The rare case (inconsistent session) falls into the same catch that handles 403/other errors.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (handleActivateExecution), apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts (activateExecution gained originSessionId)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-devlead-e-colapso.test.tsx, describe "problema 2" — the click calls activateExecution with sessionId; 403 shows the api's real sentence; the button coexists with the accept-handoff one and with the Executors link
    • Origin: code investigation + live test in Chrome — shortcut request, with the role divergence confirmed and kept by decision (not fixed by automatic alignment)

    RN-138 — An agent's messages collapse after it passes the baton

    The chat timeline showed everything always expanded, without grouping by author — in a long session with Criativo, PO, and Arquiteto taking turns, the history of those who already left the scene competed for space with whoever is speaking now. Each timeline entry gained an agentId (the actor.id of whoever generated it, when it's an agent — agent.response, agent.error, epic/story created by the PO, approval card); user entries and those that mark a TRANSITION (handoff, story promotion) are left without agentId on purpose, because they're cut points by nature.

    A CONSECUTIVE sequence of the same agentId becomes a collapsible header (the design system's Disclosure, closed by default — agent name + count, reopens with one click) when both conditions hold:

    1. the agent has already passed the baton — there's a handoff from it (fromAgent) with status: 'accepted' (the same truth the event log's handoff.accepted records, without needing to rebuild by joining events); and
    2. none of its actions is pending — the check is by actor.id across ALL the session's actions, not only those in the current sequence: an open approval race can't stay hidden behind a click at ANY point of the thread.

    A sequence of 1 entry never collapses — "So-and-so · 1 message" in place of the message itself gains nothing. Any entry without agentId, or from a different agent, breaks the current sequence exactly as a change of agent does — it only groups what is really consecutive.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (TimelineEntry.agentId, timelineAgrupada), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.agentGroup*)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-devlead-e-colapso.test.tsx, describe "problema 3" — collapses with the baton passed and no pending action; doesn't collapse without an accepted handoff; doesn't collapse with a pending action from the same agent; a message from another author in the middle breaks the grouping
    • Origin: code investigation + live test in Chrome — collapse request, with AgentTimelineTree/timeline-tree.ts as the reference of FORM (group by agent, name + count, open/closed), adapted to the chat's heterogeneous interleaved timeline

    RN-144 — The Criativo tab does not list the current execution session

    The session that receives execution.activated and the dev agents' tool-call events must be born with kind: 'criativa' — a structural rule (RN-097, garantirQuePodeAtivarExecucao), without which the event is refused. Since ProjectSessionsTab (the Criativo tab, RN-104) lists sessions filtering only by session.kind === 'criativa', the execution session appeared MIXED in the list next to real ideations — opening it in SessionPage.tsx shows a whole timeline of dev agent tool-calls, looking (to the user) like "the dev writing in the Criativo's chat". Confirmed live: a real session with 35+ dev agent events appeared normally in the list, next to real ideation sessions.

    The fix reuses the signal that RN-139 already exposes — useActiveExecutionSession/GET /projects/:projectId/execution/session — instead of the backend computing a new field per session (hasExecutionActivated or equivalent). The Criativo tab fetches the current session and excludes it from the rendered list:

    • the fetch only runs on the Criativo tab (enabled off when kind !== 'criativa') — the Chat tab never made that call and still does not;
    • the filter is by id, after the existing filter by kind — it does not change what the list IS, only what it EXCLUDES.

    Deliberate scope decision: this covers only the CURRENT execution, not OLD executions already closed (execution.activated written in a session that is now closed). Computing that would require the backend to annotate, per session, whether it has the event written — a change in the repository and in the listing endpoint, for a residual case: an OLD execution session appears with the closed badge, which already signals "this is not an active ideation" in a much less ambiguous way than the current one (which appeared active, indistinguishable from an ideation in progress). If this becomes confusing again in practice, the way out is the listing endpoint returning the signal per session — not one more filter in the front per old session.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.tsx (ProjectSessionsTab)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.test.tsx — the current one disappears from the Criativo list with normal sessions next to it, the Chat tab does not call the current-execution fetch, and without a current execution (null) the list appears whole
    • Origin: finding from code investigation + live test — a real session with active execution appearing mixed in the Criativo tab

    RN-147 — The collapsed group header shows the agent's icon, not just the name

    The Disclosure of timelineAgrupada (RN-138) received only the STRING of the name in titulo — each expanded message already has an avatar (.avatar + icon), and the collapsed header lost that visual cue precisely where it helps most to scan the thread.

    AvatarDoAgente reuses the SAME .avatar box as the expanded messages, but the chosen icon is the ROSTER's (AGENTS[id].icon) — the same source that already identifies "who is talking" in the streaming indicator (agenteExibido.icon, RN-131) — and not the per-event-TYPE icon each expanded entry uses (ModelIcon in agent.response, StackIcon in backlog.*_created, AlertCircleIcon in agent.error). A collapsed group can mix those entry types from the same agent; the header represents the AGENT, not its last entry, and only the roster icon is stable for that. Without id, or an agent outside the roster, it degrades to ModelIcon — never to an empty box.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (AvatarDoAgente, timelineAgrupada), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.agentGroupTitulo)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.arquiteto-modelo-icone.test.tsx, describe "problema 3" — the collapsed header has the PATH of the PO's icon (UserIcon), not just a generic decorative SVG
    • Origin: code investigation — Disclosure already accepted ReactNode in titulo; passing the avatar together with the name was what was missing

    RN-148 — Stories with promotion pending at the same time become a carousel, not N cards

    The PO creates stories one by one, and each backlog.story_promotion_proposed (RN-126) became a standalone card in the timeline — in a batch of several stories, that stacked N identical cards competing for the same space, mixed with the rest of the narration.

    A batch is the set of promotion proposals STILL PENDING in the session, evaluated on each render — not "created in sequence without interruption". The criterion is the SAME one each standalone card already used on its own to decide whether it became an actionable card or a divider (no later backlog.story_transitioned/backlog.story_promotion_returned with the same storyId), only looked at at once for the whole session:

    • 0 or 1 pending: nothing changes — the usual standalone card (the degradation is deliberate: a one-slide carousel gains nothing by becoming a carousel).
    • 2+ pending at the same time: they become ONE Carousel (new in the design system, apps/web/src/components/ui/Carousel.tsx), inserted at the position of the FIRST proposal still pending; the others disappear as individual cards — each one becomes a SLIDE of it. Each slide shows the same sentence as the standalone card ("história … pronta, aguardando sua promoção"), a summary/RF if the payload carries one (today it does not — see below), and the Promover/Devolver buttons of that specific story, calling the SAME promoteStories/returnStory as always.
    • "Aprovar todas" in the carousel header calls promoteStories with the ids of ALL pending ones in a single call — the endpoint was already a batch (promoteStories(projectId, storyIds[]), RN-048), so there was no contract change at all, only in who builds the list.
    • A story resolved (promoted or returned) while the carousel is open leaves the batch on the next render (the events query is invalidated in both actions) — if only 1 pending is left, the carousel dissolves on its own and the card becomes standalone again.

    resumo/RF in the slide is a READY field, not used: CreateStoryUseCase today only writes storyId/epicId/title in the payload of backlog.story_promotion_proposed — no description nor functional requirements. The slide already knows how to display description/rf if the payload ever carries them (it degrades to the title alone until then); extending the payload was left out of this delivery, since it was not asked for.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/ui/Carousel.tsx (new component, generic navigation), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (promocoesPendentes, ehLevaDeHistorias, StorySlide, handlePromoteAll), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.storySlide)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/ui/Carousel.test.tsx (navigation, ARIA, only the current slide mounted, index clamped when the list shrinks), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.carrossel-historias.test.tsx (3+ pending become a carousel; "Aprovar todas" sends the whole batch; single promotion and return keep working from a navigated slide; 1 pending degrades to the simple card; a resolved story leaves the batch and the carousel recomputes the count)
    • Origin: user request — stories produced in batch by the PO were hard to decide one by one in the thread

    RN-152 — A task's branch says which dev agent and module it belongs to, in the Code tab dropdown

    CodeBranchPicker already listed every branch of the repository, including those of the dev agents (feature/task-XXXXXXXX, Engine.Dev.AgentIo), but with no hint at all of who created it — only the raw name. ReadProjectCodeUseCase.branches solves this without an extra call to the git provider: the 8 chars after feature/task- are exactly the first hyphenated group of the task's uuid ("feature/task-" <> String.slice(to_string(row.task_id), 0, 8), not an arbitrary substring), so they match against TaskRepository .findByProjectAndIdPrefix (join by PROJECT, so an 8-char prefix never leaks a task from another project). The task's assignedTo is the agent_id (dev-<modulo>/dev-<modulo>-2, RN-087); the module is resolved by comparing against the project's CURRENT module_map with the SAME functions that generated them (devAgentId/extraDevAgentId in activate-execution.use-case.ts) — never by reverse regex, which would degenerate into ambiguity for a module name with a special character.

    producedBy: { agentId, moduleId } | null is honest degradation, the same way ahead/behind already are: null for a branch without the pattern (the user's manual one, or main/dev/qa), and also when the pattern matches but the task/module are no longer resolvable (task deleted, module removed from the current map) — never an invented value. In the dropdown, each branch produced by a dev gets the agent's icon and color (AGENTS/agents.ts, RN-087), reusing the SAME degradation that apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts already uses for the live roster: a module without a fixed key in AGENTS inherits the icon/color of dev-backend.

    • Where: apps/api/src/application/use-cases/git/read-project-code.use-case.ts (branches/producedBy/moduloDoAgente), apps/api/src/application/ports/backlog-repository.port.ts (TaskRepository.findByProjectAndIdPrefix), apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/backlog.repository.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/git/dto/code.response.dto.ts (CodeBranchProducedByResponseDto), apps/web/src/lib/api-types.ts (CodeBranchProducedBy), apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBranchPicker.tsx (IconeDoAgenteProdutor/defDoAgenteProdutor)
    • Test: apps/api/test/application/use-cases/git/read-project-code.use-case.spec.ts (describe "producedBy da branch de task" — task resolved with module and with the extra agent -2, a branch outside the pattern never gets producedBy even with a task of matchable prefix, prefix without a task in the project, module removed from the current map and task without an owner yet degrade to null), apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBranchPicker.test.tsx (a task branch shows the owning dev agent's badge; a branch without the pattern gets no badge at all)

    RN-156 — the 5s wait indicator has fixed text, without interpolating the agent

The agent's name is already visible in the header as soon as the real text streaming starts; repeating it in the wait indicator did not help reading.

    RN-157 — epic/story creation by the PO becomes a compact notice, not a full bubble

    RN-158 — Light Markdown with highlighting in the chat

    agent.response in the Session thread renders a subset of Markdown (bold **texto**, italic *texto*/_texto_, inline code `texto`, heading #/##/###, list -/1., link [texto](url) and code fence ```linguagem), via its own regex parser (apps/web/src/lib/markdown.ts), with no new dependency. chat.message (text typed by the user) stays literal — Markdown only applies to the OUTPUT of an agent/LLM, never to human input.

    Security: the parser never produces HTML — it returns a data tree that MarkdownMessage.tsx converts into React elements directly (never dangerouslySetInnerHTML). A link only becomes a clickable <a href> when the URL scheme is http, https or relative (/..., #...); any other scheme (javascript:, data:, etc.) degrades to the link text, never to an executable href.

    Code inside a fence gets per-token highlighting, reusing highlightLine/highlightFile from apps/web/src/routes/code/highlight.ts — the same function that already colors the Code tab. sh/bash gained their own shell keyword vocabulary (before, they only had the # line comment mapped and fell into the JS fallback). Fences ```sh/```bash get the visual aesthetics of a terminal ($ prompt per command line), consistent with the $ comando that ApprovalCard already uses for the terminal action.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/markdown.ts, apps/web/src/components/ui/MarkdownMessage.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/highlight.ts
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/markdown.test.ts, apps/web/src/components/ui/MarkdownMessage.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/highlight.test.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.markdown-resposta.test.tsx
    • Origin: PR #288 — agent responses with lists, code and links arrived as raw text in the thread, with no structure at all

    RN-159 — Generated Artifacts grouped by agent

A PR still without pullRequestUrl (pending execution) appears in the panel without becoming a clickable link.

    Out of scope, by a decision recorded in a code comment (ContextAside in SessionPage.tsx): module_map/C4 — they are the project's CURRENT state (one current version, overwritten on each generation), not an artifact dated per SESSION like PR/epic/story; the Overview tab (ProjectOverviewTab.tsx) is already their place today, without an anchor of their own — addressing that is out of the scope of this delivery.

The source is the SAME the Backlog tab already uses (useBacklog, ProjectBacklogTab.tsx, the same queryKey ['backlog', projectId]) — no new round trip.
