    RN-161 — Accepting the handoff to the Dev Lead chains execution activation when the effective role already authorizes it

    Revised by RN-677 (ADR 0194, owner's decision on 10/01): accepting the handoff to the Dev Lead stopped chaining the activation of execution, for EVERY role — the acceptance only brings it to PLAN, and what activates is the APPROVAL of its plan (propose_execution_plan). The text below describes the fusion as it was.

    handleAcceptHandoff (SessionPage.tsx) chains activateExecution automatically when toAgent === 'dev-lead' AND the EFFECTIVE role of whoever accepts — read from the same useCurrentWorkspaceWithRole() that already authorizes "Auto mode" (RN-153) and the Approvals/Settings screens — is owner or maintainer. For developer (or a role not yet resolved), the current flow remains untouched: accepting activates nothing, and "Ativar execução" remains as a second button. The check is client-only — POST .../execution/activate still requires maintainer on the backend (RN-137); the fusion only avoids a redundant click for whoever already had both roles. It reuses the SAME handleActivateExecution that the "Ativar execução" button already calls, which handles its own error (toast + mensagemDaApi) and never rethrows — it avoids a late activation error being reported as "não foi possível aceitar o handoff".

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (podeFundirHandoffComExecucao, handleAcceptHandoff)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-devlead-e-colapso.test.tsx, describe "problema 4"
    • Architectural decision: ADR 0069
    • Origin: user request (approved design)

    RN-172 — Handoff and approval are the OUTCOME of the turn

    In the session thread, the offered handoff and the approval card appear AFTER the last utterance of the turn in which they were born — never in the middle of it.

    This does not fix ordering: RN-155 remains fully in force, and the timeline stays ordered by the event log's seq. What the log records is the truth: po_server.ex (run_turn/2) emits, in the SAME iteration, the turn's agent.response, THEN the tool.call of offer_handoff (which writes handoff.offered) and ONLY THEN recurses to the closing agent.response. The handoff's seq is honestly lower than the last utterance's. The same holds for proposed_action.created, which is born in the middle of the turn while the agent still has something to say. Showing "passed the baton" or "approve this" in the middle of the conversation is a wrong reading of correct data — so the rule is about PRESENTATION, applied in a separate and explicit pass (afundarDesfechos), after the sort by seq, and not hidden in a three-term comparator.

    Different turns never mix. An outcome sinks to the end of the stretch right below it and stops at the first entry that fails any of the three conditions:

    1. same turn — the turn is the seq of the last OPENING before the entry, and an opening is an event with actor user (chat.message, agent.activated, story promotion/return). It protects the case in which the boundary between two turns has no VISIBLE entry at all: agent.activated opens a turn and does not become a thread item.
    2. same author — in an EXECUTION session several agents write without the user speaking a single time, and all of them stay in the same turn; one's outcome cannot cross another's utterance.
    3. is not an outcome — two consecutive outcomes preserve the order between them (the Infra's handoff.offered before the Dev Lead's, in the same confirmation).

    The system actor does NOT open a turn: it is infrastructure noise in the middle of the thread, not a decision of whoever is conversing.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (aberturasDeTurno, turnoDoSeq, afundarDesfechos, and the autor/turno/desfecho fields of TimelineEntry)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.ordenacao-e-avisos.test.tsx (describes "RN-172 — turno e desfecho (unidade)" and "RN-172 — a sequência REAL do engine, renderizada")
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "a passagem de bastão do PO para o arquiteto está aparecendo acima da última mensagem do PO" and "a mensagem de aprovação apareceu acima do chat do arquiteto até ele finalizar a resposta"

    RN-173 — The thread follows everything that grows, and only for whoever was already at the end

    The chat scrolls to the end when the content grows — and not only when a new event arrives. The two timeline sources are SEPARATE queries (events and actions), and the height still changes without either of them: opening/closing a Disclosure (per-agent collapse of RN-138, the approval card's "Detalhes"), Markdown reflowing, a diagram rendering later. That is why there are two mechanisms: the effect's dependencies cover what React knows (events.length, actions.length, streamingText), and a ResizeObserver over the thread's CONTENT covers what only the layout knows.

    The guard remains unchanged and deliberate: it only scrolls whoever is already less than 120px from the end. Whoever scrolled up to reread the history is not dragged — the thread follows the conversation, it does not hijack the reading.

    In the same thread, the approval card no longer takes up the whole 780px of the column: it gets a ceiling of 560px and is centered, as .handoffCard/.handoffDivider already are. Indenting 45px like the bubbles would be wrong — the card is nobody's utterance, it is a decision asked of the user. The Approvals tab queue does not change: there the card MUST fill the grid column. Since AT-322 the card is ONE single card on every surface and fills the container — what applies the ceiling and the centering in the thread is its container (.acaoNoFio), no longer a variant of the card.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (acompanharOFim and the two effects that call it); apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.module.css (.card) and apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.acaoNoFio)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.ordenacao-e-avisos.test.tsx (describe "RN-173 — o fio acompanha o que cresce", with the case of the user having scrolled up)
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "o scroll do chat deve ficar sempre no final seguindo o chat" and "aprovação mal diagramado deve ficar ao centro"

    RN-174 — An action that triggers an agent turn arms the thread indicator

    The "the agent is working" indicator (the three dots after 5s, RN-131/RN-156) only appears while streaming or statusAgent hold, and they were turned on in three places: the composer (handleSend), the readiness confirmations and the Phoenix channel. Every screen action that triggers a synchronous turn in the engine now arms it too.

    There are two, and neither of them is the composer:

    1. Answering the structured questions form — AnswerStructuredQuestionUseCase reuses SendAgentMessageUseCase (RN-162), and the call only resolves after the whole turn.
    2. Returning a story to the PO — ReturnStoryUseCase calls reviseStory, which is handle_call({:revise, …}) in po_server: the HTTP response waits for the PO to rewrite the story.

    The Phoenix channel does not cover the gap, and that is what makes the fix necessary rather than redundant: when it has not finished connecting yet (ticket + join, RN-108) the agent.status "working" broadcast has no listener and is lost — the screen stays in absolute silence for dozens of seconds, which is indistinguishable from "nothing is going to happen".

    The pair is iniciarTurnoDoAgente(agente) before the await (the channel's agent.status may arrive first, and without the agent pinned the indicator would be born without knowing who is speaking) and finalizarTurnoDoAgente() in the finally — on BOTH paths, because an error that left streaming on would lock the composer until the next turn. Resolving the call is a signal of the end of the turn as reliable as the channel's agent.done, and finalizarTurnoDoAgente is idempotent. The 5s deadline does not change: a turn that answers quickly still shows nothing.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (iniciarTurnoDoAgente, handleReturnStory, StructuredQuestionCard)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.perguntas-estruturadas.test.tsx and apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.promocao-inline-e-volta.test.tsx (RN-174 describes, with the failure case proving the indicator does not get stuck)
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "caso a web, api e engine demore mais de 5s para ter uma resposta, a web deve apresentar uma animação no chat mostrando que o agente está pensando"

    RN-176 — A Markdown table in the thread becomes a real table

    The chat's light Markdown (RN-158) now recognizes a GFM table and renders it with the design system's Table — the same component as Settings, Spending and Executors, not a <table> of its own: "how Brabo draws a table" is a single decision. Before, the Module Map table that the Arquiteto writes in the response came out as a paragraph with literal pipes.

    What distinguishes a table from prose with | is the separator line, and it is mandatory as in GFM: without it the block remains a paragraph, so "escolha entre a | b | c" does not become a table by mistake. The header rules the number of columns — a short line gets an empty cell, a long line loses the excess —, an escaped \| stays inside the cell, and the alignment comes from the separator's colons. Zero new dependency: the parser remains its own, regex-based, and the data tree keeps becoming React elements directly (no dangerouslySetInnerHTML).

    The artifact.module_map artifact remains OUTSIDE the thread, and the choice is deliberate: it is the project's CURRENT state, not an artifact dated per session — the same decision already recorded in RN-159 —, and it lives in the Overview. What the user asked for was the table inside the message, and that is what is now drawn; the fix serves ANY agent that writes a table, and not only the Module Map.

    In the bubble, the table scrolls horizontally instead of squeezing columns: the thread is ~700px and a module_map has 4 columns.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/markdown.ts (table block, celulasDaLinha), apps/web/src/components/ui/MarkdownMessage.tsx (TabelaMarkdown), apps/web/src/components/ui/MarkdownMessage.module.css
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/markdown.test.ts (describe "tabela (RN-176)"), apps/web/src/components/ui/MarkdownMessage.test.tsx
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "a tabela dentro da mensagem do Mapa de Módulos do arquiteto tem que ficar bem estruturada em formato tabela, utilizar design system do próprio Brabo"

    RN-177 — The log shows everything, and the history collapses by ORIGIN

    The activity feed hid six event types (tool.call, tool.result, agent.response, agent.delta, agent.status, context.compacted) without offering any alternative: whoever wanted to see what the agent and the harness exchange had to open the database. The filter remains, and remains off by default — its reason has not changed (116 of 193 real events were of these types, see isMachineEvent) —, but it became a choice: a "Eventos de máquina" button on the same rail as the type chips.

    Showing everything only solves half: a long session becomes a list nobody goes through. That is why the 5 most recent stay open and the rest goes into collapses by ORIGIN — a NEW classification, which does not replace the ActivityKind: kind says which SUBJECT the event talks about (commit, PR, permission) and decides icon and color; origem says which LAYER it came from, and it is what makes the history readable in handfuls. The six come from the data that exists — actor.kind and the type prefix —, never from assumption:

    origin what falls into it
    harness tool.*, toolloop.*, agent.status, context.compacted
    llm agent.response, agent.delta, llm.*
    usuario any event with actor.kind === 'user'
    sistema any event with actor.kind === 'system'
    agente remaining agent.*, handoff.*, delegation.*, an agent's chat.*
    eventos the domain event log (backlog, git, PR, artifact, bootstrap…)

    The precedence is what makes the classification predictable, and it is in the order of the ifs: mechanism (harness, llm) beats actor, because a tool.call belongs to the harness whatever the actor; and actor beats agent prefix, because chat.message exists on both sides and what distinguishes them is who spoke. A type that nobody foresaw falls into eventos — it never disappears, never invents a category.

    The same rule holds in the session THREAD, with the axis inverted: the thread is ascending (the newest next to the composer), so the last 5 entries stay open at the bottom and the collapsed history stays at the TOP. The cut is over the list already grouped by agent (RN-138) — what counts is what the user sees, and a collapse of twelve messages is ONE entry on screen. Revised in the thread by RN-644: there the cut counts only MESSAGES, goes back to the opening of the turn and the history is ONE chronological block, not groups by origin (which put the answer above the question). The log panel remains as described here.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/activity.ts:94 (OrigemDeEvento), :125 (origemDoEvento), :152 (agruparPorOrigem); apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.tsx:34 (the cut of 5), :66 (the toggle); apps/web/src/routes/session-fio.tsx:119 (the thread cut), :291 (dividirFio); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:516 (fio) toggle); apps/web/src/routes/session-fio.tsx:119 (the thread cut), :291 (dividirFio); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:516 (fio)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/activity-origem.test.ts, apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.test.tsx (describe "ordem, agrupamento e o toggle de máquina"), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.painel-e-agrupamento.test.tsx (describe "RN-177")
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "em log de eventos mostrar também log do sistema, concentrar a mensagem em grupo, ou seja mantém as últimas 5 mensagens mas abaixo vira o grupo de log de eventos, sistema, llm, harness, agente, usuário"

    RN-178 — The session panel reads from last to first, and the rules list paginates

    The four sections of the context panel (business rules, generated artifacts, touched files and event log) were ascending: they opened at the start of the session. In a session of thousands of events that delivers the wrong screen — whoever opens the panel wants what just happened. All four became descending, including inside the backlog tree (RN-179).

    A consequence that came along: the feed's "Carregar mais antigos" button (RN-099) switched sides. It was ABOVE the list because the list was ascending and the past was at the top; with the order inverted the past is at the end, and a button at the top would ask to scroll in the direction opposite to what it loads — the same argument as before, with the sign flipped.

    And above 5 rules the list paginates, instead of growing endlessly: the panel has the width of one column and an ideation session goes past twenty rules effortlessly. The current page is resolved by clamp (min(pagina, total - 1)) and not by a synchronization effect: a new rule arriving through the poll lengthens the list, and a useEffect would render once with the invalid page before correcting it. With 5 or fewer, the paginator does not exist — a control that paginates nothing is noise taking up height.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ContextAside.tsx:109 (REGRAS_POR_PAGINA) and the ordering of the four sections in the same file; apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.tsx:98 (the descending sort)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.painel-e-agrupamento.test.tsx (describe "RN-178"), apps/web/src/components/ActivityFeed.test.tsx
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "mostrar log de eventos, arquivos tocados e regras de negócio sempre em ordem do último para o primeiro de acordo com a data" and "caso as regras de negócio ficar acima de 5, deve-se paginar"

    RN-179 — The PO's artifact is a TREE: epic → story → task

    The "Artefatos gerados" panel (RN-159) listed epic and story side by side, flat, and ignored backlog.task_created — precisely what a dev agent picks up to work on. The three now form a tree, with the child inside a collapse of the parent, and tasks are born CLOSED: an epic with thirty tasks would take over the whole panel without anyone having asked.

    The parentage comes from the link the event already carries — backlog.story_created writes epicId, backlog.task_created writes storyId —, never from proximity in the log. A node whose parent is not among the loaded events rises to the root instead of being hung on the nearest epic: inventing parentage is worse than showing the loose node, and a task whose story fell outside the window is a normal case, not an error.

    Each level remains a link to the Backlog, and the children's collapse comes BELOW the line instead of inside the header — a Disclosure header is a <button>, and an <a> inside a <button> is invalid HTML and an ambiguous click target. The section header's counter counts the WHOLE tree, not only the roots: saying "3" with eighteen tasks inside would be the same kind of number that corresponds to nothing that RN-151 removed from the sidebar.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-backlog-tree.ts:68 (montarArvoreDeBacklog), apps/web/src/routes/ContextAside.tsx:210 (the roots become items), :225 (the tree count)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.artefatos-gerados.test.tsx (cases "épico/história/tarefa do PO viram árvore" and "nó sem pai carregado aparece na raiz")
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "mostrar também as tarefas criadas no artefato do PO, mas abaixo do épico e ter opção de colapsar"

    RN-180 — The panel says what it is NOT showing

    useSessionEvents fetches { limit: 200, latest: true }, and the context panel read that slice through a prop. Consequence: in a session of thousands of events the four sections showed the tail as if it were the whole session — a business rule captured at the beginning of the ideation simply did not exist, without any warning.

    The panel started reading the same paginated history that the Overview's Activity tab already used (RN-099), with the tail's queryKey shared with the thread — zero extra requests in the poll cycle (RN-090/RN-091). Two things changed with that:

    1. The feed gained the pager the component always had. The props onLoadOlder/hasOlder/loadingOlder are optional since RN-099 and this call site never passed them — that was why the session lost its beginning silently.
    2. A note counts how many events are missing. The number comes from SUBTRACTION over the seq (gapless and per session): menor seq baixado − 1. Never from one more request — the same mechanism as the bell's "+ N mais antigos" (RN-100). Reaching the start of the session, the note disappears, instead of asserting a zero.

    The derived sections read baixados (everything that has already come) and not events (the feed's window of 100): they do not paginate item by item, and cutting them at the window would make them show LESS than they showed before this change. It is the same button that feeds both.

    pausarPoll goes down through useSessionEventHistory to useSessionEvents, and it is not a detail: the refetch interval belongs to each OBSERVER, not to the query. A second observer of the same key with the timer on would resurrect the poll that the screen pauses during the turn — and with it the visual duplicate of the bubble in streaming.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/hooks.ts:262 (the history's pausarPoll), :388 (baixados); apps/web/src/routes/ContextAside.tsx:154 (eventosAnteriores) and the ActivityFeed with the pager, at the end of the same file
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.painel-e-agrupamento.test.tsx (describe "RN-180")
    • Origin: review of the round itself — the ceiling of 200 existed silently in the four sections

    RN-181 — Area delegation appears in the thread

    When an area (QA, Infra) delegates to subagents and consolidates the verdict, the three outcomes the lead records — delegation.completed, delegation.failed and delegation.dispensed — only existed in the log panel. Whoever followed the session saw the gate open and close without any sign that there was a second attempt underneath.

    The three are now narrated in the thread as a compact notice, in the format of RN-157 — not a bubble: it is a notification of what happened inside the area, not an utterance. The SENTENCE comes from classifyEvent, the same as the panel's, because two wordings of the same event diverge on the first payload change; and it already names the subagent and the area, so the lead is not prefixed (it would produce "QA Lead QA Automação concluiu a delegação (qa)").

    The area's external contract does not change (ADR 0038): the thread does not start addressing subagents, it only narrates what the lead has already recorded. The failure origin travels along in delegation.failed, for the same reason as RN-059 — it is what says whether the next step is changing the key, waiting for the provider or opening a bug.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/session-timeline-montagem.tsx:754
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.painel-e-agrupamento.test.tsx (describe "RN-181")
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "quando houver uma nova tentativa e consolidação de algum agente deve apresentar no chat"

    RN-182 — The theme is chosen, persisted and applied before the first paint

    The light theme exists in design/tokens.css since the beginning, under [data-theme='light'], and nothing in apps/web wrote that attribute: the only way to see the light theme was to type the attribute in DevTools. It now becomes reachable.

    The preference lives in localStorage['brabo.theme'], accepts only 'dark' or 'light', and the default is dark — the design system's primary theme. What applies it is apps/web/public/theme-boot.js, synchronous in the <head> and before the bundle: data-theme decides the colors of the whole tokens.css, and applying it after hydration would make the light-theme user see a dark flash on every load.

    It is a file, not an inline script, and the reason is the same that made the fonts self-hosted (ADR 0036): the production image serves under script-src 'self' (docker/web/nginx.conf), without 'unsafe-inline' and without a nonce. Inline would work in pnpm dev:web and would be blocked in the published image — the worst failure mode possible, because it only shows up after the deploy.

    The whole path degrades instead of breaking: localStorage can throw (private mode, storage blocked in an iframe) and theme is a preference, not a function; an unknown value falls to the default instead of becoming a data-theme that the CSS does not know and that would render with no theme at all.

    • Where: apps/web/public/theme-boot.js:41, apps/web/index.html:53
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/tema.test.ts (describe "contrato com o script de boot")
    • ADR: 0074

    RN-183 — The theme preference has one source, and the <html> attribute is the truth

    Reading, writing, toggling and observing the theme is apps/web/src/lib/tema.ts — the button lives in the shell and consumes that API, it never writes data-theme on its own.

    Three decisions inside it:

    1. temaAtual() reads the ATTRIBUTE first, and only then localStorage and the default. The attribute is what the screen is showing; falling back to the stored preference before it would make the UI assert a theme different from what is seen, in the exact case where the boot failed.
    2. lerTemaSalvo() returns null, not the default, when there is no stored preference. Whoever never chose may one day follow the operating system (prefers-color-scheme), and erasing that distinction here would take the information away from whoever decides that later.
    3. observarTema() covers the storage event, which the browser fires in the OTHER tabs of the same origin. Without it two open tabs would stay in different themes until the next reload.

    The key and the default are repeated in public/theme-boot.js because it runs before the bundle and cannot import anything. It is the only duplication able to diverge silently, and that is why the test reads the boot file and fails if the two stop matching.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/tema.ts:73
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/tema.test.ts
    • ADR: 0074

    RN-184 — Contrast is measured in BOTH themes

    While the light theme was unreachable (RN-182), measuring only the dark one was honest: measuring a screen nobody can open is measuring an intention. With the theme button, no longer measuring the light one would hide half of the product's visible surface — so the pairs are now enforced in both themes, with the same floor (4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for interface elements).

    For that, six light-theme tokens changed value. The most demanding background of the light theme is --code-bg (paper, #efe4d2, one step away from the surfaces), and whatever passes against it passes against the rest: --accent 3.56 → 4.81, --warning 3.15 → 4.98, --success 3.89 → 5.12, --violet 4.16 → 4.95, --text-muted 2.76 → 5.17, and --accent-hover followed the accent one step below. The dark theme did not change a single value, and its known debt remained locked by the same five numbers (3.89 / 3.10 / 3.88 / 3.88 / 4.41) until RN-640 (ADR 0181), which closed it with the neutral palette and turned it into a floor.

    The light theme's --text-muted was not debt: at 2.40:1 over --surface-2 it failed even the interface element floor, which is the lowest that exists. It was a defect, and the light theme has no reason to be worse than the primary one.

    • Where: design/tokens.css:208
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/contraste.test.ts, apps/web/src/design-contraste.test.ts, apps/web/test/design-contraste.test.ts
    • ADR: 0074

    RN-185 — The eight syntax roles, and the handoff's value only comes in measured

    The highlighting palette was three tokens of its own (--syntax-function, --syntax-comment, --syntax-operator) and five reuses of semantic ones (--accent, --warning, --violet, --success, --text-primary). It is this reuse that made the palette measured only in the dark theme: in the light one the semantic tokens failed against the paper --code-bg.

    It now has the handoff's eight roles, with the --syntax-* prefix the repository already uses, and each with its own value per theme. Naming the eight is what lets highlighting diverge from the semantic tokens on the day it needs to.

    The handoff's value only comes in when the measurement approves. Against the handoff's own --code-bg, five of the eight fail the 4.5:1 that code text requires — --syn-cm 4.09:1 in dark and 2.32:1 in light, --syn-kw 4.34:1, --syn-str 4.20:1, --syn-fn 4.14:1, --syn-op 4.00:1. Where the handoff fails, the measured number holds: it is the same rule as ADR 0036 — the handoff's intention holds, the mechanism (or the number) that breaks the product does not.

    The five semantic tokens are still what paints (SyntaxTokens.module.css did not change in this delivery) and that is why they are measured alongside the eight: as long as they are the real pixel, the floor is enforced on them. In the light theme each role today has the SAME number as the semantic token that paints it, on purpose — two sources with different numbers for the same pixel would diverge on the first fix made on one side only.

    • Where: design/tokens.css:111
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/contraste.test.ts (describe "contraste — paleta de sintaxe sobre --code-bg")
    • ADR: 0074

    RN-195 — The sidebar collapses into an icon rail, with the preference persisted

    Shell.tsx had a fixed width (248px) and no way to shrink. It now toggles between --sidebar-w (264px) and --sidebar-w-collapsed (62px) — the two tokens that Onda 1/frente A had already created in design/tokens.css — with transition: width .18s ease. Collapsed, it becomes a vertical rail: a square of initials per project (border in the identity color, RN-197) plus an Activities icon; clicking a project in the rail re-expands the bar, opens that project and navigates to it.

    The preference belongs to the user and survives a reload: brabo.sidebar.collapsed ('1'/'0') in apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.ts. It is manual only (colapsadoManual): there was a second, automatic state (autoColapsado), ORed in by the Code tab, and it was removed by ADR 0126 — see RN-201. No screen collapses the sidebar on its own; what is seen collapsed was requested by the user, and is stored.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx:340-351 (state and toggle), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.module.css (.sidebar, .colapsado .sidebar, .trilha*), apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.ts (lerColapsado/gravarColapsado)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.test.ts (describe "colapso"), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B — design_handoff_brabo/README.md section "Navigation shell"

    RN-196 — Expandable projects reveal the project's tabs, N at the same time

    Each project in the sidebar gets a chevron: clicking it expands the row and reveals that project's tab list (Visão geral, Executores, Criativo, Chat, Code, Backlog, Aprovações, Insights, Gastos, Configurações), each one a link to /projects/$projectId?tab=<chave>. Several projects can stay open at the same time — the state is a Set<string> of ids, persisted in brabo.sidebar.open.

    The tab list and the labels come from ABAS_DO_PROJETO (apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts, owned by frente C, running in parallel in this same wave) — the sidebar only reads the exported array, it never rewrites tab names. Shape assumption, to be checked against what frente C delivered: AbaDoProjeto has key: string, label: string and count?: (contagens: ContagensDeAba) => number | undefined, with ContagensDeAba = { promocoesPendentes, aprovacoesPendentes, hipotesesPendentes }. The sidebar can only fill aprovacoesPendentes for free (pendingApprovalsCount already comes in the dashboard summary, RN-151); promocoesPendentes/hipotesesPendentes come in as 0 — computing them would require a new query PER PROJECT OPEN in the sidebar, the same class of N+1 that RN-090/091 closed in the dashboard. The two badges remain correct within the rule of ProjectPage itself; only the preview in the sidebar is partial, and it is a deliberate omission, not a bug.

    The project of the CURRENT route always appears expanded (projetosAbertosEfetivo), even without being in the persisted Set — opening "for free" through the route writes nothing; only the explicit click on the chevron goes into brabo.sidebar.open.

    Since ADR 0211 (AT-404) this list is the project's ONLY navigation: the vertical rail left and it received everything only the rail had — the three groups with headers, the active tab marked, the vertical tablist named by the project, the keyboard (ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Home/End with wraparound, crossing groups) and the FIVE counters, each on its own tab and never summed. The deliberate omission above still holds for a CLOSED project and for the project open outside the frame (in the Session, for example): only Approvals, from the summary. With the project frame mounted, the five numbers come from the SAME hook it uses (useContagensDoProjeto), without a new request. Collapsed, the square of the open project opens a flyout with the same tabs.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx:557 (projetosAbertosEfetivo), :505 (useContagensDoProjeto), :512 (irParaAba), :527 (flyoutAberto), :215 (contagensDoResumo); apps/web/src/routes/AbasDoProjeto.tsx:78 (AbasDoProjeto), :34 (itensDasAbas), :95 (aoTeclar); apps/web/src/lib/contagens-do-projeto.ts:22 (useContagensDoProjeto), :70 (publicarAbaAtiva), :86 (pedirAba); apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.ts (lerProjetosAbertos/gravarProjetosAbertos)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.test.ts (describe "conjuntos"), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx (describe "Shell — abas do projeto aberto (ADR 0211)": the 12 tabs, the five separate counters, the active one, the click and the arrow navigating, the collapsed flyout, the drawer on the phone and the failure case — without the frame mounted, only the summary's number), apps/web/src/routes/AbasDoProjeto.test.tsx (groups, active, counters, keyboard, roving tabindex, link per project), apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.test.tsx
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B; ADR 0211 (AT-404)

    RN-197 — Two project colors, two purposes: identity is not status

    The handoff asks for a "project color dot" on the expanded row and the same color principle on the collapsed rail. The product already had a dot there — NavStatusDot — but it is status (budget/recent activity, derived without its own query from useProjectsSummary/useProjectsStatus, RN-039), not identity: its color CHANGES over time.

    Decision, documented instead of resolved: the expanded row keeps showing only NavStatusDot (it is actionable information; duplicating a second dot next to it would be noise). The IDENTITY color — stable per project, a deterministic hash of the id over a fixed palette of tokens (corDoProjeto, no new table, the same idea as AGENTS[key].color) — appears only on the collapsed rail, as the border of the initials square, which is where there is no room for the two dots and where identity (not status) is what helps to find the right project among several similar squares.

    In the same way, the handoff asks for a "badge with the total of last iterations" on the project; the product uses pendingApprovalsCount since RN-151, which is later than the handoff and fixes a real defect (a number that did not correspond to anything actionable when clicked). This badge does not change — RN-151 remains in force, and it is the handoff that diverges here.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.ts (corDoProjeto), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx (NavStatusDot, comment on the divergence)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.test.ts (describe "corDoProjeto")
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B — divergence between design_handoff_brabo/CHECKLIST-CONFRONTO.md and RN-151

    RN-199 — Theme button in the footer, functional when collapsed

    The sidebar footer gets a sun/moon button that consumes the API of apps/web/src/lib/tema.ts from Onda 1/frente A (temaAtual, alternarTema, observarTema) without reimplementing anything — only the BUTTON is new. It works collapsed (62px): the text label disappears, but aria-label/title keep describing the current theme and the click keeps toggling.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx:130-148 (BotaoDeTema)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/tema.test.ts (the API, unchanged by this RN) — Shell.test.tsx does not duplicate the theme suite, it only mounts the Shell
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B

    RN-200 — Only Projects and Activities as global items

    The two route-less items of the nav footer ("Chat global"/"Configurações", title="em breve") are removed. The handoff is explicit: only Projects and Activities are GLOBAL items — everything else is scoped to a project. "Configurações" still exists, as a project TAB (RN-196) inside the expanded row — what goes away is the loose item with no destination.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx (the .globalNav/ .inertNavItem block from FASE 17a was removed, with no global replacement)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx (describe "sem itens globais inertes")
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B — design_handoff_brabo/README.md section "Navigation shell"

    RN-201 — Active project/tab persist across pages; the sidebar collapse is ONLY the user's

    Two final handoff keys: brabo.project (the active project) and brabo.tab (the active tab) — written when the user clicks a tab link IN THE SIDEBAR (LinhaDeAba/the project name link). ?tab= in the URL only counts as an INITIAL deep link (project-tabs.ts, FASE 24) — switching tabs inside ProjectPage.tsx is local state and does not write to the URL after the first load, so these keys are the only way for the preference to survive from one navigation to another. This half never changed.

    The Code tab's auto-collapse LEFT (ADR 0126). Until then the Code tab collapsed the sidebar by itself to give width to the editor, without writing a preference: AutoCollapseContext/useAutoCollapseSidebar in sidebar-state.ts, registered by ProjectCodeTab.tsx (the only caller) and ORed with the manual collapse inside the Shell. The context, the hook, the autoColapsado state, the Provider around <Outlet /> and the disabled of the collapse button — which existed only to protect that path — were all removed.

    The reason is structural: with the project's vertical rail (routes/ProjectRail.tsx, removed in ADR 0211) always present, keeping the auto-collapse would put the Shell's icon rail right against the project rail — TWO adjacent, permanent vertical rails, on the heaviest tab of the product.

    What now holds: the sidebar collapse is a decision of the USER, and only theirs. It stays manual, stays persisted in brabo.sidebar.collapsed (RN-195), and no screen collapses the sidebar on its own.

    The cost, accepted and declared: the Code tab now opens with the sidebar EXPANDED (264px) + the project rail (180px) + the rail of the CodeShell itself (48px) — 492px of frame before the first character of code, MEASURED in the browser, against ~110px before (62px of collapsed sidebar + 48px of the Code rail). Collapsing manually remains possible and still produces the Shell rail next to the project rail — the difference is that now it was the user who chose, not the system.

    Revised by ADR 0211 (AT-404): the project rail left, and the Code tab frame became 312px with the sidebar expanded (264 + 48 of the CodeShell) and 110px collapsed (62 + 48) — with no double rail in either case. The collapse remains the user's only. The active tab the sidebar marks comes from the project frame (publicarAbaAtiva), not only from brabo.tab.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.ts:80-105 (lerProjetoAtivo/gravarProjetoAtivo, lerAbaAtiva/gravarAbaAtiva, and the comment recording the removal), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.tsx:366-369 (only colapsadoManual is left), apps/web/src/routes/ProjectCodeTab.tsx (no collapse call)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/sidebar-state.test.ts (describe "projeto e aba ativos"), apps/web/src/routes/Shell.test.tsx (describe "Shell — colapso da sidebar": the preference is only born from a click on the button and is always written). The "useAutoCollapseSidebar" describe was removed together with the hook.
    • Origin: PROGRAMA 28, Onda 2, frente B — design_handoff_brabo/CHECKLIST-CONFRONTO.md section 1; the auto-collapse half was revised by ADR 0126

    RN-202 — The sessions tab stays "Chat", never "Chat RAG"

    The most recent design handoff of PROGRAMA 28 calls the consultative tab "Chat RAG" (designs/Brabo Chat.dc.html), but the product does not have that functionality: there is no per-project indexing pipeline, no vector index, no source citation UI. ADR 0075 put embed in the LLMProvider contract — the operation exists and is PROVEN on Ollama —, but nothing CONSUMES it yet. Today's sessions tab is the ordinary consultative Chat of RN-104: an agent answering with the session context, without producing backlog, with no RAG at all behind it.

    Labeling the tab "Chat RAG" today would announce a capability that does not exist — the same mistake that ADR 0042 already refuses for a catalog model ("não ativar modelo descoberto automaticamente"). The label changes on the day the functionality arrives, together with the data behind it — never before.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts:144 (entry key: 'sessions')
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.test.tsx (describe "abas do projeto derivam de um registro só", 'RN-202 — a aba \sessions` continua "Chat", nunca "Chat RAG"'`)
    • ADR: 0078

    RN-203 — The handoff is a visual reference, not a ceiling on how many tabs the product has

    The design handoff lists 7 project tabs (Visão geral, Criativo, Código, Chat RAG, Gastos, Aprovações, Configurações); the registry (apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts) has 10. The three extra ones — executores (RN-121), backlog (RN-048) and insights (the Psicólogo's hypotheses awaiting a decision) — were born AFTER the handoff was designed, all with real data, a counter derived from a query and at least one RN of their own with a test.

    The handoff fixes how each screen should LOOK — colors, typography, spacing, the design of the frame —, and that part was followed to the letter in this same change (header, ruler, scrolling, content width). It does not freeze the tab inventory on the day it was written. Deleting the three to "match" the handoff would destroy information the product already knew how to show, for the wrong reason.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.ts:91 (the REGISTRO)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.test.tsx (describe "abas do projeto derivam de um registro só", 'RN-203 — as 3 abas que o handoff não previu continuam no registro')
    • ADR: 0078

    RN-210 — "Recommended" is real usage and cost, never a computed score

    The "Melhores modelos por capacidade" block (Settings) has no score column. The handoff shows a score per capability (code 9.4, image 9.1…), but it is FICTITIOUS mock data — no provider publishes "code quality" and the product does not measure that anywhere. Computing a number here would be the same "guess dressed up as data" that ADR 0041 forbids for MODEL capability, now about quality.

    Instead, "recomendado"/"alternativa" come from two real signals, among the models THIS workspace's curation marked for that capability (uses, RN-057): first the ones most used by agents of THIS project — the same cascade that resolves the current binding of each agent —, cost (from the catalog, ascending) as tiebreaker. "What the team has already chosen" is the most honest signal available without inventing a score. A capability with no curated model shows "sem cobertura curada" — it never hides the row, the same pattern that the Origin column of ModelsSection already uses for the skipped binding.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/settings/MelhoresModelosPorCapacidadeSection.tsx
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSettingsTab.test.tsx (describe "MelhoresModelosPorCapacidadeSection")
    • ADR: 0077

    RN-211 — Spending per provider on screen is a Ranking, not an invented categorical palette

    The handoff asks for daily bars stacked by provider and a breakdown by provider. The repository's dataviz skill requires validating a categorical palette by script before using it, never by eye: validate_palette.js fails every combination of 3+ tokens from design/tokens.css against at least one of the two themes — --accent+--violet is the only pair that passes in both (several --syntax-* are literally the same hex as --warning/--violet/--success, measured). With 9 providers (ADR 0043) and 2 validated colors, cycling the palette is the anti-pattern the skill itself names ("a 9th series is never a generated hue"), and inventing a new hex would violate this frente's instruction. The breakdown by provider becomes a Ranking — the same piece as "Por modelo"/"Por projeto"/"Por agente e pessoa", without identity by color. The DAILY series per provider is not delivered: sumGroupedBy (ADR 0076) groups by one dimension at a time, and there is no cross aggregation day×provider in this wave's backend.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/spend.ts ("Gasto por PROVIDER na tela" block), apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSpendTab.tsx (GastoDoWorkspace)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSpendTab.test.tsx (describe "a audiência do owner" — 'mostra os cinco recortes do workspace, incluindo provider')
    • ADR: 0076

    RN-212 — The "per project" block is the TokenMeter plugged into the real budget

    The Spending tab gains a block per PROJECT (not per audience): OrcamentoDoProjeto reads GET /projects/:id/budget and plants the result directly into the existing TokenMeter, which already implements the 70/90 thresholds (tokenThreshold) — no new component. Three readings: loading (silent, avoids flashing before the role resolves the audience below), no budget defined (a text note, no CTA) and error (silent — in practice almost always a 403 from someone who has a WORKSPACE role but is not maintainer on the PROJECT, and showing a banner on purpose would be a false alarm for most members, the same pattern already used by the compact TokenMeter of ProjectPage.tsx).

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSpendTab.tsx (OrcamentoDoProjeto)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSpendTab.test.tsx (describe "o orçamento do projeto")

    RN-214 — The local-model savings KPI is left out for lack of a counterfactual price

    TokenMeter already has savingsBRL/savingsPct ready to receive the number, and they remain unfed on purpose. The card would require a COUNTERFACTUAL price — how much the same call would have cost on a paid model — that does not exist anywhere in the product: the catalog (ADR 0042) only freezes the price of the model ACTUALLY used (RN-044), and there is no declared mapping "local model X ~ paid model Y". Inventing it here would be the same class of "score dressed up as data" that RN-210 already refused for the capability ranking. Gap recorded in the backlog for when a defensible and versioned counterfactual price exists.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSpendTab.tsx (comment "KPI de economia com modelos locais — CORTE DECLARADO", end of the file)

    RN-215 — The Problems tab is born with an honest empty state

    There is no integrated lint or test over the managed project's code; the bottom panel's Problems tab (handoff design_handoff_brabo) says so explicitly instead of showing an invented count (the handoff mock has a "3" badge). The same pattern already used by the Terminal (FASE 25b) and by the disabled "Testes" item of the rail.

    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBottomPanel.test.tsx — "Problemas diz honestamente que não há lint/teste integrado, sem contagem inventada"

    RN-216 — The Output tab is born with an honest empty state

    There is no build/deploy command stream in this tab — it would depend on the interactive terminal (FASE 25b), which does not exist. The tab explains this and reminds that git push/PR/deploy do not go out through the terminal in any case (RN-106).

    RN-217 — The Code tab status bar only shows real data

    The 24px status bar (CodeShell.tsx) shows ↑N ↓M commits of the current branch (via getCodeBranches, the same queryKey as CodeBranchPicker — dedup, zero extra requests, RN-090/091) and the language of the active file (linguagemPorCaminho). Cursor position and the error/test count of the handoff mock were left out: CodeEditor does not expose a trackable selection/caret and there is no integrated lint/test (the same decision as RN-215).

    RN-218 — Visible focus on the bottom panel's own tabs

    The tabs of CodeBottomPanel.tsx are a custom implementation (not the design system's Tabs) and did not inherit the calibrated :focus-visible that Tabs.module.css gained in Onda 2/frente C. Fixed with the same pattern as Input.module.css (ADR 0036), including forced-colors.

    RN-227 — The session status badge covers the 5 real states, not the handoff's 4

    The handoff asks for 4 badges (ativa/aguardando/fechada/abortada) for the 5 real states of the machine (created/active/closing/closed/closed_abnormally). closed_abnormally→abortada and created→aguardando are direct. closing is NOT merged with "fechada": it gets its own badge ("encerrando", tone accent, pulsing), because in closing the outcome (closed or closed_abnormally) is not yet known — calling it "fechada" would lie about that.

    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.test.tsx — describe "ProjectSessionsTab — selo de status (RN-227)"

    RN-228 — The pill filter groups the 2 states without their own pill by TRAJECTORY

    The handoff's pill filters (todas/ativas/fechadas/abortadas) only cover 4 of the 5 states. created (aguardando) goes into the "Ativas" pill — it has not reached anywhere yet, it is a "session in play". closing goes into the "Fechadas" pill — it is already on its way to closing without error. Each row's BADGE (RN-227) is never rewritten by the filter; the pill only groups.

    RN-229 — The Criativo tab's "cost of the month" KPI is the ACTOR's consumption, not the project total

    It reuses getMySpend(projectId, 30) — the SAME queryKey that ProjectSpendTab.tsx#MeuConsumo uses for the member's view (RN-101, ADR 0063), with no new aggregation. It NEVER shows the project total summing everyone (porProjeto in getWorkspaceSpendReport), because that data is owner-only and the Criativo tab is seen by any member of the project — showing the overall total would leak other people's spending to whoever RN-060/101 does not authorize to see it.

    RN-230 — The "ideation → commit rate" KPI is declared absent, never computed

    There is, nowhere in the product, a link between a creative session and the commit it produced. The Criativo tab shows "—" with the sentence "não medido: sessão não é vinculada a commit hoje" instead of inventing a calculation — the same class of error that ADR 0042 already refuses for a model score.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.tsx (CriativoKpis)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSessionsTab.test.tsx — "'Taxa ideação → commit' é DECLARADA ausente — nunca um número calculado"

    PROGRAM 28 — Wave 4, front E2: line virtualization and minimap in the Code tab (RN-239..242)

    RN-239 — Line virtualization: only the visible window becomes a DOM node

    CodeEditor.tsx renders only the lines within the visible window (plus OVERSCAN = 20 of margin) as [data-line-row]; the rest becomes two spacers that reserve the height without existing as a node. The decision NOT to use react-window/react-virtual: line height is FIXED (ALTURA_LINHA = 21, monospace), which reduces "which line is visible" to an integer division over scrollTop — the problem those libs really solve is VARIABLE height per item, which isn't the case for the Code tab.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.tsx (lines 38-96, 45, 48)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.test.tsx — describe "CodeEditor — virtualização", "caminho feliz: arquivo de 5.000 linhas renderiza uma janela pequena de nós, não o arquivo inteiro" (< 150 [data-line-row] nodes) and "rolar para o meio do arquivo troca a janela — linha 1 some, linhas do meio aparecem"

    RN-240 — Without a height measurement, it degrades to a generous default window, never renders everything

    When containerHeight hasn't been measured yet (1st render, or jsdom without ResizeObserver in tests), the window falls to LINHAS_SEM_MEDICAO = 40 — generous enough not to truncate the small files the tests use, small enough never to become "render everything" on a truly large file.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.tsx (line 57)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.test.tsx — "falha/borda: sem medição de altura (jsdom sem ResizeObserver, clientHeight 0), degrada para uma janela padrão em vez de não renderizar nada"

    RN-241 — The minimap reuses the SAME tokenization as virtualization — zero second pass over the file

    minimap.ts tokenizes nothing: it summarizes the output highlight.ts already produced (HighlightToken[][] from highlightFile, called only once) — the SAME one virtualization uses to color the visible lines. Tokenizing again just for the minimap would pay the cost of reading the file a second time, and that's why the minimap only comes in after virtualization is in place, never before.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/minimap.ts (lines 1-18, 39)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/minimap.test.ts

    RN-242 — The minimap is a CANVAS, a single DOM node, never one <div> per line

    The drawing is on <canvas> (desenharMinimapa), not one element per line: a minimap with one node per line would double again the count that virtualization (RN-239) just cut. With no 2D canvas context available (jsdom without the canvas package), the clickable overlay keeps working — only the drawing is skipped, never the interaction.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/minimap.ts (lines 14-16, 91)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeEditor.test.tsx — describe "CodeEditor — minimapa", "caminho feliz: clicar no minimapa rola o editor e troca a janela renderizada" and "falha: sem contexto de canvas 2D (jsdom sem o pacote canvas), o overlay continua clicável e nada quebra"

    PROGRAM 28 — Wave 4, front H4: the remaining ad-hoc collapses migrate to Disclosure (RN-249..251)

    RN-249 — The click target of a milestone with detail goes from 20px up to Disclosure's 24px floor

    The milestone WITH detail in AgentTimelineTree had min-height: 20px on the clickable header — below WCAG 2.2 AA 2.5.8 (Target Size, minimum 24px), which a previous comment already promised without delivering. The migration to the shared Disclosure fixes that for free: the generic component is already born at 24px, and there's no need to remember to set the ruler in each new consumer.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.module.css (lines 135-155, .marcoLinha)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.test.tsx
    • ADR: none — a one-off a11y fix, same pattern as ADR 0036

    RN-250 — The diff file strip in ApprovalCard does NOT migrate to Disclosure — its own animation is the reason

    The diff file strip in ApprovalCard rotates the chevron via transform: rotate(90deg) with its own transition (.chevron.open/ApprovalCard.module.css); the generic Disclosure SWAPS the icon (right arrow → down arrow) with no animation at all. Forcing the migration would erase the micro-interaction with no gain — exclusivity (only one file open at a time) already comes from outside (expandedFile), the same thing the controlled Disclosure would do. What was missing, and wasn't a peculiarity — it's the same defect Disclosure exists to close —, was aria-controls pointing to a named region: fixed directly, without changing component.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.tsx (lines 511-539)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.test.tsx

    RN-251 — Five call sites migrate to the shared Disclosure; the component gains testId as its only new hook

    ModelCatalogSection (the original reference that generated the component in PHASE 16), AgentTimelineTree (branch + milestone), code/CodeExplorer.tsx (tree folder), and code/CodeShell.tsx (bottom panel) now use the shared Disclosure — none of the six ad-hoc implementations that survived PHASE 16 is left out, except the declared exception of RN-250. testId is the only new prop a consumer needed (data- testid on the header, so existing tests keep selecting the right button without rewriting the whole suite). The controlled region exists in the DOM even when closed — only hidden and without the children mounted —, because aria-controls pointing to an id that doesn't resolve is worse than not having the attribute (a screen reader would announce control of something nonexistent); it's that same property that allows collapsing expensive lists (the OpenRouter catalog has 338 models) without mounting what nobody is looking at.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/ui/Disclosure.tsx (lines 33-71), apps/web/src/components/ModelCatalogSection.tsx, apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeExplorer.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeShell.tsx
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/ui/Disclosure.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeExplorer.test.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeShell.test.tsx

    PROGRAM 28 — Wave 5, front G3: the Chat RAG screen (RN-252..254, ADR 0082)

    RN-252 — The Chat RAG screen shows the two honest degradations the backend already declares, never hides them

    HybridSearchUseCase already returns vectorAvailable: false when the embedding provider didn't respond (RN-233) and GetRagCoverageUseCase already never includes an indexing timestamp (RN-237) — but an honest contract in the backend doesn't guarantee an honest screen if it decides not to read both fields. ProjectRagTab shows a warning above the results when vectorAvailable is false (with vectorUnavailableReason, when it exists), and RagCoveragePanel only renders a REAL count (filesIndexed/ filesInRepo, sessionsIndexed/sessionsInProject) — no text of the kind "reindexed Xmin ago" is born on this screen, because the response doesn't carry that data and inventing it would lie (same ruler as ADR 0042 for a model's grade).

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectRagTab.tsx (lines 27-31, 176-182), apps/web/src/components/rag/RagCoveragePanel.tsx (lines 27-34)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectRagTab.test.tsx — "CASO DE FALHA (degradação honesta): vectorAvailable false avisa..."; apps/web/src/components/rag/RagCoveragePanel.test.tsx — "CASO DE FALHA (degradação honesta): nunca escreve..."
    • ADR: 0082

    RN-253 — A citation with session origin navigates to the exact EVENT, reusing the Psicólogo's mechanism

    RagCitationCard doesn't invent a second navigation path: origin { kind: 'session', sessionId, eventId } calls useNavigate to /projects/:projectId/sessions/:sessionId with search: { highlightEvent: eventId } — the SAME route and the SAME parameter that the Psicólogo's evidence chips already use (HypothesisCard.tsx, Phase 4b) to scroll the thread to the event and highlight it. Origin { kind: 'file' } shows path and headingPath as text, without a link: the Code tab today has no deep-link by path, and building that navigation is outside the scope of this front.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/rag/RagCitationCard.tsx (lines 32-38, 49-56)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/rag/RagCitationCard.test.tsx — "caminho feliz: origem de sessão navega até o evento exato ao clicar"
    • ADR: 0082

    RN-254 — The reindex button is maintainer/owner on the SCREEN, mirroring the route's ruler

    POST .../rag/reindex already requires role:maintainer (RN-238) — the api guarantees it. ProjectRagTab mirrors the ruler on the CLIENT with the same pattern that ProjectSettingsTab/ProjectApprovalsTab already use for other maintainer gates (useCurrentWorkspaceWithRole, since there's no PROJECT role on the client today, only the workspace one the listing returns): the "Reindex now" button doesn't even appear for whoever isn't owner/maintainer, instead of appearing disabled — reindexing triggers N calls to the project's repository and to the embedding provider (same "changes what the product spends without asking" ruler as the parallelism ceiling, RN-083), and a visible but always-refused button would only teach people to ignore the 403.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectRagTab.tsx (lines 43-44, 109)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectRagTab.test.tsx — "botão de reindexar só aparece para maintainer/owner..."; "maintainer vê e pode disparar a reindexação"
    • ADR: 0082

    RN-268 — The Terminal tab shows the REAL state of the container, never pretends a terminal that doesn't exist

    The original plan for this front was the full interactive terminal, but the investigation confirmed that PHASE 25b remains cut: no service mounts /var/run/docker.sock, and even after ADR 0081 (Wave 4) nothing transitions project_containers in production. Implementing a terminal that pretends to execute commands — or that runs in the same container as Brabo's monorepo, the debt ADR 0055 already describes as policy and not isolation — would be inventing capability, the same mistake ADRs 0041/0042 already refuse for LLM provider and catalog model.

    What the tab GAINS is honest: under the explanatory text that already existed (PHASE 26b), CodeBottomPanel fetches GET .../container/lifecycle (RN-267) only while the Terminal tab is open (enabled: aba === 'terminal' — no background polling, the same traffic discipline as RN-107) and shows the status with a Badge (provisioning/running/stopped/failed/ removed, in pt-BR), how long ago, and the failureReason when the state is failed. A project never provisioned — the common case today — shows the sentence "not provisioned yet", never an invented badge.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBottomPanel.tsx, apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBottomPanel.module.css, apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts (getContainerLifecycle), apps/web/src/lib/api-types.ts (CicloDeVidaDoContainer)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeBottomPanel.test.tsx (describe "Terminal — o estado REAL do ciclo de vida do container")
    • Edge case: the interactive terminal itself is NOT born here — it's PHASE 25b, which remains cut and depends on the container's physical wall (the agent's worktree living inside it), not on this read route.
    • ADR: 0083

    UX Designer — the fifth conversational agent (RN-285..287, ADR 0087)

    RN-287 — uxDesignerActive in the roster, in BOTH sources (RN-090)

    Same criterion as infraActive: an accepted handoff addressed to "ux-designer" in the session. apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts (rosterFactsFromEvents/rosterFromFacts) gained the fact and pushes it conditionally into the roster as a SOLO agent — with no corresponding pushAreaMembers, because it isn't an area.

    RN-090 requires BOTH sources in sync (team panel via event log; the dashboard card via ProjectCardSummary.roster, computed in the api) so that a new agent doesn't appear in one place and go missing in the other. The fact went into both: apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/projects-summary.repository.ts widens the SAME infraActive query (an inArray(handoffs.toAgent, ['infra', 'ux-designer']) in place of the single eq, split into two Sets by toAgent) — no new query, the count of twelve constant queries that projects-summary.repository.spec.ts proves doesn't grow.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/ux_designer_server.ex, ux_designer_tools.ex, ux_designer_supervisor.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/agents.ex (identity), artifact_schemas.ex (type prototipo_navegavel); apps/engine/lib/engine/application.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex; apps/web/src/lib/agents.ts, agent-status.ts; apps/api/src/application/ports/projects-summary-repository.port.ts, apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/projects-summary.repository.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/ux_designer_server_test.exs, ux_designer_tools_test.exs; apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.test.ts; apps/api/test/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/projects-summary.repository.spec.ts (describe with both toAgents in the same session)
    • ADR: 0087

    Wave 1 of ADR 0104 — execution_mode in three values, workspace verified by the runner

    RN-428 — The story promotion carousel survives the event window

    promocoesPendentes/the PO's promotion carousel (RN-148) no longer depends on a scan over the window of the last 200 events of useSessionEvents. The source of content/count is useBacklog (Story.proposedReady, per session, complete and without a window) — in a long session, backlog.story_promotion_proposed leaves the window while the story is still really pending, and before this the carousel shrank (or vanished) silently. The same class of bug that RN-180 already fixed for ContextAside.

    When the story isn't in the loaded backlog (query hasn't answered yet), it degrades story by story to the usual window scan. The anchoring in the timeline still comes from the event within the window when it exists; if the event that opened the batch has already left the window, the carousel anchors at the TOP of the visible stretch instead of vanishing — never hiding a real state because of a read cutoff.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (useMemo timeline)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.carrossel-janela-estourada.test.tsx
    • ADR: none — a bug fix, the same class already fixed by RN-180
    • Origin: found by real use ("the chat failed to show the PO's carousel")

    RN-431 — The Architecture tab badge counts pending validation items, never "diagram not generated"

    contagens.arquiteturaPendente (tab ruler) comes from architecture.pendencies.length (story↔module cross-validation divergence), not from c4Diagram.status === 'sem_diagrama'. The other three counts of the ruler (promocoesPendentes, aprovacoesPendentes, hipotesesPendentes) all mean "something is waiting for YOUR decision" — generating the diagram is the Arquiteto's work, not the user's decision queue, and badging for it would be noise.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPage.tsx
    • Test: ProjectPage.test.tsx
    • ADR: none
    • Origin: design decision made when extracting the Architecture tab from the Overview

    RN-432 — The language preference comes in the login/refresh payload, never in an extra call

    Revised by RN-891: the column also accepts ru and zh-Hans, and the web's default went from en to pt-BR (ADR 0216).

    Foundation of the interface's i18n (Wave 6a of a larger program — the mass extraction of the rest of the app's strings is a separate stage, in parallel). A locale column in users ('pt-BR' | 'en', default 'pt-BR' — never silently flips whoever already has an account). EmitirSessaoUseCase is the ONLY point that issues a session (login, refresh, social login) — locale embedded in the body of /auth/login//auth/refresh from it, with no additional round-trip. GET/PATCH /users/me/preferences exists as a REDUNDANT path, for AccountPage to reassert the value without waiting for the next refresh.

    en is the app's DEFAULT language from now on (react-i18next, a new dependency — the same "a new dependency needs a justification" ruler that mermaid/@xterm/xterm already followed); pt-BR remains available. apps/web/src/lib/idioma.ts (same design as tema.ts): the SERVER is the source of truth, localStorage['brabo.locale'] is only a cache to avoid a flash of the wrong language on the first paint. A user without an account yet uses navigator.language only as a DISPLAY suggestion, never persisting anything before an account exists.

    • Where: apps/api/src/application/use-cases/auth/emitir-sessao.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/iam/user-preferences.controller.ts, apps/web/src/lib/idioma.ts, apps/web/src/lib/i18n.ts, apps/web/src/routes/AccountPage.tsx
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/idioma.test.ts, apps/web/src/routes/AccountPage.test.tsx, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/auth/*.spec.ts, apps/api/test/interfaces/route-surface.spec.ts
    • ADR: none — an additive extension of an already existing choke point, without changing the session format for anyone who doesn't use the new field
    • Origin: product owner's request — interface and docs in English by default, with Portuguese preserved

    The Runner's folder explorer becomes three columns, and creating a runner project no longer waits for the final step (RN-436/436)

    Two changes requested by the product owner after testing the creation of a real project: (1) the existing folder browser (FolderBrowserModal) was a simple list with a breadcrumb, when the visual reference he sent (a GNOME Files/GTK-style picker, with three columns) already existed as a ready design handoff to adapt; (2) "Browse folder..." didn't work on the project's CREATION SCREEN — only after it was created — because the Runner channel's ticket (ADR 0107) is anchored to a projectId that, until this fix, was only born on confirmation.

    RN-436 — FolderBrowserModal becomes a three-column explorer: shortcuts, a list where one click selects/double click enters, and a details panel

    The single-list layout became three columns inside the Modal size="full": shortcuts (Home folder, which calls diretorioInicial(); Root, /), the central list with breadcrumb, and a details panel on the right. The protocol (FsEntrada, in apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts) only has { nome, isDir } — no size nor modification date — and wasn't extended (it would touch apps/engine/apps/runner, outside the scope of this delivery); the details panel only shows what can be derived client-side: name, type, and the item count when the displayed item is the ALREADY OPEN folder (never of a merely selected folder, which would require an extra call to the runner).

    The TWO gestures that were previously fused into a single click (which already navigated into the folder) were separated: one click now only SELECTS (highlights the item and updates the details panel) and a double click ENTERS. The final button ("Use this folder", renamed from "Select this folder" — the folderBrowserModal.select key of pt-BR/en changed along with it) uses the selected item when there is one (selected.kind === 'dir' ? join(path, selected.name) : path) or the currently open folder when there's none. The list stopped filtering files — the whole listing appears now, with files rendered as <div aria-disabled> instead of <button>: visually dimmed and with no gesture at all, because only folders remain navigable/selectable.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.tsx, FolderBrowserModal.module.css, apps/web/src/locales/{pt-BR,en}/terminal.json
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.test.tsx — a single click selects without calling listarDiretorio again; a double click navigates; a file appears in the list but isn't reachable by getByRole('button'); "Home folder"/"Root" shortcuts; and "Use this folder" uses the selected one when there is one, otherwise the open folder
    • ADR: none — a UI redesign over a component that already existed, with no protocol change nor data boundary change (ADR 0107 still describes the mechanism behind it)
    • Origin: product owner's request, after testing the creation of a real project and finding the folder browser not much like a real file explorer — a visual reference sent by him (three-column picker, GNOME Files/GTK style)

    RN-437 — In runner mode, "Browse folder..." creates the project IN ADVANCE, and the final confirmation reuses it by identity SNAPSHOT

    ADR 0107 had already declared this gap in its own Consequences section: with no project yet, the channel's ticket (RN-429) has nothing to anchor to, and "Browse folder..." always fell into the "available after the project exists" state — even in runner mode, where creation does NOT depend on validating disk (ADR 0104, item 2). Closed by ADR 0108: NewProjectWizard.tsx (handleProcurarPasta) calls createProject when clicking "Browse folder..." when modoDeWorkspace === 'runner' — using the already typed path, or a lexically valid and clearly provisional placeholder (/workspace-a-confirmar) when the field is still empty, never blocking the click. mounted mode doesn't change: it keeps projectId={null} until confirmation, because there disk validation runs on creation and creating early with an empty path would return RN-422's refusal at a moment when the user hasn't even thought about a path yet.

    An identity SNAPSHOT ({ name, externalId, adotando } — never caminhoLocal, whose refinement is the PURPOSE of browsing) decides when to reuse: clicking "Browse folder..." again with the identity intact reopens the modal with the SAME project, without creating another; changing the name (or going back and swapping the repository to adopt) invalidates the snapshot, and the next browse creates a new project. handleConfirm applies the same ruler — it reuses the project already created when browsing when the snapshot still matches, and only calls createProject when there's nothing to reuse. montarPayloadDeCriacao, a pure function extracted from what used to live only inside handleConfirm, is reused by both paths.

    The button's prerequisite is stated BEFORE the click (AT-214): in this mode the list comes from the local agent, so without a brabo-runner running and connected to the newly created project the modal can only show the wait and the onboarding. The screen now says so in TEXT below the field (aviso-procurar-runner, ADR 0064 — never a tooltip), with the command brabo-runner --project <id> --dir <pasta> and the alternative that depends on nothing: typing the path in the field, which the runner confirms on connecting. The mechanism (the early creation, the transport through the local agent) didn't change.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.tsx (handleProcurarPasta, handleConfirm, montarPayloadDeCriacao, snapshotDeIdentidade/mesmaIdentidade)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.test.tsx, describe navegação de pasta antecipada no modo Runner — creates the project and opens the modal with the real id; uses the placeholder when the field is empty; clicking again without changing anything doesn't create another; changing the name invalidates the snapshot and creates a different project; handleConfirm reuses instead of duplicating. The preexisting test "'Procurar pasta...' mostra o estado declarado" (mounted mode) stays green, unchanged — it proves the scope didn't leak
    • ADR: 0108 — a structural change (WHEN a project comes to exist in the creation flow), with the accepted side effect of an orphan "not provisioned" project when the wizard is closed without finishing (the SAME state that any interrupted creation already produces today, not a new regression)
    • Origin: product owner's request, after testing the creation of a real project in Runner mode and not being able to browse folders before confirming — the gap ADR 0107 had already declared and postponed

    RN-438 — fs-browser-channel.ts had the SAME duplicated-path bug that RN-433 had already fixed in its sibling terminal-channel.ts

    Found while verifying RN-437 end to end in Chrome, against the real engine: the modal opened with the right projectId, but the connection dropped immediately with "The connection to the runner dropped — close and reopen to try again." instead of showing RunnerOnboardingPanel. apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts concatenated '/runner/websocket' to an engineWsUrl the api already returns READY (ws://host:porta/runner, from engineWsUrlPublico()) — the same phoenix.js Socket also appends /websocket on its own in the constructor, and the result hit the engine as GET /runner/runner/websocket/websocket, which Phoenix.Router.NoRouteError refuses. It's EXACTLY item 3 of RN-433, only in the SIBLING module — the fix there had never been replicated here, and no suite caught it because this module had no test at all until now (its own docblock said, incorrectly, that terminal-channel.test.ts covered it "indirectly"). Fix: the same line as RN-433 — engineWsUrl.replace(/^http/, 'ws'), without concatenating any path.

    Verified END TO END against a real runner project (owner logged in, teste-navegacao, created by RN-437): before the fix, the modal fell straight into the connection error; after, it shows RunnerOnboardingPanel with the command brabo-runner --project <id-real> --dir <pasta> in under 2s — the expected result without a real runner connected.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.test.ts (new — the module had no test of its own at all until this fix). The happy path confirms socket.url === engineWsUrl without concatenation; plus diretorioInicial/listarDiretorio roundtrip, failure fetching the ticket, and idempotent fechar()
    • ADR: none — it restores the behavior that ADR 0103/0107 and RN-433 already declared as intent, only in a module the previous fix didn't reach
    • Origin: found manually testing RN-437 (early creation of a runner project) against Chrome and the real engine — the first time FolderBrowserModal was exercised end to end against a real connection, because before this delivery "Browse folder..." never reached a real project before confirmation

    An attached local folder becomes the fourth scope of the RAG index, read by the BROWSER (RN-455..457, ADR 0113)

    RN-456 — The aggregate ceiling REJECTS (400) the whole upload; a large/binary individual file is only SKIPPED

    Unlike docs/adr (a background sweep, with nobody watching), attaching a folder is a SINGLE gesture with a folder picker on the screen. IndexLocalFolderUseCase refuses (400) the whole batch when the number of files (RAG_LOCAL_FILE_COUNT_LIMIT, 500) or the summed bytes (RAG_LOCAL_TOTAL_BYTES_LIMIT, 8 MiB) overflow — it never truncates silently, because whoever clicked "Attach" can choose a smaller folder. An individual file that's too large (RAG_LOCAL_FILE_BYTES_LIMIT, 512 KiB) or with an unrecognized extension (RAG_LOCAL_ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS, allowlist) is only SKIPPED (filesSkipped), it never takes down the batch — the same distinction that IndexProjectDocsUseCase already makes implicitly by filtering only .md. A path with .. or a leading slash is REFUSED (400), never accepted silently, the same discipline of never trusting a path coming from the client (RN-092/095) even when it "shouldn't" contain that. apps/web/src/lib/rag-local-limits.ts mirrors the same numbers on the client, only as a UX convenience (summary before sending) — what really guarantees it is the server.

    • Where: apps/api/src/domain/rag/rag-search-limits.ts (RAG_LOCAL_*), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/rag/index-local-folder.use-case.ts
    • Test: apps/api/test/application/use-cases/rag/index-local-folder.use-case.spec.ts (count and summed-bytes ceilings reject; a large/binary file is skipped; a path with ../leading slash is refused)
    • ADR: 0113
    • Origin: product owner's request

    RN-460 — The turn activity strip narrates in real time, and the thread only receives the response at the end

    The Session screen shows, ABOVE the composer, a strip that narrates in human language what a conversational agent (Criativo, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer, Staff) is doing DURING the turn — visual reference: Claude Code's status line. The thread only receives the response bubble AFTER the turn ends; the rule belongs to the CHAT (Phoenix channel) and applies only to the six conversational agents — the consultative chat without an active agent (SSE, streamChatMessage) keeps the usual streaming bubble, untouched. (Since RN-682 the screen no longer calls that SSE: a consultative session with no agent asks for an agent before sending.)

    Mechanism (engine): the six servers already emit a DURABLE tool.call in the event log; they now also do broadcast(state, "tool.call", %{tool: name, agent: @agent}) — EPHEMERAL, without args (never a raw payload, the same ruler as RN-096/RN-412) — right after, for the session:<id> channel to deliver in real time. There's no shared behaviour/macro among the six dispatch_tools (four distinct structural shapes confirmed by a complete reading): the change is six edits adapted to each one's local shape.

    Mechanism (web): a PURE reducer (reduzirAtividadeDoTurno/lib/atividade-do-turno.ts) accumulates the agent.delta text as "current"; when a tool.call arrives from the channel, it archives the non-empty current as a NARRATION line, resets, and ALWAYS adds a TOOL line with fraseDaFerramenta(tool) (a dictionary of the 19 tools of the six agents, lib/narracao-de-ferramentas.ts, with no RN of its own — it only exists to serve this rule) — two consecutive calls with no delta between them become two separate lines, never one. End of turn resets (finalizarTurnoDoAgente, the ONLY reset point). turnoViaCanal is an OWN flag (never derived from statusAgent/streamingAgent, which go through legitimately null windows in the middle of a turn) that decides whether the strip appears OR the old bubble — never both.
