    Presentation rule (history): agruparNarracoesDoTurno, a new pure pass that runs AFTER afundarDesfechos (RN-172) in the same useMemo — within the same turno+autor, consecutive agent.responses become a compact Disclosure ("Turn steps · N"), leaving only the last one intact and outside it. turno === 0 (the sentinel prologue of turnoDoSeq) is left OUT of the grouping, on purpose — it isn't a real turn, and old fixtures stack agent.response without a turn boundary to test another mechanism (RN-138/RN-177). An agent-agnostic function (it only reads turno/autor), afundarDesfechos itself doesn't change.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/{po,criativo,arquiteto, dev_lead,ux_designer,staff}_server.ex (tool.call broadcast); apps/web/src/lib/atividade-do-turno.ts (reducer); apps/web/src/components/TurnActivityStrip.tsx (component); apps/web/src/lib/session-channel.ts:54 (onToolCall); apps/web/src/routes/session-fio.tsx:48 (agruparNarracoesDoTurno), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:197 (turnoViaCanal)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/atividade-do-turno.test.ts, apps/web/src/components/TurnActivityStrip.test.tsx, apps/web/src/lib/session-channel.test.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.ordenacao-e-avisos.test.tsx (agruparNarracoesDoTurno), suite of apps/engine/test/engine/agents/ *_server_test.exs (tool.call broadcast without raw args)
    • Origin: product owner's request

    "Needs you" panel (RN-467)

    RN-467 — The panel of the five decision queues NEVER sums and NEVER executes: it separates by queue and forwards to the decision

    The "Needs you" chip, at the top of the project, opens a panel with the project's FIVE decision queues — action approvals, PR merges, story promotions, architecture pending items, and Psicólogo hypotheses. Two guarantees define it, and neither of them is aesthetic:

    It never sums. Each queue has its OWN header and count, and there's no project total anywhere: neither in the panel, nor in the chip that opens it — the chip announces PRESENCE (a dot, temAlgoEsperando, boolean by signature) and never quantity. It's the same product decision that already keeps the five rail counters separate (ADR 0126): summing erases WHICH queue is asking for attention, which is precisely the question of whoever opens the panel. montarFilas returns the five in the declared order of urgency (ORDEM_DAS_FILAS) and doesn't export a total function — the absence is the mechanism, not an oversight.

    The only dedupe that exists is by IDENTITY, never between queues: the SAME git_merge proposed_action arrives through both hooks (usePendingActions is the session's and doesn't filter by type; useProjectPendingActions is project-wide) and would appear twice in the same list, under two titles. It stays in the most specific group (prs).

    It never executes. The two actionable queues in the panel (aprovacoes, prs) render the SAME ApprovalCard as the Approvals tab (a single variant since AT-322, with the detail collapsed), and the buttons call the same decision endpoints — the panel is a SHORTCUT to the decision, never a substitute for it. That matters especially for git_merge: a merge into a protected branch is downgraded to require_approval UNCONDITIONALLY (apps/api/src/domain/actions/decide.ts), a ceiling that neither agent_autonomy nor permissions.json lift, and nothing in the panel touches that path. onActivateAutoMode is OMITTED on both cards on purpose: turning on "auto mode" (RN-153) is changing the agent's POLICY, not deciding the action that's in front of you — whoever wants that decides in the Approvals tab, where the workspace role is already checked. The other three queues have no decision card outside their context and therefore LEAD to the tab where the decision lives.

    The architecture pending item has no date of its own — none, in any field (ArchitecturePendency): it's a view DERIVED from crossing story and module_map, recomputed on every read and never recorded. The panel BORROWS the related story's date and SAYS it borrowed it ("story updated 18 min ago", dataEmprestada); without the story in the loaded backlog, it shows "no date" and sorts the item at the END of the queue. In no case an invented instant: rendering "now" would make the most urgent line of the screen the one least known about, with nothing exposing the lie. Adding a column and a migration in the api would solve it for real and is a separate decision, outside this one's scope.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/precisa-de-voce.ts:108 (ORDEM_DAS_FILAS), :117 (FILAS_ACIONAVEIS), :139 (montarFilas — the dedupe by identity and the date borrowing), :245 (temAlgoEsperando); apps/web/src/components/PainelPrecisaDeVoce.tsx:246-248 (role="dialog", aria-modal, label) and :279-284 (group per queue, with its count); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPage.tsx:120 (builds the queues from the five hooks the rail counters already use)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/precisa-de-voce.test.ts (the five queues separated with each one's items; nothing summed and no total function; the duplicated git_merge appears once, in the PRs queue; order by longest wait; borrowed date marked, absent becomes null and goes to the end), apps/web/src/components/PainelPrecisaDeVoce.test.tsx (five headers with each queue's count; the total appears nowhere; the empty state's own sentence; ApprovalCard decides by the action's OWN session; "Automatic mode" isn't offered; an architecture line without a date renders without breaking; Esc, click-outside, focus, and aria-expanded)
    • Origin: design review by the product owner (item #3 of the UI improvements canvas)

    Environment state on screen (RN-468)

    RN-468 — An environment signal says what it KNOWS: workspaceVerifiedAt is a record of confirmation, never a guarantee that the runner is alive

    The product shows environment state in two places, and the slice of each is the rule: the login screen shows only what's true WITHOUT identity, and the project Overview shows what's only true WITH it.

    Pre-login, the slice is imposed by the data's scope, not by aesthetic choice. What remains are the two /health endpoints — public in both services on purpose (@Public() in the api; "Sem auth de propósito" in router.ex), the same ones StatusPage already consumes without a session. Runner presence is keyed by {user_id, project_id} (runner_device_keys) and the model list is projects/:projectId/models with role viewer: before login neither subject exists, so the screen declares the absence ("Runner and local models depend on your account and a project") instead of omitting it — omitting it would make the platform look like it doesn't have what it has. The two /health keep the THREE states separate (RN-088 applied to an environment signal): checking… is not no response, and neither of them is responding. The probe has a CEILING (6s): an api that accepts the connection and never answers would leave the line at "checking…" forever, which is honest for half a second and a lie by omission after ten.

    And the form never waits for the probe. The state is local to the block, which is a SIBLING of the card — an api that's down changes a text and doesn't delay or hide a pixel of the login. That matters exactly at the moment the api goes down, which is when someone most needs the screen to at least OPEN.

    Post-login, the rule is about what the data does NOT assert. The runner signal comes from projects.workspace_verified_at — the stamp ConfirmProjectWorkspaceUseCase writes when a runner connects and confirms the folder (RN-423), and the SAME field the engine uses as a gate (TerminalExecutor refuses a command in a runner project with the field null). It is not a heartbeat, for two independent reasons: there's no process being observed, and reconnecting reporting the same path doesn't rewrite the stamp (an explicit decision of the use case), so that not even the date is "the last time the runner showed up". The screen says "folder confirmed on <date>" with the caveat of what that isn't, and never "up", "online", nor a green dot — the tone is neutro, and green there would read as a liveness guarantee the data doesn't support. What knows about NOW is the terminal's socket, in the Code tab (TerminalPanel/RunnerOnboardingPanel), and the caveat points there. The same discipline RN-467 used when borrowing the story's updatedAt.

    The runner line only exists in a project in runner mode: in the other two the field is null by definition (the mode conversion resets it, RN-450) and a "never confirmed" line there would be an invented absence.

    • Where: apps/web/src/components/SinaisDoAmbiente.tsx:72 (useSaude — the probe with a ceiling and the .catch of the connection rejection), :133 (SinaisDoAmbiente, the pre-login block); apps/web/src/components/AmbienteDoProjeto.tsx:92 (AmbienteDoProjeto), :102 (runner line, only in runner mode, with neutro tone and caveat); apps/web/src/routes/AuthLayout.tsx:46 (colunaDeIdentidade — nothing focusable there, or the first Tab stop stops being the e-mail field); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/iam/confirm-project-workspace.use-case.ts:93 (the stamp is NOT rewritten when the path doesn't change — the reason the date isn't recency)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/SinaisDoAmbiente.test.tsx (the three distinct states; a connection rejection becomes "no response" and not silence; a non-OK response likewise; ceiling of a hanging probe; no focusable element; the absence of runner/models is declared), apps/web/src/components/AmbienteDoProjeto.test.tsx (no runner line outside runner mode; confirmed requires the caveat and the block doesn't contain de pé|online|conectado agora; never confirmed teaches the path; count of local models in singular, plural, and zero; query keys reused), apps/web/src/routes/LoginPage.ambiente.test.tsx (the form renders and submits with the probe rejecting AND with the probe hanging; a single <h1>; the version still has one source)
    • Origin: design review by the product owner (item #6 of the UI improvements canvas)

    RN-469 — Saving a section is N calls, and the screen never asserts an outcome it didn't get

    In the Settings sections whose rows are a SCALAR setting per key (Parallelism per area and Spend ceiling per area, both per agent_areas), the save button is ONE, the section's, and not one per row. Saving triggers one call per dirty row — PUT .../areas/:key/max-parallel and .../budget are per-AREA endpoints, there's no transactional endpoint for "write these N ceilings", and this product didn't invent one. The consequence is that the outcome of a click can be partial, and the rule is about what the screen can say then.

    The calls are in SERIES, in the screen's order, and one failure doesn't interrupt the following ones. In series because the failure report names rows and needs to come out in the order the person sees them. Without interrupting because whoever clicked asked for the N: aborting on the first refusal would leave rows with no attempt at all, and the screen wouldn't be able to distinguish "the api refused" from "it didn't even try".

    The outcome is per ROW, and that's what the UI shows. Only the draft the api CONFIRMED is discarded; what failed stays in the field, with what the person typed, and the section stays marked as unsaved by exactly those rows — clicking Save again tries only them. The three outcomes are distinct and none disguises itself as another: all passed → success; none passed → the message the API gave, never a count; some passed → a warning saying how many out of how many and naming the ones left. "Saved" and "not saved" would both be a lie in the third case, and it's the one this RN exists to protect.

    The section declares HOW MANY rows are pending before the click. One button per section says "Save" the same with one dirty row and with five; the count ("2 unsaved changes in this section") is the counterpart of having swapped N buttons for one. Dirty is a comparison by interpreted value, not by text: 20 and 20.0 are the same ceiling, and comparing strings would send a call the api treats as a no-op. An invalid draft counts as dirty and replaces the count with the message explaining the block — two numbers about the same set would be noise, and whoever has an invalid value needs what locks the button.

    Where the rule does NOT apply, and why. A section whose control is a choice of a NAMED value (Story promotion, Models per agent, Model per area, role in Members) saves on onChange, with no button, and stays that way: the confirmation exists for a TYPED field, where saving on each key press would send 1 on the way to 12. What that sentence measures is whose value it is: it applies to the control that writes its OWN value, and doesn't reach a selector whose value is the ARGUMENT of an action over other rows — see RN-476, which is the exception and says why. And Credentials keeps a button per row despite also having drafts per key: the credential is write-only (ADR 0050) and never comes back from the server, so there's no value to compare against to decide "dirty"; the row's button alternates between "Save" and "Replace" depending on whether that provider already has a key; and it shares the card with "Test" and "Remove", which are irreducibly the row's.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/settings/secao-salvavel.tsx:105 (useSecaoSalvavel — the serial loop, the per-row outcome, and the three toasts), :130 (dirty by interpreted value), :241 (MarcaDeNaoSalvo — the count, and the invalid one replacing it), apps/web/src/routes/settings/ParallelismSection.tsx:31, apps/web/src/routes/settings/BudgetSection.tsx:37 (the two consumers), apps/web/src/routes/settings/CredentialsSection.tsx:57 (why this section was left out)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/secao-salvavel.test.tsx (the count of dirty rows; going back to the server's value clears the mark; invalid replaces the count and locks the button; one click persists all the dirty rows; a number and null in the same batch; a partial failure says how many out of how many and names the one left; the section stays marked by those that failed; the second click tries only them; one failure doesn't interrupt the following ones; none passing shows the API's message and not the count)
    • Origin: design review by the product owner (item #7 of the UI improvements canvas — "save per section instead of per row")

    RN-470 — A origem do modelo distingue binding próprio de herança do Criativo, e nenhum vazio da cascata usa o mesmo símbolo de outro

    origin: 'agent' na resposta da api significa duas coisas diferentes, e a api está certa em não separá-las: o agente tem uma linha de escopo agent em model_bindings, ou a cascata pousou em workspace e herdarModeloDeStart trocou o valor pelo modelo do Criativo — quem lê "de onde veio" precisa ver que veio de um AGENTE, não de um escopo que não existe no banco. Quem tem de separá-las é a tela, e até aqui ela não separava: a coluna Origem imprimia o enum cru, e num projeto com 3 linhas de agent no banco os 12 agentes mostravam a mesma palavra agent.

    A origem é uma CADEIA, não uma palavra. workspace › projeto › área › agente com quatro estados por nó, e os quatro existem porque dizem coisas diferentes: vigente (é daqui que sai o modelo em uso — o único nó em Badge), definido (este nível tem valor próprio, mas um mais específico venceu), vazio (nenhum valor aqui) e pulado (tinha valor e a cascata o descartou por unavailable/sem_tool_calling, RN-043). Colapsar definido em vazio devolveria o problema do enum cru um nível abaixo.

    Na herança, o nó agente fica VAZIO e um nó extra nomeia o Criativo. O nível que a cascata alcançou (workspace) aparece como definido e não como vigente: o modelo do workspace não é o que vale, e marcá-lo vigente afirmaria o modelo errado. O nó do Criativo é o único que não é escopo do banco, de propósito — ele nomeia o passo pós-cascata. A mesma cadeia vale para a seção de ÁREA, onde origin: 'agent' só pode ser esse passo: a consulta de área não põe escopo de agente na cascata.

    A derivação é do CLIENTE, e o que ela não consegue provar continua acionável. Nenhum endpoint novo: a tela já busca os quatro níveis. agent é herança quando nenhum nível acima do workspace tinha valor UTILIZÁVEL (o que foi pulado não segura a descida) e o modelo é o mesmo do Criativo resolvido. Sobra um caso indistinguível: agente com linha própria apontando para o mesmo modelo do Criativo, sem padrão de área nem de projeto. Por isso "voltar a herdar" continua aparecendo em toda origem agent — a ação segue disponível justamente no caso que a cadeia não prova, e nele ela ainda muda o futuro (sem a linha, o agente passa a acompanhar o Criativo).

    A outra metade dessa escolha: o 404 tem desfecho próprio. Manter o botão em toda origem agent significa oferecê-lo TAMBÉM onde não há linha para apagar — e ali ClearModelBindingUseCase responde 404, corretamente ("apaguei o que não existia" e "apaguei" não são a mesma resposta; colapsá-las esconderia um agentSlug errado). Para quem clicou, porém, esse 404 não é falha: o estado pedido — o agente herda — já é verdade, e chamá-lo de erro seria a tela contradizendo o que ela sabe. Então ele não passa por mensagemDaApi como as demais recusas, por dois motivos: a frase da api é pt-BR cravada no código e o idioma default do web é en, e este endpoint tem uma causa de 404 (papel insuficiente é 403 no RolesGuard, scope_id malformado não é 404) — o cliente SABE o que este 404 significa e pode dizer na língua de quem lê. Qualquer outro status continua sendo falha de verdade, com a frase da api e tom danger. Nos dois desfechos a linha é relida: se a api diz que não havia binding, quem está velha é a tela.

    E o desfecho próprio é do ENDPOINT, não da tela. A outra ação da mesma linha — trocar o modelo pelo ModelPicker da coluna MODELO VIGENTE — recusa por sete caminhos, e nenhum status identifica um deles sozinho (SetModelBindingUseCase): 400 para scope_id malformado ou modelId reprovado no DTO, 403 para papel abaixo de developer, 404 para duas causas indistinguíveis ("Modelo não encontrado" e "Projeto não encontrado") e 422 para três (RN-040 sem tool calling; RN-043 desativado no workspace ou sumido do provider). Ali o cliente NÃO pode nomear o 404 — escolher uma das duas frases seria a tela afirmando o que não sabe —, então toda recusa segue a gramática normal, com a frase da api e tom danger. As duas funções são vizinhas e tratam o mesmo status de formas opostas de propósito: o que autoriza o desfecho próprio é a causa ÚNICA, não a tela.

    Na recusa, a coluna MODELO VIGENTE continua exibindo o binding confirmado. O ModelPicker não guarda a escolha em estado local (selected sai do prop selectedModelId, que vem da query), então uma recusa não deixa na tela um modelo que a api se recusou a gravar — e a linha só é relida no sucesso, já que na recusa nada mudou no banco. É a mesma disciplina desta RN aplicada ao tempo: a tela não afirma um estado que não existe, nem por traço, nem por enum, nem por otimismo.

    Vazio tem texto próprio, e vazios diferentes têm textos diferentes. Os três — da tela diziam três coisas com o mesmo símbolo e agora dizem cada uma a sua: sem modelo em nenhum nível (nenhum nível tem binding para o agente), sem gasto ainda (o agente não registrou consumo — diferente de US$ 0,00, que é ter rodado de graça) e sem padrão em nenhum nível (a área e os dois níveis acima dela estão vazios). Mesma disciplina da RN-180: a tela não afirma com um traço o que não consegue nomear.

    • Onde: apps/web/src/routes/settings/cascata.tsx:119 (montarCadeia — os quatro estados e o nó do Criativo), :178 (herdouDoCriativo — a dedução e seu limite), :287 (CadeiaDeCascata), apps/web/src/routes/settings/ModelsSection.tsx:149 (cadeiaDoAgente), :329 (handleModelChange — por que aqui o 404 NÃO tem desfecho próprio, e por que a linha só relê no sucesso), :385 (handleClearAgentBinding — os três desfechos, e por que o 404 tem o dele), :359 (coluna Origem), :429 (não há nível abaixo), :448 (sem gasto ainda), apps/web/src/components/ModelPicker.tsx:95 (selected sai do prop — o picker não guarda a escolha, e é por isso que a recusa não deixa valor fantasma na tela), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/set-model-binding.use-case.ts:38 (as duas causas de 404 deste endpoint — este arquivo NÃO mudou), apps/api/src/interfaces/http/shared/llm-binding-error.filter.ts:39 (400/422 das recusas de curadoria — este arquivo NÃO mudou), apps/web/src/routes/settings/ModelsSection.tsx (a cadeia da área), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/clear-model-binding.use-case.ts:25 (o 404 que a api levanta — este arquivo NÃO mudou), apps/api/src/domain/llm/binding-resolver.ts:140 (o origin: 'agent' que a api devolve, e o comentário que explica por que ele está certo — este arquivo NÃO mudou)
    • Teste: apps/web/src/routes/settings/cascata.test.tsx (binding próprio termina no nó agente; herança deixa agente vazio e nomeia o Criativo; o workspace nunca aparece vigente na herança; a seção de área idem; a distinção sobrevive ao en; nível descartado vira nó riscado e o badge concorrente sumiu; os três vazios com três textos; montarCadeia e herdouDoCriativo como funções puras, incluindo o caso do padrão de área DESCARTADO, que não segura a descida), apps/web/src/routes/settings/voltar-a-herdar.test.tsx (os três desfechos do clique: apagou e relê; 404 não é erro, não repassa a frase pt-BR da api nem em en, e relê porque a tela é que estava velha; outro status vai por mensagemDaApi e não relê), apps/web/src/routes/settings/troca-de-modelo.test.tsx (a outra ação da mesma linha: grava e relê; 422 do modelo unavailable — o alcançável, já que o picker mostra o indisponível marcado — vai por mensagemDaApi e não relê; a coluna MODELO VIGENTE segue no modelo antigo, nunca no recusado; e o 404 NÃO ganha desfecho próprio, que é o contraste com a função irmã)
    • Origem: revisão de design do dono do produto (item #9 do canvas de melhorias de UI — "cascata de modelo como cadeia visível")

    The Members section respects the role, and the role is the project's EFFECTIVE one (RN-471)

    RN-471 — Role in the Members section: maintainer, and derived from the project's row over the workspace's

    MembersSection checked no role at all. The section's THREE actions — invite, change someone's role, and remove — appeared active for everyone, viewer included, and the api refused with 403. Two of them didn't even get to say so: handleRoleChange and handleRemove had no try/catch and were called from an onChange/onClick, so every refusal became an unhandled promise rejection — silence on screen and noise in the console, the same class of defect that RN-102 closed in the models table. Removing a member silently is the worst of the three: it's a consequential action with no way back through the screen, because restoring requires the UUID the removed row took with it.

    The minimum is maintainer, and it's the ENDPOINT's. The three paths require the same (projects.controller.ts), and GET requires viewer — that's why whoever doesn't edit still sees the whole table:

    action endpoint role
    invite POST :projectId/members maintainer
    change role POST :projectId/members (upsert) maintainer
    remove DELETE :projectId/members/:userId maintainer
    see the table GET :projectId/members viewer

    Copying the models section's gate (developer, and correct there) would offer the three controls here to those the api refuses. It's the SECOND section in a row whose minimum differs from its neighbor: the ruler is still the endpoint, never the screen next door.

    The role is the PROJECT's EFFECTIVE one — and this CLOSES the gap RN-102 declared, it doesn't repeat it. There the screen reads the WORKSPACE role while what authorizes is ResolveEffectiveRoleUseCase.forProject, and closing that would require a per-project role query the web doesn't have. It's the SAME gap here, seen from the other side: this section ALREADY makes that query. listProjectMembers is findMemberRole for everyone at once, and userIdDaSessao() (the access token's sub) says which row belongs to whoever is looking. The composition is literally the use case's — projectRole ?? workspaceRole —, not a second source of role invented only on this screen. While the list hasn't arrived the role is ABSENT, not the workspace's: without it there's no way to know whether an own row exists, and erring on the side of disabling is fixed by reloading.

    The override applies in BOTH directions, and FOUR places said otherwise. forProject returns the project_members row when it exists, without comparing with the workspace. So setting viewer here really downgrades — even a workspace owner, who afterwards can only be restored by someone with maintainer. The code never did the opposite, and resolve-effective-role.use-case.spec.ts itself fixes this ("papel de projeto sobrepõe o de workspace"). Where the false promise was:

    place what it said status
    description of POST :projectId/members "the EFFECTIVE role is the higher of this one and what the person already has in the workspace"; "associating someone as viewer here doesn't downgrade a workspace owner" not fixed — it's an api change
    description of AddMemberDto.role the same "higher of" sentence not fixed — it's an api change
    summary of GET :projectId/members "with their effective role"; "includes whoever inherits access from the workspace" not fixed — it's an api change
    apps/web/src/lib/roles.ts:3 "Papel efetivo nunca é rebaixado" fixed here

    The three in the api stay declared and aren't touched in passing: the descriptions propagate to api-types.generated.ts. The web one is WORSE than the other three and was therefore fixed along with it — the api ones at least live in an OpenAPI description, while that one was in the module that roleAtLeast and ROLE_ORDER export, that is, in the code the next person of this family of fixes will open and edit, reading the wrong rule before anything else. The linear hierarchy declared on the same line remains valid — it's the order roleAtLeast compares, and it didn't change. The screen, in turn, SAYS what holds, instead of letting the Select be read as a harmless suggestion.

    The CONTROL disappears, never the INFORMATION (ADR 0064). Whoever doesn't have maintainer keeps reading each member's role in the dimmed Select itself — it's the table's central information, and swapping it for text would hide the state along with the action. The reason is said ONCE, in text, in the caption: title on a disabled element doesn't open in Chromium, and one line per member would repeat a fact about WHO IS LOOKING on top of each person on the list.

    The table is a SLICE, and declares it (RN-180). listMembers is an innerJoin on project_members: whoever reaches the project only through the workspace doesn't appear in any row, although the GET summary promises "includes whoever inherits access from the workspace". That data is NOT within the client's reach — no web query lists workspace members with role —, so the caption says the list omits, instead of letting it be read as "everyone who has access". It's the remaining gap, and it's declared, not handled.

    None of this is a security boundary: what refuses is still RolesGuard.

    Invite keeps the fixed hint, and it's a decision. The other two actions use mensagemDaApi because their userId came from the list and exists — what remains is 403 and network, and the api's sentence is the most useful information there is. In the invite the userId is TYPED, and the reachable error is pointing to a nonexistent user: a well-formed UUID passes @IsUUID(), blows the FK project_members.user_id → users.id, none of the global filters handles it, and Nest answers the default 500. mensagemDaApi would return "Internal server error", worse than the hint — its padrao only applies to an error that isn't an ApiError. Uniformizing the form would worsen the content on the box's most likely path.

    • Where: apps/api/src/interfaces/http/iam/projects.controller.ts:121, :135, :147 (the three roles — these lines did NOT change), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/iam/resolve-effective-role.use-case.ts:14 (projectRole ?? workspaceRole, the override in both directions), apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/project.repository.ts:194 (listMembers — the innerJoin that makes the table a slice), apps/web/src/routes/settings/MembersSection.tsx:62 (the effective role and why maintainer), :109 (why invite doesn't use mensagemDaApi), :142 and :162 (the two missing try/catch), apps/web/src/lib/roles.ts:49 (roleAtLeast), :3 (the ROLE_ORDER comment, which asserted "papel efetivo nunca é rebaixado" — the fourth place in the table above, and the only one fixed)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/papel-na-secao-de-membros.test.tsx (viewer and developer don't edit and the click doesn't reach the api; maintainer and owner edit and the POST/DELETE arrive; an absent role is inert; the row's role survives the dimmed control; the PAIR that proves the override in both directions — a workspace viewer with a maintainer row EDITS, a workspace owner with a viewer row does NOT edit; the two refusals become a toast with the api's sentence; the screen doesn't start displaying the role the api denied; and invite keeping the fixed hint against a 500)
    • Origin: review of #443 — the gap it declared, seen from the section that has the data to close it
    • Addendum (additive, the text above didn't change): the three OpenAPI descriptions the table marks as "not fixed" WERE fixed later, by RN-472 / ADR 0127, in the same change that put the two downgrade ceilings in the api. Don't reopen that row of the table.

    The folder comes first, and the binary isn't a blocker (RN-473/474)

    RN-473 — Runner configuration through the browser starts with the FOLDER, and the binary's failure never discards what has already been configured

    Revised by RN-687 (ADR 0203, 10/01): the flow this RN orders was REMOVED — runner-bootstrap.ts and its test were removed with the browser flow. What survives of it is the ruler of the human step stated BEFORE, which the panel applies to the installer's command.

    The flow of RN-464..466 ran in the order key → registration → binary → folder (configurarPastaAutomaticamente, apps/web/src/lib/runner-bootstrap.ts). The four steps don't carry the same risk: the binary download is the ONLY one that depends on a release published on GitHub (RunnerReleasesController, a proxy of releases/latest), and with the current release having no asset for any platform it returns 502. The exception went up before showDirectoryPicker, so the folder picker never got to open — the button looked inert and the folder was unreachable, with the whole configuration lost because of the most fragile step.

    The order becomes folder → config → key → registration → private key → binary, and the last step is best-effort:

    step does failure take down the flow? why
    1. showDirectoryPicker yes without a folder there's nowhere to write anything. Cancelling (AbortError/NotAllowedError) is NOT a failure — it goes back to the initial state, with no alert
    2. brabo-runner.config.json yes it's half of the pair RN-466 reads
    3. Ed25519 pair + registerRunnerDeviceKey + …device-key.jwk.json yes a private key on disk with no counterpart on the server doesn't authenticate; that's why registration comes BEFORE writing, and the two are adjacent
    4. binary no returns falhaDoBinario filled in; the final instruction becomes npm install -g @brabo/runner && brabo-runner

    The alternative path works with no flag at all precisely because steps 2 and 3 have already run: the CLI reads both files from the cwd (RN-466). It's one of the THREE distribution paths the CLAUDE.md declares, and the only one that depends on neither the release nor a monorepo checkout. baixarKitManual (outside Chromium) follows the same ruler by inverting the order of the two downloads: the kit comes out first, the binary last.

    The terminal step is human, and the screen never pretends otherwise. A web page doesn't execute a binary on anyone's machine, and the File System Access API doesn't preserve the execute bit — hence the chmod +x remaining in the command. What the product does is shrink that step to ONE line copyable with one click; no UI text says "automatic installation".

    And it's announced BEFORE the click, not only after. The sentence explaining the step existed from the start, but was rendered only in the SUCCESS state — after the person chose the folder, waited for the key registration and the binary download. Whoever clicks a button called "Configure folder automatically" and only then finds out they still need to open a terminal was surprised, even without any sentence having lied: announcing at the end is the latest possible moment to still not be pretense. Now there's a warning in the initial state, next to the button, and the one at the end STAYS — the two say different things (one warns that the step will come, the other explains why it exists). The button's label did not change: it speaks of the FOLDER, which is indeed configured automatically, and changing it would describe worse what it does.

    The final command says which folder to run in, when it can assert which one it is. The instruction said "inside the chosen folder, run: …" without ever saying where that folder is — the File System Access API exposes only the basename (dirHandle.name), never the absolute path. The path exists on the other side: it's the projects.workspace_path the person typed when creating the project. When its basename matches the chosen folder, the instruction gets a cd <caminho> && prefix; when it does NOT match, it comes out without a prefix — nothing forces the person to choose in the picker the same folder they typed, and a cd to the wrong place would be the screen asserting what it doesn't know. The basename is the most that can be proven from here, and the rare case where it coincides for different folders fails LOUD (cd doesn't find the path, or ./brabo-runner isn't there), never silently — which is the ruler that decides whether a heuristic may come in. The manual kit (fallback outside Chromium) does not get the prefix, and it isn't forgetfulness: there the files land in the downloads folder, and cd would send you where they aren't yet.

    Declared gap: closing the tab between step 3 and the end leaves an orphan device key in the project. It's inert — the corresponding private key only existed in that tab's memory, and without it the key authenticates nothing — but today it's invisible: RunnerDeviceKeysController has POST and DELETE, and no LISTING route, so there's no screen where to revoke it. Redoing the flow registers a new key, which works. A listing is direct work on the DELETE that already exists, when requested.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/runner-bootstrap.ts (configurarPastaAutomaticamente, baixarKitManual, COMANDO_VIA_NPM), apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.tsx (ehCancelamentoDoSeletor, the outcome block)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/runner-bootstrap.test.ts (the order of the four steps asserted as a list; binary 502 and a binary WRITE failure writing the other two files and returning the alternative command; picker cancellation without registering a key nor downloading anything; the kit coming out even with the binary at 502), apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.test.tsx (the folder is announced with the failure's reason beside it; cancelling doesn't become an alert)
    • ADR: 0118 — this RN revises the ORDER of the flow it established, not the mechanism
    • Origin: product owner's request

    RN-474 — After the instruction, the screen WAITS for the runner: three states, a ceiling, and the path that holds is the one the runner reported

    Configuring the folder and showing the command left the person not knowing whether it had worked. The fourth step is a wait that resolves on its own (apps/web/src/components/EsperaDoRunner.tsx), mounted by RunnerOnboardingPanel as soon as the configuration ends — on both paths (folder written and kit downloaded).

    The mechanism is what already existed. The signal is project.workspaceVerifiedAt, the stamp ConfirmProjectWorkspaceUseCase writes when the runner connects (RN-423) and which the engine uses as a GATE (terminal_executor.ex refuses to execute in a runner project with null workspace_verified_at) — the product's own definition of "this project has a runner". It's the same data that AmbienteDoProjeto already reads (RN-468), under the same cache key ['project', id] the whole page maintains. The alternative considered was probing connectFsBrowserChannel (whose error 'Nenhum runner conectado' FolderBrowserModal detects): it knows about NOW, which is stronger, but the channel writes erroDeConexao permanently per instance, so each probe would cost a NEW ticket + socket — dozens over the wait — and it still wouldn't answer the second half of what the screen must say: WHICH path the runner reported.

    Confirmed is the stamp CHANGING, never "existing". The wait takes a baseline on the first GET and compares against it. Without that, a project already confirmed before would be announced as freshly connected the instant the screen opened.

    The three states don't collapse, and none is eternal (RN-088, RN-468):

    state what the screen says
    esperando "looking for the runner", with the ceiling DECLARED ("we stop after 3 minutes")
    confirmado the stamp's date AND the workspacePath the runner reported, saying it replaces what was typed — the screen doesn't compete with whoever runs on the real host
    semResposta the ceiling overflowed, and the screen declares what it does NOT know: reconnecting with an already confirmed folder doesn't rewrite the stamp, so absence here isn't proof of absence, and what knows about now is the Code tab. A button restarts the search without redoing the configuration
    • Where: apps/web/src/components/EsperaDoRunner.tsx (INTERVALO_MS, TETO_MS, the baseline in base), apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.tsx (who mounts it)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/EsperaDoRunner.test.tsx (the three states, one by one, each asserting that the other two are NOT on screen; the preexisting stamp that doesn't count as a new connection; the ceiling overflowing with a fake timer and the button that restarts)
    • ADR: 0118
    • Origin: product owner's request

    The kid is the link, and the refusal has a name (RN-475)

    RN-475 — The private JWK written by the browser carries the registration's kid; and the CLI distinguishes "there's no key" from "there's a key and it doesn't work"

    Revised by RN-687 (ADR 0203, 10/01): the BROWSER half left with the flow (and the e2e that proved it in a Chromium); the stamped kid still applies to the key created in the terminal (device-key finish --id, RN-551), and the CLI half — absent × refused — remains intact.

    The automatic mode of ADR 0118 (RN-464..466) never authenticated a single time since it was born. The two halves of this RN are the defect and what hid it.

    The kid isn't decoration — it's the only link between the two sides. The id that POST projects/:projectId/runner-device-keys returns identifies the runner_device_keys record that keeps the PUBLIC key. It needs to reach disk INSIDE the private JWK, in the kid field (RFC 7517), because that's how the whole chain carries it: lerChaveDeDispositivo only PASSES ON jwk.kid (it never invents nor derives an id), assinarTicketComChaveDeDispositivo puts it in the protected header of the ticket JWT, and PatAuthGuard uses exactly that kid to find the public key and verify the signature (RN-465).

    The browser discarded the return of registerRunnerDeviceKey on BOTH paths (configurarPastaAutomaticamente and baixarKitManual), and crypto.subtle.exportKey exports the RAW JWK, with no kid at all. The producer wrote a file the consumer always refuses — and the defect was invisible from both sides: each function was correct on its own. The proof is in the database: the keys registered by the flow had null last_used_at, all of them.

    The fix is structural, not one line: step 3 of RN-473 (export the public key, register, export the private key) becomes ONE function (registrarChaveEExportarPrivada), with the id flowing inside it. Discarding it again now requires deleting code, not forgetting an assignment. The kid goes only in the PRIVATE key: the public one doesn't carry it, because it's its registration that produces it.

    The refusal has a name. lerChaveDeDispositivo returns null without throwing — on purpose, and that doesn't change: the absence of the files is the NORMAL path of whoever runs with --project/--dir/--token. But null answered two different questions, and the CLI printed the SAME uso() block — which talks about flags and doesn't mention the file — both for "you configured nothing" and for "your folder is configured and the key doesn't work". A person with a perfect brabo-runner.config.json was sent to investigate precisely the side that was right.

    state who answers CLI output
    ausente normal path the uso() block, unchanged
    json-invalido refusal explicacaoDaChaveRecusada — names the file, the reason, and the two ways out (rewrite the folder through the browser, or --token meanwhile)
    sem-kid refusal likewise, naming the missing field and what it's for
    valida follows the flow —

    estadoDaChaveDeDispositivo and explicacaoDaChaveRecusada live in device-key.ts because they talk about the FILE; what to DO with the refusal (exit, with which code, with or without the usage block) remains index.ts's, as the module's docblock already said. The type of explicacaoDaChaveRecusada excludes ausente and valida: there's no explanation for the normal case nor for success, and a vague text covering the four states would be the same defect again.

    What let it through: the web test asserted that the file had been OPENED (getFileHandle called with the right name), never what was INSIDE it. A test that proves the creation of a file without proving its content lets anything be written. The double now ties name → content, and the two old assertions gained the missing half.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/runner-bootstrap.ts (registrarChaveEExportarPrivada, exportarJwkPrivada), apps/runner/src/device-key.ts (estadoDaChaveDeDispositivo, explicacaoDaChaveRecusada, EstadoDaChaveLocal), apps/runner/src/index.ts (the credential branch in lerArgumentos)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/runner-bootstrap.test.ts (the written kid IS the returned id, proven with an id different from the default; both paths — folder and manual kit; the public key WITHOUT kid; the useful key surviving the binary at 502), apps/runner/src/device-key.spec.ts (the four states, one by one; absent ≠ sem-kid while lerChaveDeDispositivo collapses both into null; the two refusal sentences different from each other), apps/runner/src/index.spec.ts (the REAL CLI, in a separate process: the folder without a key falls to the usage block, the folder with a key without kid falls to the named refusal, and the two outputs aren't equal — the defect was that they were)
    • ADR: 0118 — this RN doesn't revise its mechanism, it closes the contract it left implicit between the two ends
    • Origin: real use of the flow against the local environment by the product owner

    Environment state on screen (RN-468)

    RN-476 — Applying a model to ALL agents: one value, N calls, and a button even though the value is named

    The Models per agent table has one row per agent and a selector in each one. Choosing the same model for the 17 meant going through the 17 rows, and the cost of that isn't time: it's that nobody checks 17 dropdowns and the screen has no way to say whether one was left behind. The section gains a bar ABOVE the table — a selector and a button — that applies ONE model to all agents at once.

    The action writes at the AGENT level, on the 17 rows — not on the project. The two readings of "one model for all" exist and produce different states: writing on the project and deleting the agent and area bindings would make the 17 INHERIT, and that's the cascade's own idiom. It wasn't the one chosen, for two concrete prices: PUT projects/:id/model-binding and the area endpoints require maintainer, while the agent binding requires developer — the same person who can change row by row would no longer be able to do it all at once —, and the project binding is also the SESSION's default, which RN-040 leaves free on purpose. The choice has a declared price: the 17 rows now diverge, with origin agent in the Origin column, and going back to inheriting remains row by row.

    There's a button, even though the value is NAMED. The ruler of RN-469 says a choice of a named value saves on onChange, and it still applies to EACH ROW's selector, which writes its own value. The bar's selector isn't configuration of anything: it's the argument of the action next to it. Applying on onChange would make an exploratory click on a dropdown rewrite 17 rows the person wasn't editing, and undoing that is 17 clicks. The button NAMES how many rows it will reach, because that number is the consequence.

    The outcome follows RN-469 entirely, and that's the reason the bar invents nothing. One call per agent, in SERIES, in the screen's order, without aborting on the first refusal — aborting would leave 16 rows with no attempt and the screen wouldn't distinguish "refused" from "didn't even try". The three outcomes don't disguise themselves: all passed → success naming the model; none passed → the api's message, never a count; some passed → a warning with how many out of how many, naming the ones left by the agent's NAME, which is what the person reads in the first column. Only the rows the api CONFIRMED are reread: invalidating the 17 would erase from the screen the difference the partial report just counted.

    The gate is the ENDPOINT's, and the endpoint is the same as the row controls'. developer, through PUT projects/:projectId/agent-bindings/:agentSlug — not the neighboring area section's maintainer (RN-102). The button is inert for whoever doesn't reach it, and the reason is said ONCE, in TEXT, in the section's caption, together with the other two actions that are inert: title on a disabled element doesn't open in Chromium (ADR 0064). The selector's "fit for agents" filter opens CHECKED, as in the row picker and for the same reason (RN-040) — with more force here, because a chat-only model chosen in the bar wouldn't produce one 422, but 17, and the partial report would count the same refusal seventeen times.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/settings/aplicar-a-todos.tsx:66 (useAplicacaoEmLote — the serial loop and the three outcomes), apps/web/src/routes/settings/ModelsSection.tsx (the bar, the local state of the chosen model, and invalidating only the confirmed rows)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/aplicar-a-todos.test.tsx (writes the same model on all agents, in the screen's order; none passing shows the api's message and NOT the count, and still tries all of them; a partial failure says how many out of how many and names the one left; a role below developer leaves the button inert and the reason stays in text)
    • Origin: the product owner's request while configuring a blank environment with a single provider — all agents needed the same model

    RAG search leaves a trace, and the trace freezes the weights (RN-479..481)

    RN-491 — The Infra elects among the Arquiteto's candidates, and the election becomes a NEW version of artifact.project_image

    propose_container_start closes the half RN-487 left declared open: the Infra elects ONE of the imagemCandidata of the Arquiteto's current routing (artifact.module_routing) and proposes bringing up the project's REAL container. The election only has effect because ExecuteContainerStartUseCase REUSES DecidirImagemDoProjetoUseCase to emit a new version of artifact.project_image with decidedBy: 'infra-lead' — without that the election would be auditable and INERT: the broker composes the container by reading GET .../container-spec, which reads artifact.project_image (RN-105), never artifact.module_routing.

    The elected image must be in the candidates list, always — validated BEFORE touching the artifact. Outside the list, the action fails naming the refused image and listing the valid candidates, and neither DecidirImagemDoProjetoUseCase nor the broker gets called: the Infra elects, never invents.

    A new container_start (maintainer, the same caliber as open_infra_pr/ parallelize), deliberately OUTSIDE the block of absolute ceilings of decide.ts — the same reasoning already recorded there for propose_execution_plan/assess_implementability: it's the FIRST time this container really comes up for this election, not exceeding an already authorized ceiling. Unlike open_infra_pr, this action is NOT seeded in INFRA_AUTONOMY_SEEDS — the Infra Lead never applies anything with open_infra_pr (it only proposes a PR a human still merges), but bringing up a container is a real external effect, so it stays require_approval by default, decided case by case through the ApprovalCard. A maintainer CAN configure auto-approval later, and that's why ProposeActionUseCase also executes on the auto_approved path, the same lesson as the comment about parallelize in that file ("without this the action was born, was approved — and nothing came up").

    Revised by RN-671 (ADR 0190, 10/01): container_start becomes SEEDED auto_approve on the acceptance of the Infra's handoff, and the Infra Lead's server proposes the first startup on its own when there's routing. The rest of this rule — the candidates list, maintainer, outside the absolute ceilings — remains.

    After the broker confirms, the lifecycle transition follows ADR 0081's state machine, never reprovisions needlessly. No row yet, or a row in failed/removed: provisioning (the one that reads the newly decided image and freezes imageVersion) and only then running. A row in stopped: straight to running — the state machine doesn't even allow stopped -> provisioning, and reprovisioning would reemit an image already written in the row. A row already provisioning/running: completes the pending transition or does nothing, relying on the broker's idempotent start (jaEstavaDePe).

    Revises RN-485. "Nothing triggers a startup... the only caller that exists today does a READ" stops being true: container_start is the first real caller of ContainerBrokerPort.start, of the five operations ADR 0128/0130 declared. RegistrarTransicaoDeContainerUseCase gains its first caller outside tests too. It still holds that NOTHING fires on its own — it's always a proposed_action, decided by a human or by explicit policy.

    Dev agents do NOT start working inside the container that comes up here. That stage is a later PR, declared open in CLAUDE.md — the sentence the approval screen shows (apps/web/src/lib/aprovacoes.ts) stops on purpose before promising that.

    • Where: apps/api/src/application/use-cases/actions/execute-container-start.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/domain/actions/decide.ts (ACTION_TYPES, MIN_ROLE_FOR_ACTION_TYPE), apps/api/src/domain/containers/container-start-execution-result.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/actions/approve-action.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/actions/propose-action.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/get-infra-context.use-case.ts (moduleRouting, the first HTTP consumer of GetModuleRoutingUseCase), apps/web/src/lib/aprovacoes.ts, apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/tools/propose_container_start.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/infra_lead_server.ex
    • Test: apps/api/test/application/use-cases/actions/execute-container-start.use-case.spec.ts (happy path electing a candidate; an image outside the candidates refuses without calling DecidirImagemDoProjetoUseCase nor the broker; BrokerRecusouError/ BrokerIndisponivelError become failed, never propagate; stopped jumps straight to running), apps/api/test/domain/actions/decide.spec.ts (container_start requires maintainer; it CAN reach auto_approve when configured — unlike parallelize/protected merge/instruction_patch), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/execution/get-infra-context.use-case.spec.ts (moduleRouting present and absent), apps/web/src/lib/aprovacoes.test.ts (the sentence doesn't promise a dev agent inside the container), apps/engine/test/engine/infra/infra_lead_server_test.exs
    • ADR: 0130, 0133
    • Origin: the product owner's plan, Part 1 / PR 1.5

    RN-499 — The Infra's handoff has its OWN actionable card, outside the thread — without widening AGENTES_DE_CHAT

    OfferInfraHandoffUseCase offers the handoff to the Infra Lead (offer-infra-handoff.use-case.ts:43), and until now no screen could accept it. offeredHandoff (SessionPage.tsx) restricts the thread's card to AGENTES_DE_CHAT (RN-136), which doesn't include infra, and acceptHandoff (api-client.ts) had a single consumer — that card, behind that filter. The code's own comment recorded the consequence as accepted: "since Infra is never accepted HERE … in practice, never".

    The effect wasn't cosmetic: the handoff stayed offered forever, the Infra Lead was never activated, propose_container_start was never called (RN-491) and no project of any mode ever got to have a container up. The chain "Infra accepts → proposes container_start → approved → running" was unreachable through any screen path.

    The filter was NOT widened, and that's the rule. AGENTES_DE_CHAT is right to exclude infra: the engine's agent_command_controller.ex has no message clause for the Infra Lead, and the last clause (unguarded) would treat "infra" as if it were the Criativo — putting infra on the list would make the screen offer a conversation thread that doesn't exist and the composer send a message to the wrong agent. (Since RN-584 the engine refuses by name instead of delivering to the Criativo; the filter remains right for the same reason.) Accepting the Infra is a propositional action, not a conversational one.

    The actionable card lives, then, outside the thread: in the fixed strip between the scrolling area and the composer, the same one that hosts the manual handoff (RN-440) and already declares itself the place of handoff actions that "aren't conversation, they're redirection". Three consequences the choice buys: the Infra's handoff.offered stays narrated in the thread as a silent divider (nothing changes in the timeline); no conversational handoff changes shape; and the button doesn't disappear when the offer leaves the 200-event window in a long session — the thread's card would depend on the event being visible, this one doesn't.

    The card states the click's consequence, because it isn't obvious: accepting activates the Infra Lead, which takes over provisioning and will propose bringing up the container; the proposal still goes through the usual approval pipeline (container_start, maintainer, never auto-approvable by seed — RN-491). Accepting brings up no container at all.

    Closing: the same activeFor(h.toAgent) as the thread's card — a handoff already accepted, or Infra already active in this session by any other path, doesn't reopen the invite. And the click calls the same handleAcceptHandoff, with no second accept path.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (handoffDaInfraOferecido, and the styles.infraHandoffRow block above the manualHandoffRow)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-da-infra.test.tsx — the card appears and accepts with the right ids; disappears with Infra already active; disappears in a non-active session; the thread's silent divider stays there with no thread button; and the non-regression of RN-136: Infra (older) + Dev Lead (newer) pending show BOTH cards, each accepting its own handoff
    • Origin: exploration of the plan "Name and location on the same screen, and container before the dev agents" (D0) — the blocker that made the requirement "dev agent only after the container is up" equivalent to "dev agent never starts"

    The folder browser served by the api (RN-504)

    RN-504 — The folder browser is scoped to the projects base and NEVER leaves it

    GET /workspaces/:workspaceId/project-folders lists the subfolders of a path, and exists because the project creation wizard loses, at once, BOTH "browse folder" mechanisms it had: FolderBrowserModal navigated through the runner's websocket (fs_list_dir/fs_home_dir) and RunnerOnboardingPanel used showDirectoryPicker, which returns a browser handle and never an absolute path. Without a runner, the browser has no way to list any filesystem — and it's an absolute path that projects.workspace_path keeps.

    The containment is ONE, and it's hard. path is optional and omitted means the base (baseDeProjetos()); every provided path has to satisfy dentroDaBaseDeProjetos (RN-500), which reuses dentroDoEscopo — the same function as ADR 0055's terminal scope — and that's why it catches the prefix trap: /home/voce/brabo2 is not inside /home/voce/brabo, although the string starts the same. .. and . are refused instead of resolved, for the same reason as caminhoDeWorkspaceLocalValido: resolving would accept that the path read isn't the path requested.

    Leaving the base is 400, and not 403. 403 would say "you don't have permission to see this" and would suggest another role would see it — that's not the case. There's no role that navigates outside the base, because outside the base isn't a more privileged area, it's an area this route simply doesn't address. The request is MALFORMED.

    The ceilings are a contract, not an implementation detail: only directories in entries; at most 500, sorted before the cut (otherwise "the first 500" would be the order the filesystem returned, which changes between machines); no recursion; entries starting with . left out; and symlinks reported, never descended — readdirSync(withFileTypes) has lstat semantics, so a link pointing outside the base isn't an exit door. What's left out is COUNTED (arquivos, simbolicos, truncado): without that a folder full of code would come back as an empty list and the screen would say "empty folder", asserting about what it didn't read (RN-180).

    There's no POST. Creating a folder belongs to the mounted workspace's materialization, at the moment the container comes up — never to the picker.

    The minimum is maintainer, the same as POST .../projects and .../projects-base, and by the same reasoning one step further: projects-base reveals ONE path of the operator's machine, this route reveals the TOPOLOGY below it. Inheriting the viewer of the neighboring routes because they're neighbors is the defect RN-102 names — the minimum is the ENDPOINT's, never the section's. workspaceId doesn't enter the calculation: the base belongs to the INSTALLATION, and it's in the route because it's what gives scope to RolesGuard.

    On the client, the transport becomes one of the TWO implementations of the FsBrowser interface (apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser.ts): criarFsBrowserViaApi (new) and connectFsBrowserChannel (the runner's channel, retyped). FolderBrowserModal chooses by origem: { tipo: 'api'; workspaceId } | { tipo: 'runner'; projectId } — a discriminated union, and not two optional props, because "neither of the two" and "both" would be representable states the component would have to handle at runtime. The transport via runner is left with no caller in the web from here on, and stays in the repository by the product owner's declared decision (the runner leaves the interface, the binary keeps being refined); the protocol in apps/runner/src/channel.ts isn't touched in any way.

    • Where: apps/api/src/infrastructure/filesystem/project-folders-browser.ts (listarPastasDeProjeto, TETO_DE_ENTRADAS, PastaForaDaBaseError, PastaNaoLegivelError); apps/api/src/interfaces/http/iam/workspaces.controller.ts (listProjectFolders); apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser.ts; apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.tsx
    • Test: apps/api/test/infrastructure/filesystem/project-folders-browser.spec.ts, apps/api/test/interfaces/http/iam/workspaces-project-folders.controller.spec.ts, apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser.test.ts, apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.test.tsx
    • ADR: 0141 (the base this route scopes)
    • Origin: the product owner's plan, PR 4

    The base decides what project creation offers (RN-513)

    RN-513 — With a consented base, mounted is preselected with <base>/<slug> suggested; without a base, or without knowing, it isn't offered

    Revised by RN-573 (ADR 0161): the preselection of mounted now ALSO requires the container broker confirmed. With a base and no broker (the installation from a Release), runner is preselected and container/mounted stay inert with the reason in text; with the broker unknown, container stays selected. The rest of this rule holds as is.

    Two halves of this rule were already rules and had never reached the client. "Don't offer the mode without a base" is RN-500; "suggest <base>/<slug>" is RN-501, and ADR 0142 postponed disk validation precisely so that the wizard could propose a folder that doesn't exist yet. Until now, GET /workspaces/:workspaceId/projects-base had no caller at all in the web, and the "Mounted folder" card was offered unconditionally — in an installation without BRABO_PROJECTS_BASE, choosing the mode ended in a 400 refusal from the api after the person had chosen, typed the path, and reached the end of the wizard.

    What's new is the preselection: with a configured base, mounted is born selected. It revises the default of ADR 0072 (where container is "the normal one" and the local mode is an escape hatch) and revises it only for the local installation, where the base was consented by a human on the host (RN-511). The project_execution_mode enum doesn't change, no new mode is born, and mode conversion (ADR 0111) isn't touched.

    The query is born with the wizard, not with the step. The "Where the code will live" step is the fifth; a useQuery mounted only there would make the card appear flickering after the person was already looking at the screen.

    Unknown doesn't become an offer. While the answer hasn't arrived, and also when the query fails (403, network, api down), the card doesn't appear and container stays selected. A failure isn't permission to offer a mode whose precondition couldn't be confirmed: it's the same ruler as RN-088/RN-468 — the product doesn't collapse "don't know" with "doesn't have" — and that of ADRs 0041/0042, where a capability is only declared when proven.

    The preselection never overrides a human choice. The base arrives over the network, always AFTER the first render. The screen keeps the user's choice separate from the current mode (modoDeWorkspaceEscolhido × modoDeWorkspace), and the preselection only holds while nobody has touched the cards — swapping the mode under the hand of whoever already clicked is a defect, not a convenience.

    The suggestion never clobbers what was typed. <base>/<slug> comes in in two cases and only in them: an empty field with nothing suggested yet, or a field containing exactly this screen's previous suggestion (the name changed in the details step, and the suggestion follows). Anything else — including the empty field after the user deleted the suggestion — stays as it is. An empty slug (adoption, where the name comes from the provider) doesn't become an invented segment: the field stays empty, and canAdvanceFromWorkspace holds the step. The field is editable the whole time; the preselection didn't relax any advance ruler.

    And the mode's warning stopped lying. The previous text predated ADR 0141: it said the path was free and that it would only work if the user mounted it. Today there are two states, and they're the two the backend has — under the base, a neutral note (creation passes, and the folder doesn't even need to exist: the Infra creates it when bringing up the container, RN-501); outside the base, a warning saying that the api refuses creation (400, in resolverWorkspacePath) and naming the base, instead of telling you to go look for the variable. An empty field isn't "outside the base": there's no path for the api to refuse yet, and alarming before the person types would be the screen asserting about what it doesn't have. The comparison is by segment, never a raw startsWith — /base-outra isn't inside /base —, mirroring the api's dentroDoEscopo.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.tsx:220 (the query), :238 (podeOferecerMounted/baseDeProjetos), :283 (the current mode), :333 (caminhoForaDaBase), :360 (the suggestion); apps/web/src/lib/wizard.ts:143 (caminhoSugeridoNaBase) and :171 (caminhoDentroDaBase) — the pure functions; apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:296 (getProjectsBase)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.test.tsx (describe('NewProjectWizard — a base de projetos decide o que é oferecido') — base present, null base, query refused with 403, the human choice before the base arrives, and the two states of the warning); apps/web/src/lib/wizard.test.ts (describe('caminhoSugeridoNaBase') and describe('caminhoDentroDaBase'))
    • ADR: 0146, point 4
    • Origin: PHASE 28, session 3

    The Runner mode picker reads the disk of WHOEVER CHOOSES again (RN-533)

    RN-533 — In runner mode the folder browser asks the local agent, and the screen says whose disk it is

    The creation wizard had one folder browser for two modes that read different disks. RN-504 pointed both to the api — right for mounted, whose folder lives under BRABO_PROJECTS_BASE, on the SERVER — and declared the price in writing: "for runner the list stops being the disk of the user's machine and becomes the base". That was accepted because "the runner mode leaves project creation in the next PR", which did not happen: the mode is still offered, and the direction of ADR 0151 (point 7) is the opposite. While it lasted, choosing runner and clicking "Browse folder…" browsed a disk that is not that project's — and nothing on the screen said so, so the error would only show up through the list's CONTENT.

    The MODE decides the transport, and they are two different questions. mounted stays byte for byte through the api (GET workspaces/:workspaceId/project-folders, hard-scoped to the base — RN-504 intact); runner goes back to fs_list_dir, which always existed on both sides and whose transport in the web never left the repository. It is not a leftover transport that gained a use: it is the question "what is on YOUR machine?", which the api has no way to answer, being asked again of whoever has a way.

    The anchor of the runner mode is the EARLY creation of RN-437. The channel ticket is scoped to a real projectId, so the click creates the project before opening the modal — the original purpose of that rule, which RN-504 had left without a consumer. And failing that creation does not open the modal in any transport: falling back to the api would show the SERVER's base under the label of "your machine", exactly the defect this rule closes.

    Without a connected local agent, the mode is still offered — and what declares it is the PICKER, not the mode step. RN-513 hides mounted when the base is unknown or absent, and it is right: there the api HAS a signal (GET .../projects-base), and without a base creation ends in a 400 at the end of the wizard, with no fix on the screen. Here neither of the two things holds. There is no runner presence signal in the api — RN-521 already declares that in writing —, so hiding the mode or graying out the button would be deciding "it doesn't have" from "I don't know", the collapse that RN-088/RN-468 forbid; and the mode is not broken without a runner, because the path is confirmed later, by the CLI (RN-423) — what is missing is only the NAVIGATION. So the declaration goes to the only place that actually asks: the picker, at the moment it asks.

    The reason is DISCRIMINATED, and the two outcomes do not collapse. MotivoDeFalhaDoAgente is sem-agente (the engine ASSERTS there is no runner connected — a server fact) or sem-resposta (request ceiling, dropped socket, refused ticket — ignorance, and stated as ignorance on the screen). Before this rule what decided was erro?.includes('Nenhum runner conectado') in the COMPONENT — a screen matching a substring of a pt-BR sentence that lives in an .ex of the engine, exactly what RN-532 refuses in writing on the other side ("the text is for a human to read, the reason is for the other side to DECIDE"). The matching remained in one place, the module that owns the channel protocol, and the degradation is named: a sentence rewritten in the engine falls into sem-resposta — the "I don't know" state —, never into "there is an agent". The api transport never fills in motivo, and that is a decision: an api refusal is an api refusal, it has its own message that TEACHES and there is no local agent to install to fix it.

    The wait is that of RN-474, REUSED and not rewritten. Both reasons lead to EsperaDoRunner — same three states (esperando/confirmado/ semResposta, which never collapse), same 3-minute CEILING, same wording, same signal (workspaceVerifiedAt CHANGING, never "existing"). It gained an optional callback because here the confirmation has a CONSEQUENCE: the listing that failed is redone on its own, instead of the person having to discover they need to click something. The callback fires ONCE per instance — without the ref, each round of the probe would re-fire it and the caller would enter a loop — and it does not assert "it is up now" (workspaceVerifiedAt never said that, RN-468): that is why whoever receives it REPEATS the request, and if the agent has already dropped again the screen goes back to the same state, honest both times. The wait is ONE per screen: RunnerOnboardingPanel gained mostrarEspera, false only where whoever mounts it already shows its own — two would be two probes, two ceilings and, in the worst case, two sentences disagreeing about the same stamp.

    And the picker says WHOSE disk it is, in BOTH origins. The only signal before that was the label of a shortcut ("Projects base" × "Personal folder"), which names a place and not a machine. The api's sentence is not symmetry decoration: without it the runner's would read as an exceptional warning, when it is information both owe.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser.ts:60 (MotivoDeFalhaDoAgente, and why only the runner transport fills it in); apps/web/src/lib/fs-browser-channel.ts:66 (FRASE_DO_ENGINE_SEM_RUNNER, the only substring match that remained) and :68 (motivoDaMensagem); apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.tsx:227 (agenteNaoRespondeu, the decision by reason) and the origin line in both origins; apps/web/src/components/EsperaDoRunner.tsx:91 (onConfirmado) and :136 (the ref that fires it once); apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.tsx:131 (mostrarEspera); apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.tsx:304 (origemDoNavegador, the transport chosen by mode, undefined when there is no project to anchor)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/FolderBrowserModal.test.tsx (the list declaring the disk of the user's machine; sem-agente falling into the onboarding panel; the decision by REASON and not by text, with a wording that nobody promised to keep; sem-resposta with the caveat that this does not prove absence, ONLY ONE wait and the ceiling announced; the listing redone on its own when the stamp changes); apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.test.tsx (the mode deciding the transport in both directions; the early creation anchoring the channel; the creation that FAILS opening no browser at all)
    • ADR: 0151 point 7
    • Origin: PHASE 29, session 9. Declared and NOT done: the picker does not trigger workspace_create — choosing a folder is choosing, and creating is something else. Engine.Runners.PastaDoProjeto.criar/3 (RN-532) still has no caller, and wiring it would require a new HTTP route in the api, outside the scope of this session. The definitive fix of the substring match — the engine sending a code alongside the text, as the runner already does with the five motivo of RN-532 — also belongs to the engine side, and is declared in the code

    Creating a project does not provision a repository (RN-541)

    RN-541 — Creating a project does NOT create a git repository; the bootstrap is postponed until the Arquiteto's handoff

    Creating a project and provisioning git were, in practice, the same gesture — and what joined them was a SCREEN. ProvisioningPage fires provisionRepository in the mount effect and is its only caller in the web; the wizard navigated there on confirm, so every project was born with a full repository and Gitflow, without anyone having asked.

    The product owner wants the opposite: what is generated for now goes without git, and the repository is only born when development really starts — the moment when the Arquiteto passes the handoff to the Dev Lead.

    The postponement mechanism is not navigating to that screen. On create, the wizard goes straight to the PROJECT page. Nothing else needed to be turned off: since the trigger lives in the useEffect of a route that is no longer visited, ceasing to visit it IS the postponement. The route still exists and is still reachable — the Dashboard leads to it when provisioning failed and needs to be resumed.

    Provider and credential leave creation, and stay in adoption. With no repository to create now, asking "where to host" would be collecting a decision the screen does not use, and the credential step would demand a PAT for a repository nobody will create. Adopting is the opposite by construction — it points to a repository that already exists, and without a provider there is nothing to point to. The wizard now has three steps when creating and four or five when adopting.

    The Confirm screen stops promising Gitflow. It announced "N Gitflow steps" when creating, counting BOOTSTRAP_STEPS; that would become a lie the moment the bootstrap stopped running there. It now says WHEN the repository is born, instead of asserting it is already being born.

    Declared consequence: between creation and the handoff, the project exists without a repository. Nothing breaks — GET .../repository answers null and not 404, the project page already has the "repository not provisioned" state and the Repository section in Settings simply does not appear —, but every path that needs a working tree fails NAMED until then (remoto_de_trabalho returns :not_found, and open_adr_pr refuses with "Projeto sem repositório provisionado"). That stops being an edge and becomes the normal state of the beginning of a project.

    The trigger is the next delivery, and without it a project created today would stay without a repository forever: accepting the Arquiteto → Dev Lead handoff now provisions local, the only provider that does not ask for a credential. Since RN-582, that trigger is accepting the handoff to the Arquiteto (the Dev Lead one stayed as a second door), and activating execution without a repository is refused.

    The warning "on GitHub's free plan, a private repository does not accept branch protection" left the screen along with it — not because it stopped being true, but because it became unreachable: the condition was provider === 'github', and when creating there is no longer a chosen provider.

    • Where: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.tsx:379 (stepKeys, now dependent on adotando), :540 (the provider guard restricted to adoption) and :590 (the navigation to /projects/$projectId)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.test.tsx, describe('NewProjectWizard — o git é adiado') — "criar não passa mais pela página de provisionamento — vai direto ao projeto", "criar tem TRÊS passos: provider e credencial saíram", "adotar mantém provider e credencial" e "a tela de Confirmar não promete mais o Gitflow agora"
    • Origin: request of the product owner — "o que for gerado por hora segue sem git; git mesmo só será iniciado após iniciar desenvolvimento em projetos novos, neste momento que deve rodar o bootstrap"

    The screen recognizes an already paired MACHINE local agent (RN-548, PHASE 30)

    RN-548 — With an active MACHINE key, the runner panel stops telling to pair and starts saying what is missing — without ever asserting that the agent is up

    Session 7 of PHASE 30, decided by ADR 0154. It is the WEB half of the machine identity: RN-543 made the key listing mark the KIND (projeto | maquina) "so that a machine key would not stay invisible on every screen", and it was left without a consumer. This RN is the consumer.

    The defect, measured. RunnerOnboardingPanel is the panel the THREE onboarding points mount (TerminalPanel, FolderBrowserModal and the workspace step of NewProjectWizard), and it had a single question: "configure the folder". With ADR 0154's machine key, that question becomes wrong in the most common case — the machine has already been paired, what is missing is the agent running. Telling the person to repeat the pairing the machine already has is the panel answering the wrong question, with a flow that ends in their terminal.

    Seven states, and none becomes the other (RN-088/RN-470). semPapel (we did not ask), verificando (we asked and it did not come back), naoSei (the question failed), semChaveDeMaquina (it came back and there is none), revogada (there was and it was revoked), pareadaNuncaUsada (there is an active key that no agent used — the ORPHAN of RN-519 reborn in the new kind) and pareada. "I don't know" never becomes "it doesn't have", and the two empties have different texts because the gesture is different: one asks to pair, the other explains why the agent stopped connecting. The failure is derived from isError and not from there being a status — a network error has no status, and deriving it from the status would leave a refused fetch spinning in "verifying…" forever, which is a lie by omission after ten seconds. semChaveDeMaquina is the only one that renders NOTHING, and on purpose: the whole panel already IS the answer to "no machine paired".

    Recognizing is NOT saying the agent is up, and the rule is the usual one. workspaceVerifiedAt is the record of a confirmation and not a heartbeat (RN-468); a registered key is the same thing one step earlier — it proves the machine was paired one day, never that there is a live process now. A screen that confused the two would be WORSE than today's, because today's at least does not lie. That is why the alert's tone is accent and never success (green would read as "it's up" — the same arithmetic AmbienteDoProjeto does on the runner row), the caveat is stated in text alongside, and what answers for the NOW remains EsperaDoRunner (RN-474), REUSED and not rewritten: once the machine is recognized, what is missing is the agent CONNECTING, which is exactly its question.

    And there is a second limit, which the data imposes and no wording erases: the list belongs to the ACCOUNT, not to this browser. runner_device_keys does not know which machine the browser is talking about, so the strongest the screen can assert is "your account has a paired machine" — never "this machine is paired". That is why the browser flow (ADR 0118) is not removed: it changes PLACE, into a <details> whose label names the only case in which it is still the answer ("I am on another machine"). Retiring it is BRB-031, the maintainer's decision, and not a consequence of this delivery.
