    The KIND shows up, and what shows up is its COST. The panel does not list keys nor offer revocation — that is the screen the web never got, the open half of RN-519, and it is its own front. What it says is what the kind means to whoever reads: a machine key serves ALL the owner's projects in runner mode, and revoking it takes down the local agent in all of them (RN-520, whose {projeto, usuário} reach does not change here). Without that sentence, RN-543's mark would be decoration.

    The role is the ENDPOINT's, and the api is the authority. The minimum comes from roleAtLeast (RN-102) against developer, which RunnerDeviceKeysController requires on the three routes — the screen stops asking what the api would deny, and says why ONCE in TEXT (title on a disabled element does not open in Chromium). This is not a security boundary: what refuses is the RolesGuard. The real 403 falls into the SAME state, and it is not redundancy — the role read is the WORKSPACE one and what authorizes is the project's EFFECTIVE one (RN-471), an OVERLAY in both directions: the proxy avoids the obviously lost question, the api corrects the proxy.

    Where it shows up, and why it is not the same in the three mounters. Recognition is per PROJECT, because the route is GET /projects/:projectId/runner-device-keys — it exists in TerminalPanel and FolderBrowserModal, which always have a project, and in NewProjectWizard only AFTER the early creation (RN-437). Without a projectId there is no one to ask and the panel stays byte for byte as it was. The difference is not a design choice: it is the shape of the endpoint.

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/agente-de-maquina.ts (the whole derivation — reconhecerAgenteDeMaquina, podeLerChavesDeDispositivo, maquinaJaPareada), apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.tsx:173 (the two queries and ReconhecimentoDeMaquina), apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.module.css (.reconhecimento, .gesto), apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:434 (listRunnerDeviceKeys), apps/web/src/lib/api-types.ts:773 (RunnerDeviceKeyListItem, RunnerDeviceKeyEspecie), apps/web/src/locales/{en,pt-BR}/terminal.json (agenteDeMaquina.*)
    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/agente-de-maquina.test.ts — the happy path (active and used, with the MOST RECENT use among N) and the seven states without collapse, including the failure WITHOUT a status and the PROJECT key that does NOT count; apps/web/src/components/RunnerOnboardingPanel.test.tsx — the panel announcing the pairing with the service gesture and the ADR 0118 flow collapsed and still reachable, the two caveats ("it is not a running agent", "it belongs to your ACCOUNT") plus the reach one, the REVOKED key going back to telling to pair, the failed query saying it does not know, the role below developer without calling the route, and the wizard without a projectId with no one to ask
    • Gap DECLARED, CLOSED later by RN-552: when this RN closed, nobody CREATED a machine key (it was install.sh, ADR 0155 point 4, session 6), so this screen was only exercisable with a key registered by hand — the same half RN-543 and RN-544 already declared. The command the panel offers is brabo-runner service status --project <id>, the form that exists TODAY; the per-machine form arrives with session 4's unit — CLOSED later (AT-106): once the MACHINE key is recognized, the panel offers brabo-runner service status --machine (the machine unit of RN-545), and the PROJECT key block keeps offering --project <id>; both active kinds each show their own. The command is still for the person to ASK — the screen does not assert the answer (keys agenteDeMaquina.comandoDeServico and chaveDeProjeto.comandoDeServico, tests in RunnerOnboardingPanel.test.tsx in both branches and in the pair). An active PROJECT key does NOT change the panel: it is the sibling defect, one scope below, and closing it is a separate decision — CLOSED later (AT-107): the panel recognizes an active PROJECT key of THIS project (reconhecerChaveDeProjeto, lib/agente-de-maquina.ts, same listing and same queryKey, no new route), with the same vocabulary — accent tone, a registered key is not an agent up, the trigger is checking the service — and the cost of the KIND stated: revoking takes down the agent IN THIS project, not in all of them. Another project's key and a revoked one do not count, and the ADR 0118 <details> stays reachable. And the web still has no SCREEN on which to list or revoke a device key (the open half of RN-519) — this delivery consumes the listing and does not build it. CLOSED later by RN-561, which builds it: the Settings section reuses podeLerChavesDeDispositivo from here and the SAME queryKey, and that is why revoking from there invalidates the recognition of this panel
    • ADR: 0154
    • Origin: PHASE 30, session 7 — the phase's scope

    Mode conversion stops being a leap in the dark (RN-559, RN-560)

    RN-559 — Converting to mounted opens the SAME folder browser as the wizard; the runner branch stays typed, and the screen says why

    ExecutionModeSection was the only one of the five places in the product where one chooses a folder and the path was typed in the dark — the other four already opened FolderBrowserModal since RN-473/RN-504. The gap was declared in CLAUDE.md and the declared reason belonged to the other branch: onboarding a runner BEFORE the conversion saves registers a key in a project that is not yet runner, and ConfirmProjectWorkspaceUseCase refuses with 400. None of that holds for mounted, and that is what this rule closes.

    Same component, same transport, no new rule. The mounted branch mounts FolderBrowserModal with origem: { tipo: 'api', workspaceId } — the same discriminated union NewProjectWizard uses for that mode (RN-533), because the folder lives inside the SERVER's base (ADR 0141) and it is the server that sees it. No new browser nor new endpoint is born, and the browser does not guarantee a valid path: what validates is still validarExecutionModeEWorkspacePath and the database CHECK.

    The runner branch does NOT get a browser, and that is a decision, not forgetfulness. Its transport ({ tipo: 'runner', projectId }) requires a runner connected to THIS project, and a project that is not yet runner has none — the wait would end in an error that looks like a bug. The order "convert, then onboard" remains a separate product decision. What changes is the screen now saying so in text instead of only not offering any button, which is the rule of ADR 0064: the control is removed, never the information.

    The base is a FOUR-valued state, and they do not collapse. The route GET workspaces/:workspaceId/projects-base (RN-500) may be in flight, have failed, have answered null or have answered a path — and each of those has its OWN text, by the rule of RN-088/RN-468: "I don't know" never becomes "it doesn't have". The button stays on the screen grayed out in the first three, with the reason stated in TEXT right below, and not by title (which on a disabled element does not open in Chromium).

    base state the button what the screen says
    in flight grayed out "querying this installation's projects base"
    the query failed grayed out it was not possible to KNOW whether it exists; reload
    answered null grayed out this installation did not declare a base
    answered a path live the base, NAMED, and that it is in it that the browser opens

    The mounted mode does not leave the selector when the base is missing, and that is the deliberate difference from the wizard (RN-513): a project that IS ALREADY mounted cannot see its own mode disappear from the control because of a query in flight. What disappears is the BROWSER, not the conversion.

    The minimum is the ENDPOINT's. PUT projects/:projectId/execution-mode and GET workspaces/:workspaceId/projects-base both require maintainer, read via roleAtLeast over ROLE_ORDER and never via a hand-written list of roles (RN-102) — the section did role === 'owner' || role === 'maintainer', which is right by accident while the minimum is high. Whoever does not reach the minimum does not fire the base query: it would end in a certain 403, and the "I don't know" it would produce would be about authorization, not about the base.

    The value chosen in the browser is treated as a TYPED value: it fills in the field and only goes to the api when someone confirms with the section's button. This section does not become autosave (RN-469).

    • Code: apps/web/src/routes/settings/ExecutionModeSection.tsx:68 (the four states of the base, as a union), :211 (the minimum via roleAtLeast), :220 (the query that does not go out for whoever does not reach maintainer), :260 (the derivation of the four), :280 (what the browser requires to exist), :414 (the button, grayed out and not hidden), :423 (the text of the runner branch), :475 (the FolderBrowserModal with origem: { tipo: 'api', workspaceId }); apps/web/src/locales/pt-BR/settings.json:231 and apps/web/src/locales/en/settings.json:231 (executionMode.path.*, the five texts in both languages)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/conversao-de-modo.test.tsx — the happy path (open the browser, choose loja, the field fills in and the conversion sends what was CHOSEN, only after the button), the base query FAILING (inert button, own text, the modal not mounting even by force, and the field remaining editable), the ABSENT base with text DIFFERENT from the failure one and the loading one, the runner branch without a button and with the reason in text, and the role below maintainer (controls grayed out, current value still visible, base query not fired)
    • Gap DECLARED: the runner branch of this section remains typed in the dark — the CLAUDE.md gap shrank to it and did not close. Closing it requires deciding the order "convert, then onboard", which is a separate product decision and remains without an owner. Update: the order was decided and measured in RN-612 — the runner branch gets onboarding AFTER saving, and the browser stays out for another reason, stated there
    • ADR: 0111, 0141
    • Origin: AT-049 (EP-021/HS-033)

    RN-560 — The conversion warning says what it refuses, what it carries and what it does NOT carry — naming the old path

    The section's fixed warning asserted "this migrates the agent's working folder", in both languages. ConvertProjectExecutionModeUseCase does not have a single line that copies or moves folder content: it moves permissions.json (the POLICY, with allow/deny/ask intact — RN-448), zeroes workspaceVerifiedAt (RN-450) and mirrorPath (RN-515), deprovisions the container when LEAVING container (RN-449) and writes the new workspacePath. materializarWorkspaceMontado only CREATES the new folder (RN-501). Whatever is in the old folder — uncommitted work included — stays there, orphaned.

    The orphan was already a declared gap in CLAUDE.md since RN-447..450. What this rule closes is not the orphan: it is the screen asserting the OPPOSITE of what the server does. This is an assertion fix, not a feature — migrating content between modes stays out, without an owner.

    Three facts, and not a paragraph, because they answer different questions:

    1. what the conversion REFUSES — a dev agent working or stuck right now becomes a 409, and the api's sentence is the one that shows up in the toast (the text that already existed and still holds);
    2. what it CARRIES — the project's policy changes scope with its content intact;
    3. what it does NOT carry — the folder's content, uncommitted work included, naming the old path.

    The warning NAMES the path because "stays on the old disk" without saying WHICH disk tells the person to go searching. The value is one line away (project.workspacePath) and disappears from the screen the instant the conversion saves. When there is no path to name — the project is container, and the old folder is a SERVER volume — the sentence is a DIFFERENT one, pointing to the managed folder: a variant with an empty {{caminho}} would say "whatever is in ", which is the screen refusing to name what it knows. It is the case in which the person has the LEAST means to guess.

    It does not promise detection. Asking the disk "is there uncommitted work?" is per-mode I/O and impossible to answer for runner from the api side: the folder lives on a machine the server does not see. It is not measured, it is not asserted (ADRs 0041/0042). And it blocks nothing: the conversion remains the same call, with the same refusals.

    The Alert's tone went from accent to warning, because the third fact is a LOSS of reach and not neutral information. It stays without role="alert": it is text that was already on the screen when it opened, and an assertive live region there would become an interruption without cause.

    • Code: apps/web/src/routes/settings/ExecutionModeSection.tsx:333 (the three facts, one per line), :337 (the variant that names the old path × the managed folder one); apps/web/src/locales/pt-BR/settings.json:220 and apps/web/src/locales/en/settings.json:220 (executionMode.warning.*, the four texts in both languages — en is the app's default language (RN-425), and an honest warning only in Portuguese is the same lie with an accent)

    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/conversao-de-modo.test.tsx — the happy path (the warning containing the old path AND the words "uncommitted work"), the variant without an old path (a container project pointing to the managed folder, and the variant with a path NOT rendered), and the negative proof in BOTH language bundles: the migration promise survives in neither, and the four keys exist in both

    • The CONDITIONAL consequences (AT-144). The question this rule left open — list ALL consequences or only the one that misled — was decided by the maintainer: only those that hold for THIS project. The warning adds, after the three facts, one line per consequence that applies, and NONE for what does not apply:

      line when it shows up the same condition as the use case
      the mirror destination is zeroed, NAMED mirrorPath filled in every conversion zeroes it (RN-515)
      the registered container is stopped and removed the project is container TODAY and the lifecycle exists outside removed removerContainerSeExistir only runs when LEAVING container (RN-449)
      "I could not read whether there is a container" the project is container and the lifecycle read FAILED — ("I don't know" never becomes "it doesn't have", RN-468)
      the folder confirmed on <data> stops being valid workspaceVerifiedAt filled in every conversion zeroes it (RN-450)

      The container line does not show up in mounted/runner even with a registered container, because the use case does not remove it there — the warning would say the opposite of the server, which is the defect this rule exists to close. The data are those the section already has (Project: mirrorPath, workspaceVerifiedAt, executionMode) plus the container lifecycle, which the section did NOT load: it comes from the route that ALREADY exists, GET projects/:projectId/container/lifecycle (viewer), queried only when the project is container — no new endpoint. While the read is in flight, the line simply does not show up.

    • Origin: AT-050 (EP-021/HS-034); the conditionals, AT-144 (EP-021/HS-034)

    The device key gets a screen, and the screen states the reach of revoking (RN-561)

    RN-561 — The Settings tab lists and revokes device keys, marking the KIND — because revoking the MACHINE one takes down the local agent in ALL the owner's projects

    The missing half of RN-519, declared in writing ever since in CLAUDE.md: "what remains open is only the SCREEN: apps/web has nowhere to list nor revoke, so whoever wants to revoke calls the route". Both routes exist — the DELETE since ADR 0118 and the GET since RN-519 —, and no screen used them. This RN is the screen, and it opens no new route: what the listing already returns is enough to draw everything that follows.

    Where it lives, and why PROJECT. It takes the Settings tab from 17 to 18 sections and is the 13th in render order (device-keys, group pessoas, right after access-tokens — the neighbor that does the same with PATs); the registry's order is the SAME as the barrel's, because it is what the scroll-spy uses. Project because the endpoint is /projects/:projectId/runner-device-keys: an ACCOUNT screen would require a new route, and the scope is about the one that exists.

    The KIND shows up, and it is not decoration — it is the reach of the screen's only irreversible action. Since RN-543 the listing marks projeto | maquina, and a MACHINE key (null project_id) serves all the owner's projects and that is why it shows up in the list of ALL of them. Showing it without the mark would make the screen assert it is "this project's key", and whoever clicked revoke would think it takes down the local agent here when it takes it down in all of them — the screen would lie precisely about what can least be wrong. The mark is warning for the machine one and muted for the project one, and never success: green would read as "it's up", which is the arithmetic RN-548 already refused.

    The confirmation DEPENDS on the kind, and also states the collateral cost. Revoking opens a dialog with THREE sentences, and the middle one is the one that changes: the machine one names all your projects in runner mode (the loop of RevokeRunnerDeviceKeyUseCase over listRunnerModeReachableBy), the project one names its project. A single text for both would lie in one of the halves. The third sentence is the cost RN-520 declared and this delivery does not change: the disconnection target is {projeto, usuário} and never {chave}, so another runner of the same user on the same project also drops — even authenticated by a PAT or another key — and reconnects on its own if its credential is still valid.

    Recorded use is NOT an agent up, and the vocabulary is the SAME as RN-548. lastUsedAt is the data class of workspaceVerifiedAt (RN-468): the record of a confirmation, never a heartbeat. The caveat is stated in TEXT in the caption, and the status column talks about REVOCATION ("active"/"revoked", a fact about the row), never about connection — what knows about NOW is still the terminal's channel. Inventing a second vocabulary for the same distinction would be the screen saying the same thing in two ways, and one of the two would age.

    Five states, and none becomes the other (RN-088/RN-470). No key ("No device key of yours serves this project"), a REVOKED key (which stays in the list by RN-519's decision — making the row disappear would make the screen assert it never existed, and it gets no button because revoking again, even if idempotent in the api, would suggest an effect that did not remain), NULL lastUsedAt (the ORPHAN key of RN-473: registered by a tab that closed in the middle of the flow and never used by any runner — its own text, and an explanation below the table that only shows up when there is one, because explaining what does not occur is noise), "not loaded yet" and "I could not read". "I don't know" never becomes "it doesn't have".

    The role is the ENDPOINT's, and it is the project's EFFECTIVE one. The minimum is developer — what RunnerDeviceKeysController requires on the THREE routes (RN-102) — and the comparison comes from the SAME function RN-548 created (podeLerChavesDeDispositivo, over roleAtLeast), never a second rule and never a hand-written role === 'x' || role === 'y'. What changes in relation to RN-548 is the INPUT: there the panel read the WORKSPACE role and declared the gap, because it mounts in places that do not fetch project_members; here the data is at hand — the section lives in the same tab as MembersSection, with the SAME queryKey, and react-query deduplicates —, so the role is composed as in the use case (projectRole ?? workspaceRole, an OVERLAY in both directions, RN-471) and the gap closes instead of repeating itself. An ABSENT role is not an INSUFFICIENT role: while the member list is in flight the screen says it is verifying, and does not accuse the reader of not reaching developer. Whoever does not reach it still sees the whole section, what a device key is and what revoking the machine one costs — the CONTROL disappears, never the INFORMATION (ADR 0064) —, and the reason is stated ONCE in TEXT (title on a disabled element does not open in Chromium). Since the three routes require the SAME role, whoever does not reach it also does not see the LIST: the screen stops asking what the api would deny, instead of turning a predictable 403 into "I could not read". This is not a security boundary — what refuses is the RolesGuard.

    One at a time, and that is why there is no batch outcome. The screen does not offer "revoke all", so the rule of RN-469 — a UI action that becomes N calls is not a transaction, and the screen says so — does not apply: offering the batch would create a partial outcome to narrate for an action nobody asked for. A failure in revocation shows the api's OWN sentence and the row stays in the list.

    • Code: apps/web/src/routes/settings/RunnerDeviceKeysSection.tsx:76 (the whole section; the effective role at :107, the query at :130, the confirmation by kind at :326); apps/web/src/routes/settings/sumario.ts:62 (the new entry, 13th in render order, of the 18); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSettingsTab.tsx:86 (the composition, in the SAME order); apps/web/src/locales/{en,pt-BR}/settings.json (runnerDeviceKeys.*). It consumes listRunnerDeviceKeys/ revokeRunnerDeviceKey (apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:431) and podeLerChavesDeDispositivo (apps/web/src/lib/agente-de-maquina.ts:68) without changing either of them
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/settings/chaves-de-dispositivo.test.tsx — the two kinds marked in the same list; the revoked one that stays, marked and without a button; the orphan with its own text and the explanation that does NOT show up without it; empty and failure with different texts; the caveat that recorded use is not an agent up; the reach warning in BOTH kinds, with the call going out only after confirming; cancel without revoking; the failure bringing the api's sentence with the key still in the list; viewer without calling the route and with the reason in text; the project role overlay in BOTH directions; and the absent role that says "verifying" instead of accusing
    • Declared and NOT closed: (1) the revocation target remains {projeto, usuário} and never {chave} — the screen SAYS so and does not change it, because changing it requires a new column in runner_socket_tickets and a new auth contract (RN-520), its own front with an ADR; (2) in an installation without a PROJECT, the machine key freshly created by RN-552 remains unreachable: both routes are project ones, and without a project there is no one to ask — CLOSED in RN-611, with a route and section per ACCOUNT; (3) the maintainer view (list/revoke any user's) stays OUT by RN-519's decision, not by this screen's omission; (4) no browser E2E — what is proven here is what the screen ASSERTS, and that the component suite proves
    • Origin: AT-012 (EP-003/HS-007)

    RN-568 — The presence of QA, SecOps and the area members in the team panel is decided by the WHOLE session, not by the 200-event window

    The Overview and the Executors tab build the roster with deriveAgentRoster over the session's LAST 200 events (useSessionEvents, latest: true). Two of the presence facts came from that window: gatesEverOpened (qa and secops come in when some pr.gate_changed/infra.gate_changed has already happened) and delegatedSubagents (an area member comes in with a recorded delegation). Both events are among the first of an execution — in a long session they leave the window, and QA, SecOps and qa-automacao vanished from the panel of an execution that kept running. It is the same class of defect that had already been closed for executionActivated, and the workspace summary (RN-090) already aggregated both facts over the whole session (bool_or and selectDistinct in projects-summary.repository.ts) — both screens loaded it and ignored those two fields.

    The rule: deriveAgentRoster/rosterFactsFromEvents accept an OPTIONAL agregado (AgregadoDaSessao), and both screens fill it with the summary. Four decisions, each with a reason:

    1. Absent is "I did not read", never "there was none". Without a loaded summary (first paint, error), the fact comes only from the window, exactly as before. An absent read never becomes "QA is not here".
    2. The aggregate ADDS to the window, it does not replace it (logical OR on the gate, union on the delegations). Both facts are MONOTONIC within a session — a gate that opened does not un-open, a recorded delegation does not disappear —, so the window only produces false NEGATIVES, never false positives. An aggregated false that disagrees with a gate SEEN in the window is a late summary (the two reads have their own poll cadences), and cannot hide QA.
    3. It only holds when the summary is of the SAME session the screen reads (latestSessionId === sessionId). The summary aggregates the project's most RECENT session; the Overview reads that same session, but the Executors tab reads the current EXECUTION session (RN-139). An ideation opened afterwards makes the two diverge, and then the aggregate would be of ANOTHER session — the screen goes back to deciding only by the window.
    4. Only PRESENCE changes. rosterFromFacts (the single rule, shared with the dashboard card) stays intact, and each agent's STATUS (gateStatus, subagentStatus) is still read from the window. No new request and the 200 ceiling does not change.

    delegatedSubagents came in through the SAME mold because it fits without changing a rule: the delegations table the summary reads and the delegation.* events the window reads are born together in RecordDelegationUseCase (one row, one event, the three outcomes), so the two sets describe the same thing.

    executionActivated (AT-130). It is the third fact of the same mold: the Executors tab read it from the summary WITHOUT the guard of item 3, so with a session newer than the execution one the summary (of it) said false and the dev agents vanished — or true, and they appeared without proof. Now it goes in agregado.executionActivated only when latestSessionId === sessionId, and deriveAgentRoster ADDS it (logical OR, monotonic) to the window and to the parameter, which also starts looking at execution.activated in the events. No new field in the API. Tests: ProjectExecutorsTab.test.tsx (summary of ANOTHER session, in both directions). Still open: with a long execution session AND the summary of another session, the event may have left the window and the tab goes back to deciding only by it — it is the cost of the guard, the same as for the other two facts.

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts:324 (AgregadoDaSessao),

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts:324 (AgregadoDaSessao), :296 (the optional parameter of rosterFactsFromEvents), :304 (the union of the delegations), :312 (the gate's OR), :410 (the parameter passed on by deriveAgentRoster); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.tsx:97 and apps/web/src/routes/ProjectExecutorsTab.tsx:111 (the aggregate with the session guard)

    • Test: apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.test.ts:628 (the whole block: :633 a gate outside the window brings QA/SecOps with status from the window, :658 without an aggregate the window decides alone, :676 an aggregated false does not hide a gate seen in the window); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectExecutorsTab.test.tsx:385 (QA and area member come back through the summary), :397 (summary of another session does not decide); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.test.tsx:301 (SecOps comes back through the summary)

    • Origin: AT-047 — the gap declared in CLAUDE.md and in the comments of the two screens

    RN-572 — The team tree translates every dev.* type the engine emits, and only states work in progress where the panel says trabalhando

    In a real installation (09/14) the dev agents "did not get past started the task". The event log had dev.started followed by dev.blocked_by_container, with the reason spelled out (RN-502). The team tree (timeline-tree.ts) had no translation for the block — what is not in TRADUCAO is discarded —, so the branch stopped at the previous milestone and the present sentence asserted work about a stopped agent. The team panel, on the same execution screen, already said aguardando for the same event (DEV_STATUS_EVENTS, RN-411): two screens, two tables, and only one of them was covered by the comparison with the engine's vocabulary.

    The rule, in three parts:

    1. Every dev.* type the engine emits has a decision in the tree — translated in TRADUCAO or declared in TRADUCAO_FORA with the reason (empty today). Omitting stopped being possible: scripts/ci/vocabulario-de-eventos-dev.spec.ts derives the engine's vocabulary through the same extractor as the event inventory and fails, naming the type, whatever is missing; and it also fails a translated dev.* type the engine does not emit. On the first round it found TWO missing (dev.blocked_by_container and dev.error) and ONE extra (dev.rearmed, which the api writes with actor user and which is why it never reached any branch — the tree only hangs milestones of actor agent).
    2. Whether a dev agent's last milestone is work in progress is not decided in the tree. For a dev.* milestone, the branch only stays ACTIVE when the panel gives trabalhando to the same event (statusDoEventoDev, read from DEV_STATUS_EVENTS). aguardando, ocioso, falhou and travado leave the branch stopped — and with them dev.idle, dev.blocked, dev.awaiting_gate, dev.awaiting_approval and dev.idle_tripped, which before also counted as active. A dev.* type the panel did not decide does NOT become active: when in doubt the screen does not assert work.
    3. The container block is its own milestone (espera, color warning, the same as the gate), with the engine's reason as detail — it is what says what to do. And dev.started stops saying "started the task": measured in the engine, it goes out on RECEIVING the order to work, before try_claim (DevAgentServer.handle_cast(:work)); it now says "looking for a task", and what asserts the task is dev.working, which now shows its title.

    What this rule does NOT do, and why:

    • container.start_failed does not enter the tree. It is the reason for the block, but the actor is system (action-executor): hanging it on a dev agent's branch would be inventing authorship, and the only other place in the structure, the tronco, IS NOT RENDERED by any consumer (measured: AgentTimelineTree and the Activities section of the Shell only read ramos). The event stays in the chronological log, one click away, and the blocked branch already says, through the reason, that the container needs to be started.

    • The tree's labels stayed in fixed pt-BR in this fix — migrating them was a change to the whole screen. AT-134 did that migration later, and it did NOT touch the rule above: the DECISION per type (whether it becomes a node, which MarcoTipo, which detail) stays in TRADUCAO, in the code, and it is what the spec compares with the engine; only the TEXT left, as a KEY (ChaveDeRotulo) resolved in executors:timelineTree.label.*, with the present sentence in timelineTree.now.* and the origin detail in timelineTree.detail.origin, in both languages (en is the default, RN-425). montarArvore receives the language as an argument (getFixedT), and the two consumers (AgentTimelineTree and the Activities section of the Shell) pass it from useTranslation — switching language rebuilds the tree. A key the table uses without text in one of the two locales fails in timeline-tree.test.ts.

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/timeline-tree.ts:161 (TRADUCAO), :205 (dev.started), :206 (dev.working with the title), :222 (dev.blocked_by_container), :227 (dev.error), :246 (TRADUCAO_FORA), :307 (the ativo read from the panel), :96 (ChaveDeRotulo, AT-134), :135 (tradutorDaArvore); apps/web/src/locales/{en,pt-BR}/executors.json (timelineTree.label, timelineTree.now, timelineTree.detail); apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts:134 (statusDoEventoDev); apps/web/src/components/AgentTimelineTree.module.css (.espera)

    • Test: scripts/ci/vocabulario-de-eventos-dev.spec.ts:300 (the tree block: :308 every engine type decided, :324 nothing translated that the engine does not emit, :342 nothing on both sides); apps/web/src/lib/timeline-tree.test.ts:130 (the measured sequence: dev.started + dev.blocked_by_container leaves the branch stopped with the reason — failure case), :149 (dev.started does not assert a task), :156 (states the panel does not call work leave the branch stopped; dev.working stays active with the title), :168 (dev.error ends with the reason), :367 (AT-134: every key used has text in both locales, the subtrees have the same keys, and the language passed beats the active one)

    • Origin: AT-087 — real installation of 09/14; the labels' i18n, AT-134

    The repository is born on accepting the handoff to the Arquiteto, and execution does not start without it (RN-582)

    RN-600 — Project creation through the screen does not send the body the api is already known to refuse, one creation at a time, and the refusal shows up on the screen

    In the owner's test on 09/26 the api logged 11 POST /workspaces/:id/projects → 400 in ~4 s, and the same runner project, created via the api with a path, came out 201. Measured by reading and reproduced in the wizard's vitest: there is no automatic retry (createProject is called directly, without useMutation), so each POST was a CLICK. The probable cause is the runner mode's "Browse folder...", which creates the project EARLY (RN-437): in the details step the path field is ABOVE the name, and clicking before typing the name sent { name: '', slug: '', executionMode: 'runner', workspacePath: '/workspace-a-confirmar' } — CreateProjectDto refuses a name with fewer than 2 characters and a slug outside kebab-case —, and the failure became only the toast "Could not prepare folder browsing right now", with no reason; clicking again was the only answer the screen suggested. A ONE-letter name passed Continue and came back 400 at "Provision" for the same MinLength(2).

    Three halves, all on the screen (the api is right to refuse):

    1. Do not send what is already known to be refused. nomeAceitoPelaApi mirrors the DTO (2+ characters, trimmed, and a non-empty slug) and becomes the name gate of the details step. In runner mode, "Browse folder..." stays INERT while the name does not pass, or while a TYPED path is not plausible (caminhoLocalParecePlausivel), and the reason is stated in text below the field (procurar-bloqueado, ADR 0064). An EMPTY path still does not block — the provisional placeholder of RN-437 covers it. It is the revision of RN-437's sentence "never blocking the click", which talked about the PATH and did not foresee the empty name. The name goes trimmed in the body.
    2. One creation at a time. criacaoEmVoo (a useRef) locks BOTH paths that create — "Browse folder..." and "Provision" —, because the disabled only reaches the button on the next render. In jsdom the disabled already holds the second click; the ref is what holds two events in the same browser frame.
    3. The refusal on the screen. The 400 failure of the early creation now puts the api's sentence (mensagemDaApi, the class-validator list joined) in the SAME step Alert that "Provision" already used (RN-170); other errors stay with only the toast.

    What was not measured: the api log of the owner's test did not survive the container restart, so the BODY of each of the 11 POSTs was not seen — the cause above is the only one that reading the code and vitest reproduce, and it does not prove it was it. The adjacency (RN-437) stays as it was: the project created early with the provisional path does not receive the refined path later at "Provision" (it is REUSED, not updated) — what fixes it is the runner when connecting (RN-423).

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/wizard.ts:108 (nomeAceitoPelaApi); apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.tsx:112 (montarPayloadDeCriacao), :202 (criacaoEmVoo), :321 (bloqueioDoProcurarNoRunner), :486 (handleProcurarPasta), :536 (handleConfirm)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/NewProjectWizard.test.tsx:850 (describe criar projeto sem rajada de 400 — without a name, 11 clicks and no POST; relative path; one-letter name; trimmed name; three clicks in the same frame create one; the api's refusal on the screen); apps/web/src/lib/wizard.test.ts:210 (nomeAceitoPelaApi)
    • Origin: AT-215 (the owner's test, 09/26), sibling of AT-214

    Mode conversion stops being a leap in the dark (RN-559, RN-560)

    RN-612 — Converting to runner is convert, THEN onboard: saving mounts the onboarding panel of the now runner project, and the folder browser stays out by construction

    The runner branch of the conversion was typed in the dark (RN-559) because the order was undecided: onboarding BEFORE saving registers a key in a project that is not yet runner, and ConfirmProjectWorkspaceUseCase refuses the confirmation with 400. The maintainer's decision was to measure the other order with the MACHINE key (RN-543/RN-548) and implement it if the friction were acceptable. The measurement was done in the CODE (without bringing up the environment) and gave this:

    who will serve the project what happens after saving gestures
    MACHINE agent waiting (zero runner projects, RN-550) the next query of GET /runner/projects lists it and the agent connects on its own — 15/30/60 s cadence of espera-de-projetos.ts zero, within 60 s
    MACHINE agent already serving ANOTHER project the list is read ONCE, at start, with a live connection (RN-550) — the new project does not come in one: restart the service
    no machine key the same pairing as project creation (RN-473) pair + one command

    In all three cases the friction is that of project creation or less, so the conversion becomes two steps in the same section: saving to runner mounts, right below, the SAME RunnerOnboardingPanel as the Code tab and the wizard, with the projectId of a project that now IS runner — recognition of the paired machine, EsperaDoRunner and the ADR 0118 pairing come along, with no new rule. Above the panel the section SAYS the machine agent's asymmetry (waits on its own × restart), with the restart command for both platforms (systemctl --user restart brabo-runner.service, launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/dev.brabo.runner): the screen does not know whether the agent has another live connection, so it names both cases instead of asserting one.

    The panel shows up when the SAVED project is runner, with no pending mode change in the selector, and with the folder not yet confirmed — or until the end of the visit in which the conversion saved, so EsperaDoRunner shows the confirmation instead of disappearing the instant it arrives. An already confirmed runner project does not get it.

    The typed path is PROVISIONAL, and the screen says so. What decides the folder of a runner project is the runner: the confirmation OVERWRITES workspacePath (RN-423), and the machine agent does not even look at the typed value — it uses <base>/<workspaceDirName> (RN-544). The field stays mandatory because the database CHECK requires a non-null path outside container; the section does not invent a placeholder.

    The folder browser stays OUT of the runner branch, and now for the right reason. It is no longer the order: it is that the transport { tipo: 'runner', projectId } requires a runner connected to THIS project, and a connected runner has already confirmed its OWN folder (--dir, or the one derived from the base). Choosing another there would be a second runner → runner conversion the next reconnection would undo. Offering the button would be a control that controls nothing; the section says so in text (ADR 0064).

    • Code: apps/web/src/routes/settings/ExecutionModeSection.tsx:300 (mostrarProximoPasso), :313 (setConvertidoParaRunner, the visit in which the conversion saved), :452 (the next-step block), :483 (RunnerOnboardingPanel, the same as the Code tab); apps/web/src/locales/pt-BR/settings.json:249 and apps/web/src/locales/en/settings.json:249 (executionMode.nextStep.*) and :241 (executionMode.path.runnerTyped, rewritten); the cadence measured in apps/runner/src/espera-de-projetos.ts:60 (CADENCIA_DA_ESPERA_MS) and the list read once in apps/runner/src/index.ts (rodarComoAgenteDeMaquina)
    • Test: the RN-612 describe in apps/web/src/routes/settings/conversao-de-modo.test.tsx — the happy path (before saving there is no panel; saving mounts the next step, with the restart command, and the panel asks for THIS project's keys), the machine key recognized, the failure case (an already confirmed runner project does not get the step), a draft of another mode hiding the step, and the text in en
    • Gap DECLARED: restarting the machine agent that already serves another project remains a gesture in the terminal — re-querying the list with a live connection was refused by RN-550, and this rule does not reopen that. And the measurement is of CODE, not of screen: the environment was not brought up
    • ADR: 0111, 0154
    • Origin: AT-143 (EP-021/HS-033)

    The repository is born on accepting the handoff to the Arquiteto, and execution does not start without it (RN-582)

    RN-614 — The screen shows the policy's reason and the scope's root, in a single sentence, in both places

    RN-567 put in proposed_action.created the rule that decided (reason) and RN-609 put the scope's root (scopeRoot, only in terminal) — and both declared the screen did not change. Whoever audited through the screen an action that PASSED still did not know which rule let it through.

    The rule: the data shows up in TWO places (the maintainer's decision), and both say the SAME sentence, assembled by ONE function, fraseDaDecisaoDaPolitica:

    • the event's line in the log panel — classifyEvent gains the branch for proposed_action.created (type permission, bad when denied), with the policy's outcome and, after the dash, the sentence;
    • the ApprovalCard, on its own line right below the sentence of what happens (RN-096) — never inside the collapsed payload.

    The action (proposed_actions) keeps neither of the two fields: the screen reads them from the EVENT, which it already loads (decisaoDaPoliticaDaAcao, by actionId). That is why the card has THREE states, which do not collapse: a screen that does not read the event log (the "needs you" panel, PRs, containers) does not pass the data and the card goes SILENT; a screen that reads (the session chat and the Approvals tab's queue, both over the most recent session) and did not find the event in the loaded window says "outside the events loaded on this screen"; and the found event gives the sentence. The Insights tab has no ApprovalCard — a Psicólogo hypothesis is not a proposed_action.

    Since AT-336 (RN-656), each card of the Approvals tab has its OWN reason, read through the action when the loaded log does not cover it; the next paragraph is the state of AT-333, which it replaced — the single note remains only for the read that FAILS. Since AT-340 the same holds for the cards of pending items from OTHER sessions in the chat (RN-626), which before went silent: the thread's log never covers another session's action.

    In AT-333, the Approvals tab's queue started saying the gap ONCE. The queue is the PROJECT's (RN-638) and the events are those of ONE session, so there "outside the loaded events" came out on almost every card — a truth repeated per card, the tool explaining itself. The api does not expose the reason per action (the action does not keep it, and the events read does not filter by actionId: measured), and fetching without a window would require a new endpoint. The tab then starts COUNTING the pending ones without the event and saying, in a note above the queue, how many there are and why ("of these N actions", or "of N of the M actions below"), in the mold of RN-180; those cards go silent (the "screen that does not read" state), and the card whose event is loaded keeps showing the sentence. The note only counts with the log in hand: before the events arrive it asserts nothing. The session chat keeps the three states per card — there the cut is the thread's own.

    Absent is not "no reason", and the absolute never shows up. An event prior to RN-567 says "policy reason not recorded"; a terminal prior to RN-609 says "scope root not recorded"; another type, without scopeRoot, does not talk about a root (the scope is only consulted in terminal). The four anchors have their own text — managed folder, segment inside the projects base, folder name on the runner's machine, and indisponivel ("no relative form", without a segment). An anchor the screen does not know says it does not know, and does not print the segment: the screen rebuilds no path at all. The sentences are in en and pt-BR (approvals.json, key politica).

    • Code: apps/web/src/lib/decisao-da-politica.ts:111 (fraseDaDecisaoDaPolitica), :65 (lerDecisaoDaPolitica), :124 (decisaoDaPoliticaDaAcao), :145 (linhaDoEventoDePolitica); apps/web/src/lib/activity.ts:721 (the branch for proposed_action.created); apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.tsx:272 (the card's line); apps/web/src/routes/session-timeline-montagem.tsx:825 and apps/web/src/routes/ProjectApprovalsTab.tsx:307 (decisoesDaPolitica, since AT-336 through useDecisoesDaPolitica) and :499 (who passes the data)

    The language of the agents' answers (RN-618..620, RN-622..624, ADR 0177)

    RN-620 — The session bar shows the answers language of WHOEVER IS VIEWING, with the origin, and changing it there is the override only for that person in that session

    1. Next to the model selector, in another scope. The bar's model belongs to the SESSION and holds for everyone; the language belongs to the PERSON (GET .../sessions/:sessionId/response-language, resolved for the caller, RN-618). Changing it there is the session override — the maintainer's decision, AT-169 answer 2 — and never touches another participant's nor the Account.
    2. Never the value without the origin (the rule of RN-470): "set by you in this session", "chosen by you in the Account", "detected from your messages and confirmed by you" and "the interface language" are four texts, and none collapses into another. Loading and failed also have their own text — the failure does not pretend a language. The detected one never becomes an assertion about the person ("you are Brazilian"), only about the messages (AT-080).
    3. "Follow the Account" releases the override (language: null) and says in WHICH language that results — the same chain without the session step, derived on the client from the chain the route already returns, without a new endpoint. "Another code…" opens a field that only sets on the button (RN-469), and the api's refusal arrives in the toast with its sentence. A link leads to the Account, where the choice that holds in every session lives.
    4. Who changes it: the PUT's minimum (developer), via roleAtLeast over the WORKSPACE role (the declared gap of the screens that do not fetch project_members). Below it the value and the origin stay on the bar, the selector stays inert and the reason comes in text.
    5. What the bar does NOT assert: never "the agents answer in X" as a fact about the person — only the effective one and the origin. Since RN-622 that effective one reaches the model in the messages of whoever is viewing, and the warning "does not reach the agents yet" left the origin line. The line truncates at the bar's 60px and carries the whole text in the title — the text is on the screen, the title only returns what the ellipsis cut.
    6. On the narrow bar, the language CHANGES place and does not disappear (AT-317). The bar arranges itself by its OWN width (modoDaBarra, measured by ResizeObserver; an unknown width is the full bar): from 1720px everything stays inline; below that model, language and budget become ONE control whose trigger summarizes the model and the language code (below 920px only the icon, with an accessible name), and its panel brings the whole indicator with the origin WRAPPING instead of cutting. The detection question (RN-624) lives in the panel, and while it exists the trigger gains a mark and the accessible name announces it. The session title is the only item that shrinks (with a floor and the whole name in the title), and the buttons do not wrap — "Close" becomes an icon with an accessible name only on the minimal bar.

    • Origin: AT-165, on the decisions of AT-169 (answer 2) and AT-168 (answer 7)

    Deciding in the chat, where the owner is (RN-626)

    RN-626 — The session chat decides what the agents proposed in another session, shows the turn that was in progress and proposes the merge — without changing any ceiling

    The real use of 2026-09-29 measured the cost of the owner talking in one session and the dev agents proposing in another: their approvals never appeared where he was, 45 of 173 clicks on "Sempre permitir" came back 409 (the card was still clickable after the action had already left pending), and reopening the session with a turn in progress gave back a free composer and the 409. The four fixes are SCREEN fixes — the api, the engine and every ceiling stay as they are.

    1. The card goes inert and says the api's sentence (AT-256). ApprovalCard holds the buttons while the decision is in flight (the double click was a certain 409) and, when the call returns a promise that rejects, shows the sentence the api sent ON the card — never a generic toast. A 409 (the action is no longer pending) leaves the buttons inert with the reason in text until the list swaps the card for the outcome line. The caller returns the promise and refetches the list EVEN on refusal (finally): it is ONE invalidation per click, no new poll, and the invalidation uses the channel and budget of RN-579.
    2. Reopening the session resumes the turn from the log (AT-268). The screen's turn state is local useState and was lost on leaving. On the first read of the events of an active session, turnoEmCursoNoLog looks at the most recent persisted agent.status of each agent (the same read as turnoTerminouNoLog, from RN-578); whatever is working reopens the strip and locks the composer through the same entry points as an accepted turn (iniciarTurnoDoAgente + acompanharTurnoPeloLog), and the read of the log tail closes it. It runs ONCE per mounted session (a recurring effect would rearm the strip with the old working still in the cache). The working left by an engine restart is closed by a new event at boot (RN-586), so what remains is a live turn. It does not queue messages: a message with a turn in progress is still refused with a named 409 — the queue is a pending decision of the owner (AT-267).
    3. Pending items of OTHER sessions of the project appear in the chat (AT-265). PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes reads GET /projects/:id/actions?status=pending (the same endpoint as the PRs tab and the "needs you" panel) and draws the ones that were NOT born in this session — this session's are already decided by the thread. It is a SHORTCUT and never a substitute (RN-467): the SAME ApprovalCard, the same endpoints, with the sessionId the action itself carries; onActivateAutoMode stays out (it is policy, not decision). The two queues — approvals and PR merges — have their OWN title and count and are never summed, and each row states the originating session. The drawing has a ceiling of 20 cards, and the excess is stated ("Mostrando N de M"): a declared cut, as in RN-180. The poll is the session's (5 s with the channel down, the 15 s fallback with it live — intervaloDaSessao), because the session channel does not announce another session's writes.
    4. "Mergear" on the card of the open PR (AT-266). Under the card of an executed pr_open, and only when the action.pr_open event carries the PR id and there is no live git_merge (pending, approved or auto-approved) for it, the button PROPOSES the git_merge through the same endpoint as the PRs tab (actor user, in this session). The proposal appears in the thread as the usual card, with Aprovar/Negar: the confirmation is the same and belongs to the human — merge into a protected branch remains unconditional require_approval (RN-418), without auto-approval, without "sempre permitir", without auto mode. The dedupe and the api's refusals (PR already merged, merge already proposed — RN-663) reach the owner as the api's sentence; a pending gate is not a refusal, it is a text notice next to the button.
    5. The role is the ENDPOINT's, and the gap is declared. Deciding an action and proposing an action require developer (actions.controller.ts); the screen uses roleAtLeast over the WORKSPACE role, because the Session does not fetch project_members — the same single gap of RN-471, declared here and not closed. Whoever does not reach it SEES the pending item and the button, with the controls inert and the reason in text (bloqueio of ApprovalCard): what is taken away is the control, never the information.

    What this rule does NOT close, declared: (a) "Mergear" does not propose AND approve in a single click — it is two clicks, the proposal and the confirmation, on purpose; (b) resuming the turn reads the window of events already loaded, and an agent.status outside it is not seen (not knowing is not "is working"); (c) the list of pending items from other sessions has the ceiling of 20 cards, and the rest stays in the Aprovações tab; (d) architecture pending items and the Psicólogo's hypotheses still have NO inline decision (there is no ApprovalCard for them).

    • Code: apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.tsx:228 (decidir, the decision in flight and the refusal on the card); apps/web/src/lib/session-turno.ts:59 (turnoEmCursoNoLog), :532 (useRetomarTurnoDoLog); apps/web/src/lib/pendencias-do-projeto.ts:24 (usePendenciasDoProjeto), :56 (separarPendenciasDeOutrasSessoes); apps/web/src/components/PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes.tsx:65 (PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes); apps/web/src/routes/MergearNoChat.tsx:28 (prAbertaDaAcao), :56 (jaHaMergeDaPr), :89 (MergearNoChat); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:150 (podeDecidir), :272 (useRetomarTurnoDoLog), :767 (PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes)
    • Test: apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.decisao-em-voo.test.tsx:37 (double click), :52 (409 on the card and inert buttons — failure case), :67 (an error that is not 409 gives back the buttons); apps/web/src/lib/turno-em-curso-no-log.test.ts:18 (working), :22 (idle and awaiting_approval close), :38 (no status in the window — failure case); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.retomar-turno.test.tsx:159 (the screen resumes and the log unlocks), :182 (idle does not invent a turn — failure case); apps/web/src/components/PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes.test.tsx:71 (separate queues, the action's sessionId), :99 (role below developer — failure case), :109 (409 on the card), :126 (cut); apps/web/src/routes/MergearNoChat.test.tsx:62 (PR from the event), :75 (dedupe), :100 (proposes as user, without approving), :121 (api refusal — failure case), :134 (role — failure case)
    • Origin: AT-256, AT-268, AT-265 and AT-266, from the analysis of the real use of 2026-09-29 (items 4, 5 and 10a), round 27, lane web-sessao

    RN-631 — The chat recipient is chosen and visible, and the handoff offer is matched by handoffId

    The composer message's recipient was DERIVED: "the most recent agent.activated within the 200-event window" (finding 9-fix). It was a default recipient on the SCREEN side — the defect class that RN-584 closed in the engine — and it was INVISIBLE: the composer did not say who the message went to, and accepting a handoff on this screen changed nothing beyond the log. In the real use of 09/29 the session had handoff.accepted to the Infra and then to the Arquiteto, and the user's "hi" was answered by the Arquiteto without the screen saying so. And the acceptance card matched the offer to the event by the ACTOR/toAgent pair (e.actor.id === offeredHandoff.fromAgent), although handoff.offered has carried handoffId from the start (AT-251).

    The rule:

    1. The OPTIONS are the agents that converse (AGENTES_DE_CHAT) and that are already in the session: agent.activated in the window, roster.activatedAgents from the project summary (RN-630, the WHOLE session, only with the summary of the SAME session, in the mold of RN-568) or an ACCEPTED handoff (the list of handoffs has no window). The last two terms are RN-180: the agent does not vanish from the selector because the activation left the 200 events. In the creative session the Criativo is an option before being activated, because the first message activates it (finding 3).

    2. The recipient is what the person CHOSE — in the composer's "Para" selector, or by accepting a handoff ON THIS screen (the gesture of calling that agent; a failed acceptance changes nothing). Nothing in the log chooses for them: an acceptance made in another tab or through another path only adds an option.

    3. A SINGLE option is the recipient, and the composer names it before sending. It is not a default in the sense of RN-584 — there is no other agent the message could go to.

    4. Two or more options and no valid choice: no recipient. The "Enviar" (and Enter) is locked and the screen says in TEXT that a choice is needed — never "the most recent". Zero options is the session chat without an agent (SSE), and the screen says so too. Revised by RN-682: zero options also locks sending — the consultative session without an agent asks for an agent, and SSE to the raw model is no longer called by the screen.

    5. The choice is remembered per session in localStorage — a convenience for the viewer (without storage the screen just asks for the choice again), never product state.

    6. "Parar" stops the agent OF THE TURN (turnoAgentRef, streamingAgent, statusAgent), and only in its absence the recipient: changing the selector in the middle of another agent's turn must not stop the wrong one. The model shown in the topbar follows the recipient.

    7. The offer is matched by the payload's handoffId, never by the actor: each handoff.offered is the card of its own offer, with no pair to confuse nor a "most recent" to choose. Offers to an already activated agent (window OR roster.activatedAgents) do not become acceptable again.

    8. Every pending offer is actionable, and none hides another (AT-253). The card picked ONE offer (handoffs.find, the oldest pending), and the MANUAL handoff (ADR 0109, RN-441) — recorded with the PERSON as actor, and therefore never matched by the actor — had no "Aceitar" and hid the following offers. Now every pending offer to an agent that converses (except the Infra's, which has its own card, RN-499) has the button on its event; two pending ones to the SAME agent give a single button, the most recent one's, and accepting it removes the other through activeFor. The manual handoff pill says "Handoff manual" instead of the person's id.

    9. "Already in the session" is read from window-LESS sources scoped to the REQUESTED session (review of PR #759). The summary's roster.activatedAgents belongs to the project's MOST RECENT session, and activating execution CREATES a new session that becomes that one: the chat session lost the whole source and went back to depending on the 200 window. The sum now has THREE sources — the summary, only when it is this session's; the handoffs of THIS session (whoever received an accepted one and whoever OFFERED one, who was in it); and the per-agent spend of THIS session (GET .../token-usage, aggregated without a window: an agent with a row there ran a turn, and only an activated agent runs a turn). Each source only fixes false negatives, and spend is proof of PRESENCE, never of absence. Declared gap: an agent activated by a direct start, without a handoff, that never ran a turn, in a session that is no longer the most recent, still depends on the window — and whoever has a role below developer (the minimum of the spend route) lacks the third source. Closing it requires the activation aggregate PER SESSION in the api; it was not done here.

    10. A pending offer whose event left the window gets a button in the fixed strip above the composer (review of PR #759). The actionable card belongs to the handoff.offered, and the list of handoffs has no window: in a long session the offer stayed offered and without a button anywhere. The criterion is the CUT, not absence — the offer is older than the oldest event in the window (a just-created offer whose event has not arrived yet does not flash in the strip), and an empty window does not assert a cut. The strip has only the acceptance; the "Ativar execução" shortcut of the Dev Lead card stays in the thread.

    The guard of RN-584 (scripts/ci/destinos-do-composer.spec.ts) now reads DESTINATARIO_DA_SESSAO_CRIATIVA instead of the literal from SessionPage.tsx: every destination the composer can give still has its own clause in the engine. No api or engine change.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-destinatario.ts:210 (agentesEmConversa), :237 (resolverDestinatario), :272 (useDestinatarioDoChat), :116 (useAtivadosNaSessaoInteira), :141 (ativadosSemJanela); apps/web/src/routes/SessionComposer.tsx:287 (destinatarioRow), :193 (ofertasForaDaJanela); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:350 (aceitarHandoff); apps/web/src/routes/session-timeline-montagem.tsx:547 (handoffIdDoEvento), :555 (origem); apps/web/src/lib/session-handoffs.ts:68 (activeFor), :120 (ofertasAcionaveis), :142 (ofertasForaDaJanela)

    • Origin: AT-251, AT-253

    RN-632 — A PROJECT read polls at the project pace, and every screen that listens to the session channel invalidates through the SAME window

    The real use of 09/29 hit the user's 300 req/min ceiling (RN-579) with two tabs open: 192 × 429 at 07:14, 106 of them on GET /projects/:id/sessions and 20 on GET /projects/:id, and three approve_always clicks refused in the middle (AT-278). RN-579 had put the SESSION screen reads under the channel; the PROJECT ones and the other screens that listen to the channel were left out. The ceiling does NOT change: what drops is the request.

    The rule:

    1. A project read that no channel announces polls at 15s (INTERVALO_DO_PROJETO_MS), UNCONDITIONALLY. The session:<id> channel only announces writes of its own session; the project's session list and the rail counters (approvals of the most recent session, pending merges of the project, stories to promote, architecture pending items, hypotheses) change by writes of ANY session — there is no notice to wait for, so the mechanism is a long poll, in the same fallback that RN-579 already accepts for the session. It applies to useProjectSessions/useLatestSession (they were 5s), to the five counters of the ProjectPage frame and to the architecture of the Executores tab. The tab that shows the data as its SUBJECT (Backlog, Arquitetura, PRs) keeps its own poll, and the fastest observer dictates the key's pace. Whoever writes in this tab still invalidates the key immediately.
    2. Whoever only needs the data on click does not poll. Each row of /containers reads its project's most recent session to PROPOSE stop/remove/start; at a 5s poll it was 12 req/min PER PROJECT — nine projects, 108/min from that page alone, the number that matches the 106 in the log. useLatestSession(projectId, false): mount and window focus are enough.
    3. Every screen that listens to the channel goes through criarInvalidadorDoCanal. The Executores and Visão geral tabs invalidated the events on EVERY event.appended, with no window: a dev agent in a burst (10 notices/s) made 630 event GETs per minute in the Executores tab alone. Now both use the SAME invalidator as the Session screen (window per target, the TYPE decides the target), and get as a bonus what was missing: the dev agent's action proposal invalidates the actions immediately, instead of waiting for the 15s fallback. The Visão geral requests the backlog on every notice (the blocked tasks come from it) through the extras parameter, with the same window.
    4. Waiting for the runner stops when it is over. EsperaDoRunner only stopped the GET /projects/:id probe (3s) at the CEILING; once the connection was confirmed, the ceiling's effect exited early and the probe kept going forever — 20 req/min while the panel was on screen, and, being the fastest observer of ['project', id], dragging the page's whole key. Now it stops at the ceiling OR on confirmation.

    Numbers (duas-abas.orcamento.test.tsx, the real screens — Shell, SessionPage, ProjectPage on the Executores tab, ContainersPage — over a fetch that counts per route; each tab measured alone, because two tabs are two JS contexts, and summed; req/min, dev → this rule):

    Scenario Before After
    session chat + Executores, live channel 70 + 138 = 208 70 + 72 = 142
    same, dev agent in a burst (10 notices/s) 70 + 764 = 834 70 + 97 = 167
    same, channel that never connects 155 + 186 = 341 155 + 120 = 275
    /containers with 9 projects 132 (108 from /sessions) 24

    What this rule does NOT close: with the channel DOWN in both tabs, 275/min is still close to the ceiling — the Session screen goes back to the usual 3s (RN-579: never worse than it was), and 25 req/min of slack vanish with a burst of clicks. The Shell keeps polling projects-summary and execution/session at 5s and, in the chat tab, the events of the EXECUTION session at 3s (the tab does not have its channel) — 44 of the 70 req/min of the chat tab. A session list created in another tab shows up here within 15s (it was 5s). The incident's number was MATCHED, not reproduced: the log did not say which tabs were open, and the reading that /containers was in one of them is inference from 106 ≈ 9 × 12.

    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/duas-abas.orcamento.test.tsx (the two tabs with a live channel, with a burst, with the channel that never connects, and /containers; all four fail on dev); apps/web/src/components/EsperaDoRunner.test.tsx ("confirmada a espera, a sonda PARA" and the failure case: without confirmation it keeps going); apps/web/src/routes/ContainersPage.test.tsx ("a linha lê a sessão mais recente SEM poll")
    • Origin: AT-278 (extension of RN-579; does not change RN-108 — the single-use ticket and manual reconnection stay as they are)

    RN-633 — The manual handoff does not declare readiness, and "already active" for the offer is the project's

    Two reads of the Session screen left the handoff stale (AT-293, AT-294):

    1. The MANUAL handoff does not hide "Estou pronto para produzir" nor "Confirmar arquitetura pronta" (AT-293). prontidaoJaDeclarada and arquiteturaJaDeclarada only asked "is there a handoff leaving the Criativo/Arquiteto?", and the manual handoff (ADR 0109, RN-441) records as fromAgent the LAST activated agent (request-manual-handoff.use-case.ts): requesting a manual handoff to the Staff with the Criativo active hid the readiness button with no readiness at all declared. The manual one is recognized by the human ACTOR of handoff.offered (matched by handoffId, RN-631) — the api's Handoff does not carry the actor. Outside the 200 window the actor is unknown, and then: for the Criativo the artifactId counts (the readiness handoff carries the product_brief, criativo_server.ex; the manual one never carries an artifact); for the Arquiteto the two automatic ones carry no artifact, so the one of unknown origin keeps counting, as before. Declared gap: the durable mark would be the actor (or a manual mark) on the api's Handoff itself; it was not done here (the api belongs to the lifecycle lane, ADR 0182).
    2. An offer to an agent already ACTIVE in another session of the project is not actionable (AT-294). "Active" was the SESSION's: after "Ativar execução", the Dev Lead runs in the execution session, and the offer to him in the chat session still had "Aceitar". Now the offer (the thread card, the out-of-window strip and the Infra card) adds to "already joined this session" the summary's roster.activatedAgents, which is the project's MOST RECENT session — the execution one, once activated. activeFor is still THIS session's, because it is what the manual handoff selector uses. Declared gap: sessions that are neither this one nor the most recent are not seen; closing it requires a PER-PROJECT activation aggregate in the api, which does not exist.

    No api or engine change.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-handoffs.ts:41 (origemDoHandoff), :108 (jaAtivo), :180 (prontidaoJaDeclarada), :192 (arquiteturaJaDeclarada); apps/web/src/lib/session-destinatario.ts:156 (useAtivosNoProjeto)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-obsoleto.test.tsx (manual with the Criativo active does not hide "Estou pronto"; manual with the Arquiteto active does not hide "Confirmar arquitetura pronta"; the agent's handoff still hides — failure case; outside the window the Criativo's with a brief counts; Dev Lead active in the execution session leaves the offer mute; without him, the offer stays actionable — failure case)
    • Origin: AT-293, AT-294

    RN-634 — After "Ativar execução", the screen goes to the execution session

    Execution runs in a NEW session (RN-135, RN-139), and the chat session the click came from may even close. The "Ativar execução" shortcut of the handoff card to the Dev Lead (RN-137) — and the merge with the acceptance (RN-161) — left the person in the chat session, looking at the place the work left from (AT-295). Now handleActivateExecution reads the sessionId that POST .../execution/activate returns and takes the screen to it, with the notice "Levando você à sessão de execução.". Without sessionId in the response, with the same id as the current session, or without anyone to navigate (the screen mounted outside the route), only the usual notice, and the screen stays. A refused activation does not navigate and shows the api's sentence. The one that navigates is the ROUTE (irParaSessao, the router's navigate): the screen does not depend on the router.

    • Where: apps/web/src/lib/session-acoes-de-handoff.ts:147 (handleActivateExecution); apps/web/src/router.tsx:339 (irParaSessao)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.handoff-obsoleto.test.tsx (navigates to the returned sessionId, with notice; refusal does not navigate — failure case; without anyone to navigate, it only notifies)
    • Origin: AT-295

    RN-638 — Whoever asks "what awaits a decision" reads the PROJECT's queue, never the most recent session

    The rail's Aprovações counter, the "needs you" panel (RN-467), the Aprovações tab, the Visão geral roster and the Código tab read the pending ones from useLatestSession — the session CREATED last —, which is only the execution one by coincidence. An ideation or a chat opened after the execution zeroed the counter and hid from the roster the dev agents waiting for a decision (AT-297). And the block of pending items from other sessions in the chat (RN-626) only existed in an active session, while the container_start that the Infra Lead proposes in the CHAT unblocks the dev.blocked_by_container that appears in the EXECUTION (AT-298).

    The rule:

    1. The queue is the project's. Those screens read useProjectPendingActions (GET /projects/:projectId/actions?status=pending, in any session), all through the SAME key ['project-pending-actions', projectId, undefined] — at the project pace of RN-632, and at 3s only in the Aprovações tab, which has it as its subject. The ProjectPage frame takes the PR counter (git_merge) from the SAME read, instead of a second query. The queues stay separate, each with its own badge — nothing is summed (RN-467).
    2. Each card decides through the session the action ITSELF carries. In the Aprovações tab, approve, deny, "sempre permitir" and the batch call the endpoint with action.sessionId, and invalidate the project queue by prefix and the actions of the action's session. The tab's PR and gate blocks read events and actions of the current EXECUTION session (RN-139), and only without it of the most recent one.
    3. Pending items from other sessions appear in any state. The chat block also shows in closed and technical sessions — deciding is about the action of the OTHER session, not conversation in this one (RN-581 keeps refusing conversation) —, and the Executores tab gets the SAME block for the execution session: the chat's container_start becomes visible where the block appears.
    4. The proposal arrives through the channel, without waiting for the poll. Every proposed_action.*/action.* notice from any session channel a screen listens to also invalidates the project queue (pendenciasDoProjeto in criarInvalidadorDoCanal, 2s window), and dev.blocked_by_container too — the proposal that unblocks it is born in another session, whose channel nobody there listens to (AT-299). No poll went up.
    5. The block fits in the column and shows all of them (AT-318). At the width of the thread and the composer (780px), collapsible, with the PRESENCE of each queue in the header — "Aprovações 3 · Merges de PR 1", each with its own number, never the sum (RN-467) — visible even when collapsed. Cards are born with the detail CLOSED (detalheRecolhido in ApprovalCard): open, they covered ~60% of the thread and left one card in view out of three. The origin label shows ONCE per session, with the time of the oldest — per card, it repeated between one card and the next. The ceiling of 20 cards and the declared cut (RN-180) remain.

    What this rule does NOT close: the notice only arrives where there is a screen listening to a session channel (Sessão; Visão geral, the most recent session; Executores, the execution one). In a tab without a channel (Aprovações, PRs, Backlog…) or for a proposal made in a session whose channel no open screen listens to, the counter arrives through the project poll, within 15s; opening a session channel in the frame would change the session lifecycle (the channel is RN-064's heartbeat) and was left out. The Aprovações counter still counts the pending git_merge, as before — the panel dedupes it into the PR queue. The gap of the WORKSPACE role in place of the effective one (RN-471) applies to the new Executores block as it does to the chat's.

    • Code: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectPage.tsx:82 (the counter), :121 (the merges from the same read); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectApprovalsTab.tsx:111 (pendentesQuery), :115 (sessaoDeTrabalho), :322 (handleApprove); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectOverviewTab.tsx:114 (pendentesDoProjeto); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectExecutorsTab.tsx:107 (pendentesQuery), :290 (the block); apps/web/src/routes/code/CodeShell.tsx:101 (pendentesQuery); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:782 (the block without isActive); apps/web/src/lib/canal-vivo.ts:134 (alvosDoEvento), :180 (the key by prefix); apps/web/src/components/PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes.tsx (porSessao, presenca); apps/web/src/components/ApprovalCard.tsx (detalheRecolhido); apps/web/src/lib/hooks.ts:455 (useProjectPendingActions); apps/web/src/lib/precisa-de-voce.ts (acoesPendentes)
    • Test: apps/web/src/routes/ProjectApprovalsTab.test.tsx (the execution's pending one with a newer ideation, decided through its own session; a decided one does not enter — contrast); apps/web/src/routes/project-tabs.test.tsx (the badges from the project queue); apps/web/src/routes/ProjectExecutorsTab.test.tsx (the chat's container_start in the execution tab; the execution's own pending one does not become a block — contrast); apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.sessao-encerrada.test.tsx (the block in closed and closed_abnormally; the session's own one does not enter); apps/web/src/lib/acoes-da-sessao.test.ts (the channel invalidates the project queue by prefix and with a window; tool.call does not); apps/web/src/components/PendenciasDeOutrasSessoes.test.tsx (three cards in view with the detail closed and per-queue presence without a sum; collapsed, the presence stays in the header; one origin label per session); apps/web/src/routes/duas-abas.orcamento.test.tsx (the burst invalidates the project queue in the Executores tab, within RN-632's budget)
